# HARDIMAN-X System Specification Document (SSD) v3.1

**Tethered Full-Body Hydraulic Load-Carrying Exoskeleton for Nuclear Decommissioning**

Author: Sheldon K. Salmon (ORCID 0009-0005-8057-5115) · Drafting assistance: Claude (Anthropic), under architect direction · v3.1 · November 14, 2026

**Status:** Concept-level specification. No hardware exists and nothing here has been built or tested. Every performance value is a design baseline until verified under §13; a decided value is not a verified one (§15). Lineage: converted from the KINETIC-ADS SSD v1.0 (Appendix A) and grounded against the General Electric Hardiman I final report [GE-71] and, as a secondary source, the NASA teleoperator survey [NASA-68] (§2).

**Change log:** v3.1 is the current stand-alone baseline. All earlier versions are retired and held in a separate archive; this document carries no earlier history and refers to none. Changes from this point are recorded below, newest first.

| Version | Date | Change |
|---|---|---|
| v3.1 | 2026-11-14 | Review pass (invariant review of the previous baseline). The operator's standing height (footplate) is now a named input and the guard stature rating, route basis, and operator CoM are re-derived from it; the thermal basis is recomputed at the rated operator mass; the minimum deployable build (STD plus pads) is stated; headline ratings carry their conditions; composite requirements are split into single-property sub-requirements and informative statements are marked; a compliance matrix (Appendix I), a parameter register (Appendix H), and an assumption map (C.3) are added; terms, abbreviations, and evidence tags are defined once; the provenance of Appendix F is recorded; stale review procedures are replaced; every undecided value stays decided, and the evidence still outstanding is counted in §15. |

## 0. Document Conventions

- **shall** marks a mandatory requirement; **should** a recommendation.
- IDs take the form `HMX-<section>-<nn>`, with a verification method: (T) test, (A) analysis, (I) inspection, (D) demonstration. A pack code in the tag (for example HMX-3.6-01 (A, T, OP-ODG)) means the requirement applies only when that option pack is fitted.
- **[TBD-n]** marks a value that a later review finds cannot yet be set; each is listed in §15, and none is open at v3.1.
- Values marked *derived* are calculated from other values here; formulas are in Appendix B.
- Resolution tags: **[D]** directly confirmed via cited source; **[R]** reasoned from confirmed evidence; **[S]** structurally inferred from adjacent technology; **[?R]** extrapolated beyond available data; **[SPEC]** speculative or architectural proposal. Baselines carried from KINETIC-ADS are documented there. Vendor and trade sources are cited by tag (References) and listed for verification in Appendix C.
- Citations of the form [GE-71 p.N] refer to the Hardiman I final report and [NASA-68 p.N] to the NASA teleoperator survey; facts so cited were read from those sources.
- The companion file `hmx_config_check.py` holds the configuration model and generates the Appendix F tables. It shall be re-run after any change to a pack, mass, or position, and the script, a version hash, and the date of the last run shall be published with each release (Appendix F.0).
- **Evidence-tag grammar.** The base tags are the five above plus `[?]` (unknown). A tag may carry one qualifier after a comma: `secondary`, `vendor claim`, `single prototype`, `trade source`, `directional`, `rule of thumb`, `heuristic`, or `verify`. Two tags in one bracket, as in `[R, ?R]`, mean both apply. `[A on B]` (for example `[R on ?R]`) means a conclusion of kind A resting on inputs of kind B. Source citations are a different bracket form: `[B13-ANSUR]` or `[GE-71 p.3A]`.
- **Record types.** A record `HMX-x-nn` is atomic unless its tags include `GRP` (a group, whose sub-requirements `HMX-x-nn.1`, `.2`, … each state one property). A citation of a group cites all of its sub-requirements. A record tagged `INFO` is a design-basis statement: it carries no verification and is excluded from the compliance matrix. The compliance matrix is Appendix I and the parameter register is Appendix H.

## 1.0 System Overview & Operational Envelope

### 1.1 Purpose and Scope

This document is the system-level requirements baseline for HARDIMAN-X: structure, hydraulic power and tether, actuation, control, nuclear-environment provisions, operator interface, emergency systems, concept of operations, and the verification those subsystems depend on. It is published as a design specification and prior-art record, not as evidence of a working system.

### 1.2 System Description

HARDIMAN-X is a tethered, legged, full-body hydraulic exoskeleton. The operator stands inside an open protective frame (the Operator Interface Frame, OIF). An outer slave frame carries machine mass and payload to the floor, bypassing the operator's skeleton. Force-feedback impedance control converts operator intent into scaled slave motion. Hydraulic power arrives through the tether from a Hydraulic Power Skid (HPS) placed outside the contaminated zone; onboard accumulators cover peaks and ride-through.

### 1.3 Operational Envelope

| Parameter | Baseline |
|---|---|
| Rated payload (brief two-handed lift) | STD: 200 kg within about 0.38 m of the CoM line; 200 kg at 1.0 m needs OP-STAB, whose mass derates the rating by the GVWR rule (HMX-1.8-04, Appendix F). Conditions: a force ratio of at least 16.7:1 so that the grip can hold it (HMX-5.4-01); a floor meeting HMX-14-02; an operator dressed mass of at most 100 kg (heavier operators derate it one for one); the offsets and packs stated on the rating plate |
| Continuous handling payload | 100 kg (HMX-3.1-07); STD single support holds 100 kg out to about 0.69 m offset fore-aft and 0.60 m for one-sided holds (HMX-3.2-03, HMX-3.2-12, Appendix F) |
| Dry mass | STD 643 to 688 kg against the ≤ 700 kg budget of §3.1; each pack adds its own mass (Appendix F); GVWR 1,000 kg (HMX-1.8-04) |
| Minimum deployable build on a code-basis floor | STD plus OP-PAD: 658 to 718 kg dry, which exceeds the 700 kg budget by up to 18 kg at the upper bound; rated 182 kg brief and 91 kg continuous (HMX-1.8-01, Appendix F.4b) |
| Configuration | Standard build plus option packs; rating by the GVWR rule; admissibility rules CAR-1 to CAR-9 (HMX-1.8, Appendix F) |
| Gross ground load | ≤ 1,000 kg (*derived*: 700 machine + 200 payload + 100 operator) |
| Foot contact area | 0.15 m² per foot: 32.7 kPa double support, 65.4 kPa single support (*derived*) |
| Gait | Quasi-static only, ≤ 0.5 m/s (HMX-3.2-07) |
| Envelope | No larger than the heritage 1.83 × 0.99 × 0.74 m (72 × 39 × 29 in) with packs stowed; route openings at least 1.09 m wide and 2.10 m high (2.05 m high with OP-ODG) at the baseline footplate height (HMX-3.3-01) |
| Reach | Vertical ≥ 1.83 m; horizontal ≥ 1.0 m (HMX-3.3-02) |
| Active degrees of freedom | 30 (legs 2 × 6, arms 2 × 6, back flex 2, hands 2 × 2); table in HMX-3.4-04; hip axial rotation, torso twist, and lateral bend are passive (HMX-3.4-05) |
| Force scaling | Selectable 1:1 to 20:1 (Hardiman I arms used 25:1 [GE-71 p.3A]); at 20:1 and rated payload, handle force ≈ 98 N (*derived*) |
| Hydraulics | 3,000 psi (20.7 MPa), retained (HMX-5.1-01); peak 30 L/min ≈ 10.3 kW hydraulic; continuous 15 L/min ≈ 5.2 kW (*derived*) |
| Tether | 50 m; tension fuse 400 N ±20%; handler-held ≤ 200 N (HMX-4.2-06, HMX-4.2-07) |
| Environment | Indoor decommissioning, wash-down capable, radiation classes per §7.1 |
| Work period | 4 h in-zone per sortie within an 8 h shift, upper bound only; set by the dose plan (HMX-7.2-08) and the ambient ceiling (HMX-7.2-10) |
| Occupancy | One operator in mode M1; none in mode M2 |
| Rated operator | Stature 1.54 to 1.84 m for unguarded builds and 1.54 to 1.64 m for OP-ODG builds at the baseline footplate height of 0.155 m (both sexes; the 1.64 m guard limit is the 5th-percentile man and the 45th-percentile woman); dressed mass up to 100 kg, with heavier operators derating the payload one for one (HMX-8.1-07, HMX-3.4-06, Appendix B.11) |
| Autonomy | None. Untethered operation is out of scope |
| Tool interface (OP-UHTH) | Universal Heavy-Tool Hardpoint on the forearm rails (HMX-5.4-04); a tool counts as payload against the 200 / 100 kg ratings |
| Overhead protection (OP-ODG) | Passive overhead debris guard, Level I impact case of 1,365 J (HMX-3.6-02); no claim of crush diversion (HMX-3.6-03) |
| Egress | At most 6 min from event to clean side; independent 15 min escape air (HMX-9.1-05, HMX-7.2-13); mechanical release limit 60 s, target 10 s (HMX-9.1-07) |

### 1.4 Architecture Basis

- **HMX-1.4-01 (I)** Machine mass and payload shall reach the floor through the slave frame. The operator's body shall carry only operator weight and interface forces.
- **HMX-1.4-02 (A)** Pumps, motors, and power electronics shall sit in the HPS outside the contaminated zone. Onboard electronics shall be limited to sensing, valve drive, safety logic, and displays. Onboard electro-hydrostatic actuators (EHA) are not adopted ([SPEC] on [R] and [S] reasoning; no supplier mass or radiation data were found [?]): (a) an EHA adds a motor, pump, and booster tank to each actuator [B10-EHA2] [D], so about 28 of them would put motors, rotor sensors, and power drives in the zone where today only valve torque motors sit (HMX-5.3-02), and power-management parts failed first in the dose data of HMX-7.2-11; (b) a power bus would be routed onto a wash-down machine, against HMX-4.3-04; (c) heat is not the deciding argument: EHA efficiency is high but its heat dissipation is poor without a central tank [B10-EHA1] [D], and at an assumed efficiency of 0.7 and 1 to 3 kW of mechanical power the on-machine heat is 0.4 to 1.3 kW against the 5.2 kW the return oil carries out today (Appendix B.8); (d) mass is worse, not better: an EHA knee actuator reached 2,518 N/kg (9,400 N from 3.69 kg) [B13-EHA] [D, single prototype], and at that ratio the rated forces of the 14 cylinder joints (452 kN at the peak convention of HMX-3.2-13, 226 kN at the usable force) would weigh 180 kg or 90 kg, against about 48 kg for the present cylinders and 18 kg of servo valves (Appendix B.8) [R]; revolute EHA modules reported 43 to 107 W/kg [B13-EHA] [D]; (e) the valve-based plant, the 50 Hz valve basis (HMX-5.3-04), and the hardware-limb test (HMX-6.1-05) would all need redoing for pump-controlled bandwidth. Supplier data at the 1 kN·m class would firm up the mass comparison (Appendix G, EXT-17). The decision shall be reopened if drive electronics qualify to the R3 qualification dose of 15 kGy (HMX-7.2-11) or if the tether hydraulics prove unhandleable (HMX-4.2-04).
- **HMX-1.4-03 (D)** Mode M1 (operator in frame) is baseline. Mode M2 (remote bilateral control from a master frame outside the zone) should be supported by the same slave machine.
- **HMX-1.4-04 (A, INFO)** Legged quasi-static locomotion with four foot-mounted deployable stabilizers (HMX-3.2-03) is baseline. The tracked variant TRK (HMX-1.4-08) is the defined fallback, with gates that trigger it.
- **HMX-1.4-05 (A, INFO)** A seated ride-mode variant of M1 is not pursued. Heritage shows it was easier to achieve but less natural, and it still needed a tether and external stabilization [GE-71 pp.7, 14, 18]; it keeps the legs, the stabilizers, and the floor-pressure problem that the tracked variant removes, so TRK is the fallback.
- **HMX-1.4-06 (A)** Resilience enhancement set, VNRA [SPEC]. The Vascular-Neural Resilient Architecture groups three optional enhancements: a structural-health fibre network (§3.5), redundant onboard hydraulic distribution with leak isolation (§4.4), and operator-intent feed-forward (§6.6). Each is advisory or availability tier: no tier 1 safety function (HMX-6.2-02) shall depend on any of them, and the machine shall meet every requirement of this document with all three disabled. Adoption of each is gated by its release test (CAR-8, HMX-13.2-12). Concepts proposed and not adopted are recorded in Appendix D.
- **HMX-1.4-07 (A)** Adopted upgrade set [SPEC]. The tool hardpoint (§5.4) and the overhead debris guard (§3.6) are option packs (OP-UHTH, OP-ODG). The thermal control and monitoring (§4.1) and the mechanical emergency egress release (§9.1) are STD. None adds a tier 1 function (HMX-6.2-03), none relies on pyrotechnic or stored-energy devices, and none changes a safe state of §6.3 except by the rows listed there. The machine shall meet every requirement of this document with the tool interface unfitted.
- **HMX-1.4-08 (A, T, INFO)** Tracked variant TRK, the fallback configuration ([R] on [?R] masses, Appendix B). TRK is STD without the legs (195 kg of the 640 kg allocation: 140 kg structure and 55 kg actuators) on a tracked base of 200 to 300 kg [?R], so the dry mass is 648 to 793 kg and the GVWR rule rates it 107 to 200 kg brief. It needs no stabilizers or pads: two tracks of 1.2 × 0.25 m carry 16.4 kPa static and 20.4 kPa dynamic, under the 23 kPa floor basis, and a 200 kg payload at 1.0 m moves the CoM 0.20 m against a half-track length of 0.60 m, leaving 0.31 m at the 5° test slope before the 65 mm margin (0.60 − 0.20 − 0.0875 m). Drive demand is 0.75 kW (2.2 L/min) at 0.5 m/s with a rolling resistance of 0.1 on the 3° slope. TRK is not offered unless a gate triggers: (a) the hardware-limb gait test of HMX-6.5-06 fails; (b) leg fatigue at the SC4 cycle count (HMX-3.2-05) is not met; (c) the site floor is not certified for the stabilizer-foot pressure of HMX-3.2-03 and no spreader mat is acceptable. TRK is a separate build, not a pack; it has not been through the admissibility rules except GVWR, floor, and stability.

### 1.5 Applicable Standards

Editions and status below are as of November 2026. Where no standard directly covers a heavy-payload nuclear exoskeleton, the closest published standard is the compliance basis and its equivalence rationale is recorded in the compliance matrix (HMX-13.1-01); a draft standard is a design reference only. Editions marked 'confirm' were not checked against the publisher and are on the external evidence list of Appendix G. The governing jurisdiction, regulator, and the editions in force at the site are recorded in the site acceptance criteria (HMX-14-02).

| Domain | Standard (edition) | Role | Status |
|---|---|---|---|
| Machinery risk assessment | ISO 12100:2010 | Compliance basis | Edition 1 (2010), confirmed 2022; a revision is at draft stage [B13-ISO12100] [D] |
| Control safety | ISO 13849-1:2023 (4th edition, April 2023, replaces the 2015 edition) | Compliance basis | Current [B9-ISO13849] [D] |
| Hydraulic fluid power safety | ISO 4413:2010; fluid cleanliness ISO 4406 | Compliance basis | ISO 4413 current [B9-ISO4413] [D]; confirm ISO 4406 edition |
| Functional safety | IEC 61508 edition 2.0 (2010) | Compliance basis | Newest edition in a national adoption record [B13-IEC61508] [D, secondary] |
| Personal-care and physical-assist robots | ISO 13482:2014; ASTM F48 family | Design reference | Industrial exclusion (HMX-1.5-01); 2014 edition current, revision at FDIS stage [B13-ISO13482] [D, secondary]; confirm ASTM F48 editions |
| Industrial robots | ISO 10218-1 and -2:2025 | Reference only | Published February 2025 [B9-ISO10218] [D, secondary]; assumes statically stable robots |
| Actively stable industrial mobile robots | ISO/CD 25785-1 | Design reference, watch item | Committee draft [B9-ISO25785] [D]; publication expected about 2027 [D, secondary] |
| Nuclear instrumentation and control | IEC 61513, with IEC 60880 / IEC 62138 for software categories | Reference | Confirm editions |
| Radiation protection and decommissioning | IAEA GSR Part 3 and GSR Part 6; ICRP ALARA principle; site regulator | Compliance basis | Confirm editions; regulator per site (HMX-14-02) |
| Lifting equipment | ASME B30 series; ASME BTH-1 | Equivalence rationale required (HMX-3.2-05) | BTH-1 2023 edition and B30.20-2018 identified [B13-BTH1] [D, secondary]; edition fixed at design freeze |
| Falling-object protection | ISO 3449; ISO 3164 | Equivalence by analogy (HMX-3.6-02) | ISO 3449:2005 Edition 5, confirmed 2022 [B13-FOPS] [D]; confirm ISO 3164 |

- **HMX-1.5-01 (A, D)** Standards gap [D]. ISO 13482 excludes industrial environments, and the standard for actively balanced industrial legged robots, ISO/CD 25785-1, is still a committee draft [B2-ISO, B9-ISO25785]. Neither can be cited as a compliance basis today. This machine is quasi-static and stabilizer-supported (HMX-3.2-04), so ISO 12100 and ISO 13849-1 with the equivalence rationale above are the basis and ISO/CD 25785-1 is a design reference. The draft's status shall be re-checked at each design review, and a published edition shall trigger a gap check against §3.2 and §6.3.

### 1.6 Safety Philosophy and Risk Acceptance

- **HMX-1.6-01 (A)** No single failure shall seriously injure the operator, except events shown by analysis and test to occur below 4×10⁻⁷ per sortie for serious injury and 4×10⁻⁸ per sortie for fatality (HMX-1.6-06).
- **HMX-1.6-02 (A)** No single failure shall both cause uncommanded motion and remove the means to reach a safe state.
- **HMX-1.6-03 (A)** Safety claims in this document are requirements, not facts, and each shall be closed by evidence under §13.
- **HMX-1.6-04 (A)** Every loaded joint shall default to holding load and posture on loss of power, signal, or pressure. Passive hold is the default state; motion requires continuous positive command. The heritage design likewise locked all actuators on loss of hydraulic pressure or control signals [NASA-68 p.99].
- **HMX-1.6-05 (I)** Design freeze shall not occur while any hazard classed catastrophic in §10 is open.
- **HMX-1.6-06 (A)** Operator-risk targets ([D] limits, [SPEC] allocation). The UK HSE tolerability framework puts the upper limit of tolerable fatality risk for workers at 1 in 1,000 per year and the broadly acceptable boundary at 1 in 1,000,000 per year, both for all hazards combined [B3-ICHEME] [D]; the same source suggests a factor of ten lower for a single hazard. Targets for this machine: fatality at most 1×10⁻⁵ per operator-year, which at 250 sorties a year is 4×10⁻⁸ per sortie, or 1×10⁻⁸ per hour over a 4 h in-zone sortie; serious injury (irreversible) at most 4×10⁻⁷ per sortie, a factor of ten above fatality [?R]. The 10⁻⁶ per year boundary remains the ALARP aspiration. The numbers drive HMX-6.2-02.

### 1.7 Defined Terms

- **OIF:** Operator Interface Frame worn by the operator. **HPS:** Hydraulic Power Skid outside the contaminated zone.
- **Safe posture:** payload set down, machine kneeling or seated, all loaded joints held by valves and brakes (§6.3).
- **ALARA:** keeping radiation dose as low as reasonably achievable. **TID:** total ionizing dose.
- **M1 / M2:** operator-in-frame mode / remote bilateral mode.
- **VNRA:** Vascular-Neural Resilient Architecture, the optional resilience set of HMX-1.4-06. **SHM:** structural health monitoring. **FBG:** fibre Bragg grating. **RIA / RI-BWS:** radiation-induced attenuation / radiation-induced Bragg wavelength shift. **EMD:** electromechanical delay between muscle activation and force.
- **UHTH:** Universal Heavy-Tool Hardpoint (§5.4). **MEER:** Mechanical Emergency Egress Release (§9.1). **ODG:** Overhead Debris Guard (§3.6). **FOPS:** falling-object protective structure (ISO 3449); **DLV:** deflection-limiting volume (ISO 3164), the operator space a protective structure must not enter.
- **STD:** Standard build. **OP-xxx:** option pack (HMX-1.8-02). **GVWR:** gross vehicle weight rating, here the fixed 1,000 kg gross of machine, operator, and payload (HMX-1.8-04). **CAR:** configuration admissibility rule (HMX-1.8-05). **Trim:** a named bundle of packs (HMX-1.8-07); the fore-aft offset of the centre of mass from the foot centre is always called the CoM offset. **Mass action:** a design action that changes STD mass without adding a feature (Appendix F); a moment arm is always called a lever arm or a lever of a named joint. **TRK:** tracked variant (HMX-1.4-08).
- **Class** is always qualified: radiation class (R1 to R3, §7.1), BTH-1 service class (SC1 to SC8, HMX-3.2-05), tool class (T1 to T3, HMX-5.4-10), or metabolic class (HMX-8.1-03). **Margin** is always qualified: mass margin (§3.1), CoP margin (HMX-3.2-02), knee margin (HMX-3.2-13), or phase and gain margin (HMX-6.1-09).
- **Abbreviations.** **CoM:** centre of mass. **CoP:** centre of pressure. **DoF:** degree of freedom. **FMEA:** failure modes and effects analysis. **SIL:** safety integrity level. **PL:** performance level. **PFH:** probability of a dangerous failure per hour. **NDT:** non-destructive testing. **PCV:** primary containment vessel. **EHA:** electro-hydrostatic actuator. **WBGT:** wet-bulb globe temperature. **CAV:** clothing adjustment value. **VFS:** visual field score. **ANSUR II:** the 2012 US Army anthropometric survey. **IBC:** International Building Code.

### 1.8 Configuration Architecture: Standard Build and Option Packs

[SPEC] unless tagged. The machine is specified as a Standard build that is safety-complete by itself (every STD safety requirement is met with no pack fitted), plus option packs that a customer or site chooses, each with a known mass, centre of mass, interface, and release gate. A rated gross weight, as on a vehicle, makes the trade automatic: the more options fitted, the less payload the plate states. This keeps the feature set from growing past what the physics allows.

- **HMX-1.8-01 (A, I, INFO)** Standard build (STD). STD is the machine of §3.1 (all six allocation lines) plus the tether fuse module and the MEER hardware, with: every tier 1 and tier 2 safety function (HMX-6.2-02, 6.2-03); brakes, load holding, and the safe-posture store (§4.3); the tether and its tension fuse (§4.2); the MEER, rescue release, and escape air (HMX-9.1-07 to 9.1-09, HMX-7.2-13); fluid temperature monitoring (HMX-4.1-05, 4.1-06); hand end effectors (HMX-5.4-01, 5.4-03); and the dose, thermal, and decontamination requirements of §7. STD mass is 643 to 688 kg (Appendix F.1), which leaves 12 kg of headroom under the 700 kg budget at the upper bound and 57 kg at the lower (8 and 53 kg if the ankle upsizing of up to 4 kg [?R], HMX-3.2-13(b), does not fit the 55 kg leg-actuator line). The STD arms, shoulders, and elbows are designed to the 1.0 m reach basis of HMX-3.2-13(c), and STD carries no pack provision hardware (HMX-1.8-06). STD is rated 200 kg brief within about 0.38 m of the CoM line and 100 kg continuous within about 0.69 m (one-sided holds 0.60 m), on a floor that is certified for the bare-foot pressure (CAR-9). On a code-basis floor, which implies 23 kPa, STD alone is not deployable: the minimum deployable build is STD plus OP-PAD (HMX-1.8-07, Appendix F.4b).
- **HMX-1.8-02 (A, INFO)** Option packs. A feature is added to this specification only as a pack, or into STD by a documented trade that re-runs HMX-1.8-05. Eight packs are defined, seven of them orderable (Appendix F.3): OP-STAB (deployable stabilizers, HMX-3.2-03), OP-PAD (floor-load pads, HMX-3.2-01), OP-UHTH (tool hardpoint, HMX-5.4-04 to 5.4-11), OP-ODG (overhead debris guard, §3.6), OP-VNRA-F, OP-VNRA-H, and OP-VNRA-I (the three VNRA enhancements of HMX-1.4-06: fibre network §3.5, redundant hydraulic path §4.4, intent feed-forward §6.6), and OP-NEUT (conditional neutron layer, HMX-7.3-02), which is reserved and not orderable: alone it rates 116 kg brief and with any stability or floor pack it falls below the 100 kg minimum, so it waits for a heavy-variant decision. Each requirement of a pack carries the pack code in its tag, and each pack section opens with an applicability line. The MEER and thermal monitoring are STD, not options, because they bear on operator survival and fluid safety.
- **HMX-1.8-03 (A, T, GRP)** Independence and containment. Sub-requirements HMX-1.8-03.1 to HMX-1.8-03.3.
- **HMX-1.8-03.1 (A, T)** No tier 1 or tier 2 function of STD shall depend on any pack, and the machine shall meet every STD requirement with all packs absent.
- **HMX-1.8-03.2 (A, T)** A pack shall not remove, bypass, or weaken an STD safety function: for example, the guard shall not impede the MEER exit, the rescue release, or the recovery hardpoint (HMX-3.6-05), and the tool port shall fail closed (HMX-5.4-07).
- **HMX-1.8-03.3 (A, T)** A fault in a pack shall be containable by one action that returns the machine to STD behaviour (tool port isolated, stabilizers retracted by the posture logic, pack software disabled).
- **HMX-1.8-04 (I, A)** Gross rating and payload. GVWR is 1,000 kg (derived: 700 kg dry budget + 200 kg brief payload + 100 kg operator; at that gross the knee cylinder margin with ψ = 1.15 is 1.23 and the single-foot reaction is 74% of the floor basis, 92% with the 1.25 dynamic factor, HMX-3.2-01, Appendix B). Rated brief payload = min (200 kg, GVWR − m_op − M_dry,hi, where m_op is the larger of 100 kg and the operator's dressed mass) and rated continuous payload = min (100 kg, brief ÷ 2), where M_dry,hi is the upper-bound dry mass of STD plus the fitted packs until the unit is weighed; after weighing, the rating is re-issued from the measured mass plus 1% and the measured CoM plus 10 mm (HMX-13.2-09). Each kilogram of pack mass above the STD headroom therefore costs one kilogram of brief payload. The rating plate shall state the pack list, weighed mass, rated payloads, rated offsets with and without stabilizers, bare-foot floor pressure, stabilizer reach, the rated operator stature range, the operator dressed mass (HMX-8.1-07), and the minimum force ratio for the rated brief payload (HMX-5.4-01). A tool is payload and counts inside the rating (HMX-5.4-04). Each limit is rated at its own worst-case bound: payload rating, floor, and knee loads at the high mass bound; stability at the low mass bound (the lightest machine plus operator, 743 kg for STD, shifts furthest per unit of payload moment) and at the tallest CoM; offset ratings are capped at the 1.0 m reach (HMX-3.3-02). Until the unit is weighed and its CoM measured (HMX-1.8-06), offset ratings are quoted at the 5th-percentile values of the sensitivity sweep (Appendix F.7), not at the nominal values.
- **HMX-1.8-05 (A, T)** Admissibility rules. A configuration is admissible only if all nine rules hold, computed by the method of Appendix B and the checker of Appendix F: **CAR-1** rating by HMX-1.8-04. **CAR-2** rated brief payload at least 100 kg [SPEC]. **CAR-3** knee margin 1,040 ÷ (0.5 × gross × 9.81 × 0.15 × 1.15) at least 1.20 [SPEC] at the gross with rated payload, and the single-support lever and joint-torque bases of HMX-3.2-13 met. **CAR-4** with OP-STAB, a stability margin of at least 65 mm at the 5° test slope with the rated brief payload at 1.0 m offset; any offset rating follows m·d ≤ Δx × (M₀ + m) with Δx = 0.20 [+ stabilizer reach 0.17] − 0.065 − h tan 3° − |x₀| and M₀ the build's lower-bound machine-plus-operator mass (743 kg for STD), with offsets capped at the 1.0 m reach (HMX-3.2-03). A payload held at both a fore-aft offset d_x and a lateral offset d_y shall satisfy m d_x ÷ L_x + m d_y ÷ L_y ≤ 1, with L_x = Δx (M₀ + m) and L_y = Δy (M₀ + m). **CAR-5** unloaded fore-aft CoM within 20 mm of the foot centre by layout [SPEC]. **CAR-6** stowed envelope at most 1.83 × 0.99 × 0.74 m, with the OP-ODG height of 1.95 m allowed only with the stature rating of HMX-8.1-07 and the route openings of HMX-3.3-01; deployed depth at most 0.74 m. **CAR-7** safe-posture margin 3.74 L ÷ demand at least 1.25, with demand 2.04 + 0.40 L plus 0.39 L with OP-STAB (HMX-4.3-02). **CAR-8** a pack is released for delivery only when its release gate is passed (HMX-13.2-12) and, for site-conditioned packs, the site acceptance criteria of HMX-14-02 are met; until then it is a development-only pack. **CAR-9** floor: a bare foot needs a certified floor capacity at least the dynamic pressure (1.25 × gross × 9.81 ÷ 0.15 m², 81 to 82 kPa); otherwise OP-PAD. Stabilizer feet (0.068 m²) carry 53 kPa static and 66 kPa dynamic and need a floor certified for that pressure, which OP-PAD does not provide, or a spreader mat, or the TRK variant (HMX-3.2-03, Appendix F.3).
- **HMX-1.8-06 (A, T, GRP)** Configuration control. Sub-requirements HMX-1.8-06.1 to HMX-1.8-06.5.
- **HMX-1.8-06.1 (A, T)** Each unit shall carry a configuration record (packs, weighed mass, CoM, rating plate).
- **HMX-1.8-06.2 (A, T)** Adding or removing a pack is a design change: the checker shall be re-run, the rating re-issued, and the stability, floor, and egress checks of §13 re-shown for the new build.
- **HMX-1.8-06.3 (A, T)** Each pack shall have defined mechanical, hydraulic, electrical, and software interfaces so that fitting it does not alter an STD safety structure. STD carries no pack provision hardware, so fitting a pack later is a depot design change with the re-verification above; any installed provision counts in the mass it belongs to.
- **HMX-1.8-06.4 (A, T)** Each pack mass shall be verified by weighing.
- **HMX-1.8-06.5 (A, T)** The first weighing shall also measure the CoM, and the accumulator and controller block shall be adjustable ±0.05 m fore-aft so that the unloaded fore-aft CoM offset can be brought within 10 mm (limit 20 mm, CAR-5); the adjustable mounting is assumed to cost about 1 kg inside its line [?R].
- **HMX-1.8-07 (A)** Trims. A named trim is a bundle of packs, not a new design: STD; MIN-DEPLOY (PAD), 182 kg brief; HANDLING (STAB, PAD), 132 kg brief; TOOLING (STAB, PAD, UHTH), 121 kg; PROTECTED (STAB, PAD, ODG), 115 kg; and PROTECTED-TOOLING (STAB, PAD, UHTH, ODG), 104 kg, 119 kg with mass action L1 (the composite accumulator shell of HMX-3.1-04), four kilograms over the 100 kg minimum without it, so it is quoted only after weighing. Every trim containing STAB needs the floor of HMX-3.2-03 or the TRK variant. FULL (every pack) is shown only to bound the space and is never admissible. A trim shall be quoted only with the rating the checker computes for it, under the quoting rule of HMX-1.8-04 (Appendix F.4).


## 2.0 Heritage & Lessons Applied

### 2.1 Source and Heritage Baseline

The primary source is the Hardiman I final report by Fick and Makinson, General Electric, 1971 [GE-71], approved for public release. Facts below were read from it; this is a single source, and the earlier program reports it lists have not been obtained [?]. A secondary 1968 survey, [NASA-68], is summarized in §2.4.

| Parameter | Hardiman I [GE-71 p.3A unless noted] | HARDIMAN-X baseline |
|---|---|---|
| Architecture | Master-slave, co-axial: operator stands inside the slave, which carries the load; master skeleton attached to the operator [p.2] | Operator in OIF inside the slave frame; force-sensing coupling in place of a position-tracked master |
| Force scaling | 25:1 in arms, bilateral [pp.2, 3A] | 1:1 to 20:1 |
| Weight | 1,500 lb (680 kg, *derived*) | ≤ 700 kg |
| Lift capability | 1,500 lb (680 kg), a design specification; the completed system was not run [p.10] | 200 kg rated, 29% of the heritage specification (*derived*) |
| Powered joints | 30 (15 per side: hand 2, arm 7 including back flex, leg 4, foot 2) | 30 (legs 2 × 6, arms 2 × 6, back flex 2, hands 2 × 2); trace in §3.4 |
| Hydraulic power | 25 hp at 3,000 psi (≈ 18.6 kW, *derived*) | 3,000 psi; 10.3 kW peak (*derived*) |
| Electrical | ±15 V DC, 3 A | 48 V DC control power |
| Walking speed | 2.5 ft/s (0.76 m/s, *derived*), design value | ≤ 0.5 m/s |
| Envelope | 72 × 39 × 29 in (1.83 × 0.99 × 0.74 m, *derived*) | No larger than heritage (HMX-3.3-01) |
| Reach | 72 in vertical, 36 in horizontal (1.83 m, 0.91 m, *derived*) | Vertical ≥ 1.83 m; horizontal ≥ 1.0 m (HMX-3.3-02) |
| Power delivery | Umbilical, electric and hydraulic, in the first prototype [p.3] | Tether to the HPS |
| Control | Arm: electrohydraulic bilateral servo; leg and foot: electrohydraulic unilateral servo with indirect force feedback; hand: hydromechanical rate control with force feedback | Impedance control with dual-channel force sensing on every load-bearing joint |

### 2.2 What the Record Shows

- **Outcome.** The first completed arm assembly met all design requirements, and the documented arm tests were on the left arm [pp. iii, 15]. The leg system reached only limited performance within the funding and time authorized, and the completed machine was assembled but never run as a system [pp. iii, 10].
- **Capability figures are design values.** The 1971 specification gives 1,500 lb lift [p.3A]. The 1967 text quoted on p.i says in excess of 1,000 lb at about 25:1. The report documents no system-level lift test, so heritage payload figures are specifications, not demonstrated results [pp.i, 3A, 10]. A 1968 survey says the design could lift and manipulate 1,500 lb and cites a roughly 2,000 lb load as the collapse hazard [NASA-68 pp.99-100], so the heritage payload is quoted three ways.
- **Mass.** The machine's mass was not reported as a limitation; by specification it lifted its own weight in payload. Its recorded problems were leg-system over-constraint in double support, joints-in-series interaction, runaway at joint stops, unverified hip-joint instability, inadequate analysis models, and limited funding and time.
- **Timeline and effort.** The contract began in November 1965, the first arm ran in summer 1969, legs and girdle were completed in late 1970, and work ended 31 August 1971. Hardware alone took about 25,000 shop hours [pp. iii, 2, 9, 10].
- **Servos.** A purely hydromechanical servo reached the practical limit of manufacturing precision, so the program moved to electrohydraulic servos [p.8]. Interaction between servo joints in series was a fundamental problem usually ignored in multi-joint manipulators; it was solved with a computer model and verified on the ankle, knee, and hip of one leg [p.9].
- **Control split.** Servo buildup and compliance forced separate masters for arms and legs, in place of one complete master skeleton [p.7].
- **Complexity gap.** Each arm was an independent single-mode subsystem of nine joints. The legs had twelve interacting joints and three control modes [p.10].
- **Runaway at joint limits.** With a leg fully extended, the operator's attempt to extend further grounded the master against the slave. The resulting signal folded the knee, and the operator's instinctive reaction made it worse. Mechanical stops that kept master travel inside slave travel removed the condition [pp.15-16].
- **Both legs active.** Activating both legs together produced violent, uncontrollable motion. The cause was not verified; intermittent hip abduction-adduction instability or a jammed position-input mechanism were suspected [p.17].
- **Both feet grounded.** The slave would not settle into a stable posture and one or more joints saturated. Preliminary analysis attributed this to over-control: with master and slave tied together at both feet, too many joints were actively controlled and fought each other. The proposed fix was to deactivate selected joint servos so they comply [pp.11, 17-18].
- **Models.** The report states that the analysis models lacked enough kinematic detail for the complex leg modes [p.10].
- **Workaround.** To show any walking, the master was fastened to the slave at the girdle and the operator sat on a seat attached to the slave (operator ride). Limited ambulation was shown with a tether carrying most of the weight and an assistant with a fulcrum for rotational stability; the machine could not balance or walk unsupported [pp.10, 14, 18].
- **Safety changes.** Mechanical stops and shields were added to the leg system for operator safety [p.14].
- **Sensing range has a lower bound too.** The foot-centering mechanism and ankle-inversion tickler were removed because they did not allow enough master-slave lateral displacement to generate error signals large enough to drive the knee and hip [p.16].
- **Test provisions.** Early serial leg tests were run with the operator remote from the machine using extension handles on the master. Later tests clamped one foot to the floor, hydraulically locked the other leg folded, and used a safety tether [pp.14-15].
- **Stop rule.** The report states that the source of the both-legs violent motion had to be identified and resolved before the machine could be walked in the intended mode [p.17].
- **Missing cues.** In ride mode the operator's feet receive no force cues, and the report concludes that leg force feedback would be needed for unassisted walking [p.14].
- **Human factors.** Readily applicable human-factors data did not exist, so GE built it from existing sources and mockup studies, and used a passive instrumented exoskeleton to find the minimum joint count [pp.3, 6].

### 2.3 Lessons Applied

| Documented problem [GE-71] | HARDIMAN-X response | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Over-control with both feet grounded; joints fought each other | Command only the independent degrees of freedom in double support; hold the rest compliant | HMX-6.5-02 |
| Three leg modes; grounded-mode debugging took far longer than planned | Explicit mode logic enforced by the safety controller; mode-by-mode staged testing | HMX-6.5-01, HMX-13.2-01 |
| Runaway at joint limits, worsened by operator reflex | Sensing range inside slave range; no drive signal from stop contact; untrained-operator tests | HMX-5.2-07, HMX-6.5-03 |
| Interaction of joints in series | Multi-joint stability analysis verified on a hardware limb | HMX-6.1-05 |
| Models lacked kinematic detail | Model-to-hardware validation per leg mode | HMX-6.1-06 |
| Suspected jammed position-input mechanism | Jam-free input mechanisms with defined failure response | HMX-5.2-06 |
| Servo buildup forced split masters | Separate arm and leg control loops | HMX-6.1-07 |
| Walking needed a tether and a stabilizing assistant | No unsupported-walking claim; development gantry; tracked-base fallback | HMX-3.2-06 |
| Arm succeeded, legs limited | Arm-first development sequence | HMX-13.2-01 |
| Ride mode was easier but less natural | Seated ride-mode variant not pursued; tracked variant as fallback | HMX-1.4-05, HMX-1.4-08 |
| Stops and shields retrofitted | Designed in from the start | HMX-5.2-07 |
| No ground cues to the operator's feet in ride mode | Footplate force sensing for ground-contact cues | HMX-6.5-04 |
| Human-factors data not available | Passive frame and mockup studies before hardware | HMX-8.1-05 |
| Heritage mass equals ours at 29% of its lift | Mass and rating re-checked against CAD mass properties at first design review | HMX-3.1-02, HMX-13.2-09 |
| Too little master-slave displacement to generate drive signals (foot centering) | Sensing range bounded above and below | HMX-5.2-08 |
| Early trials run with operator remote; leg locked; foot clamped; tether | Remote mode and fixtures as development test provisions | HMX-13.2-02, HMX-13.2-10 |
| Cause of both-legs violent motion never verified | Unexplained motion halts testing in that mode until root-caused | HMX-13.2-03 |
| Co-axial concept superposed master and slave axes for simpler controls and human-factor correspondence | Operator-to-machine axis correspondence requirement | HMX-8.1-06 |
| Intent: walk, bend, turn with minimum restraint | Mobility requirement | HMX-3.3-03 |
| Envelope and reach stated in the heritage specification | Envelope and reach requirements | HMX-3.3-01, HMX-3.3-02 |
| Joint count of 30 includes back flex within each arm's 7 | Joint-by-joint trace of the 30 DoF | HMX-3.4-01 |

### 2.4 Secondary Source: NASA SP-5070

[NASA-68] is a 1968 survey of teleoperator controls. It predates [GE-71] and describes Hardiman at the design stage, so where the two differ, [GE-71] governs. Page numbers are printed pages; text was read from the scan's OCR layer [?].

- **Operator range.** Hardiman's articulation and dimensions were set for operators from the 10th to the 90th percentile, accommodated without major adjustment [p.99].
- **Series-joint stability.** Dynamic feedback through serially connected joints can be positive, and unstable with no operator in the loop. GE's simulation showed proportional, rate, and lag compensation prevented it. The bilateral three-joint model was stable from 0 to 1,500 lb on an analog computer, and GE advised keeping those terms adjustable over wide ranges [pp.47, 49].
- **Design method.** The survey recommends limited analysis, then simulation, then an engineering mockup with a human at the controls [p.50]. GE's Walking Truck human-factors work used an unpowered simulator, mechanically linked to the legs and suspended from a crane [pp.95-96].
- **Leg input.** Tickler control was found unsatisfactory for the legs, and joint-angle control was proposed for some leg joints [p.100].
- **Fail-safe concept.** The design locked all actuators on loss of hydraulic pressure or control signals and linked the limbs so small master-slave errors could not build up. Collapse of a heavy loaded exoskeleton was named as the hazard [pp.22, 99].
- **Hand.** The slave hand is described as one servoed joint closing an opposed thumb against a V-shaped palm-finger structure, plus an unservoed on-off thumb-tip joint [p.99]. [GE-71 p.3A] lists two hand joints with force feedback, so the heritage hand differs by source.
- **Control quality measures.** The survey lists backlash, sensitivity and deadband, cross-coupling, drift, stiffness, friction, and inertia as control figures of merit [pp.21-22].
- **Time delay.** Human loop delay is about 113 to 328 ms for alert, pre-warned operators; confusion is possible near 0.25 s; force feedback is of diminishing use as delay grows [pp.31-32].
- **Joint count and cost.** Handyman, with ten servoed joints per arm-hand and built for the Aircraft Nuclear Propulsion program, gained dexterity at a cost in reliability and money and saw little operational use [p.89].
- **Biped falls.** GE's Pedipulator Balance Demonstrator showed operators balance a servoed biped easily, but a fallen biped stays out of service until a crane rights it [p.94].
- **Nuclear precedents.** Argonne master-slaves proved effective in hot cells, and Oak Ridge's all-hydraulic Hydroman handled heavy loads in hot cells [pp.92-93].

| Finding [NASA-68] | HARDIMAN-X response | Requirement |
|---|---|---|
| Designed for 10th to 90th percentile operators | Anthropometric range requirement | HMX-8.1-07 |
| Series-joint instability tamed by adjustable compensation | Tunable in development, locked in operation | HMX-6.1-05 |
| Analysis, simulation, then mockup | Added to joint-layout validation | HMX-8.1-05 |
| Tickler unsatisfactory for legs | Force-referenced leg input | HMX-6.5-05 |
| Lock on loss of pressure or signal; collapse hazard | Corroborates passive-hold default | HMX-1.6-04 |
| Hand described differently by source | Hand baseline flagged as unconfirmed heritage | §5.4 |
| Control figures of merit | Control-quality limits | HMX-5.2-09 |
| Human and loop delay values | M2 budget bounded by survey values | HMX-6.1-04 |
| Handyman's joint count cost reliability and money | Each DoF justified | HMX-3.4-01 |
| Fallen biped needs a crane | Fall-recovery requirement | HMX-3.2-08 |

## 3.0 Structure, Kinematics & Stability

### 3.1 Frame and Mass Budget

Frame material: titanium and low-cobalt stainless steel (HMX-7.2-03). Joints and links shall be sealed and wash-down capable.

| Allocation | Baseline (kg) |
|---|---|
| Structure | 330 |
| Actuators and servo valves | 120 |
| Onboard accumulators, manifolds, fluid | 50 |
| Sensors, controllers, safety controller | 40 |
| Operator cockpit, restraint, air interface | 50 |
| Decontamination covers and local shielding | 50 |
| Subtotal | 640 (*derived*) |
| Margin | 60 (≈ 9%, *derived*) |
| Dry mass baseline | 700 |

A bottom-up estimate (Appendix B) puts the current best estimate at 350 to 500 kg (actuators 118 kg against 120 allocated, structure 100 to 159 kg against 330), and 440 to 650 kg with a 25 to 35% concept-stage growth allowance. The 640 kg allocation sits at the top of that range; nothing is claimed back from it before CAD mass properties exist, because unit-level growth for structures and mechanisms has exceeded standard allowances [B8-ICEAA] [D, secondary].

- **HMX-3.1-01 (I, A)** Mass shall be tracked against this budget at every design review. Growth beyond the margin requires a payload or scope trade, not a silent budget change. Mass is tracked per build, and under the GVWR rule (HMX-1.8-04) each kilogram of option mass above the headroom reduces rated payload by one kilogram.
- **HMX-3.1-02 (A)** The 700 kg mass baseline is close to Hardiman I's 680 kg [GE-71 p.3A] for 29% of its specified lift capability (*derived*; heritage lift was a specification, not a system-level test result, §2.2). Mass and payload rating shall be re-checked against CAD mass properties at the first design review and re-issued after weighing (HMX-13.2-09).
- **HMX-3.1-03 (A, INFO)** Structural design factor and mass exposure [R on ?R]. Payload-path structure is designed to a nominal factor of 3.00 (HMX-3.2-05), which with the dynamic factor of HMX-3.2-10 is an effective 3.45 on externally loaded members. Sizing shows that fatigue and stiffness, not strength, govern the load-path tubes (HMX-3.1-07, HMX-3.1-09), so the strength-governed fraction of structure is 0% and the factor adds almost no mass. A structure-growth exposure of 0 to 43 kg (a 10% strength-governed fraction × 330 kg × 1.30) is nevertheless carried in Appendix F.1 as an upper bound until CAD; the stiffness sizing of HMX-3.1-09 (+4 to +8 kg) lies inside it.
- **HMX-3.1-04 (I, INFO)** Sub-allocations [R]. The 50 kg accumulator line carries one 10 L accumulator (about 31 kg dry [PARKER-EHV], verify) plus about 3.9 kg of oil at full charge, about 35 kg, leaving about 15 kg for manifolds, filters, burst-protection valves, and onboard hoses. A composite-shell accumulator could cut shell mass by up to half [HYDAC-ACC], about 15 kg [?R] (mass action L1). The 40 kg sensors and controllers line carries the safety battery of HMX-4.3-08 (3.4 to 5.3 kg).
- **HMX-3.1-05 (A, INFO)** Stability, floor, and tether hardware. The tether tension-fuse module (3 to 5 kg) is in STD (Appendix F.1). Stabilizers with feet (27 to 50 kg) and floor pads (15 to 30 kg: 6 mm aluminium plate at 0.48 to 0.60 m² per foot, with a ×1.5 allowance at the high end) are option packs (OP-STAB, OP-PAD) and are counted under the GVWR rule, not against the 60 kg margin.
- **HMX-3.1-06 (A)** Local-shielding line ([R] on [D] attenuation data, [?R] geometry). The 50 kg "decontamination covers and local shielding" line cannot shield the operator: as lead over an assumed 4 m² operator surround it is about 1.1 mm thick, 0.16 half-value layers for Cs-137 and 0.09 for Co-60, a 6 to 10% dose reduction (HMX-7.2-12). The line shall be read as decontamination covers plus, at most, about one half-value layer on one electronics module (21 to 37 kg of lead), which leaves 13 to 29 kg for covers. If more shielding is wanted it is a payload or scope trade under HMX-3.1-01.
- **HMX-3.1-07 (A, T)** Link sizing and rated payload ([R] on [D] material data, [?R] duty inputs). *Sizing check.* One representative leg link, a 60 × 2 mm Ti-6Al-4V tube 0.48 m long (the heritage shank length, HMX-3.4-02), carries the 735 N·m knee moment of §5.1. Annealed Ti-6Al-4V has 880 MPa yield, 113.8 GPa modulus, and a fatigue strength of 510 MPa plain or 240 MPa notched (Kt 3.3) at 10⁷ cycles [B5-AEROMET] [D]. Independent fully reversed rotating-bending tests on bars of 900 to 1,150 MPa tensile strength gave 345 to 475 MPa smooth and 305 to 455 MPa notched at Kt 3.6 [B13-TI] [D, secondary], so the 240 MPa notched basis is conservative and the 510 MPa smooth value is at the optimistic end; at 345 MPa the plain-fatigue section need is 735 × 2 ÷ 345 = 4.3 cm³, still inside the 5.1 cm³ of a 60 × 2 mm tube, so the OD 80 × 2 mm sizing is unchanged. MMPDS allowables were not obtained (Appendix G, EXT-04). The tube's section modulus is 5.1 cm³ and its second moment of area 15.3 cm⁴ (*derived*). Strength at the effective factor of 3.45 needs 2.9 cm³; fatigue with a safety factor of 2 [?R] needs 2.9 cm³ plain and 6.1 cm³ at notched details; buckling needs 0.7 cm⁴ against 15.3 cm⁴. The tube passes strength and plain fatigue and fails at notched details, so fatigue governs, and stiffness governs beyond that (HMX-3.1-09). The links are therefore OD 80 × 2 mm (Z 9.3 cm³). The 330 kg is a budget allocation, not a mass model; the FEA and modal test of HMX-13.2-08 shall confirm the sizing. *Payload is a weak mass action* [R]: cutting 100 kg of payload saves only about 12 kg (about 10 kg of structure, assuming 30% of structure is load-governed [?R], plus about 2.5 kg of pads). *Rating.* The 200 kg brief and 100 kg continuous ratings stand [SPEC] subject to the GVWR rule. Any mass overrun shall be closed in this order: composite accumulator shell (about −15 kg), FEA confirmation, stabilizer and pad design, the guard level and canopy area (Level I only is the first scope trade), then payload last.
- **HMX-3.1-08 (A, INFO)** Pack and tool mass. UHTH rails, carriage, lock, tool port, and isolators: 6 to 11 kg [?R] (HMX-5.4-04). Overhead debris guard: 13 to 17 kg (HMX-3.6-04). The tool itself (up to 50 kg) is payload, not machine mass. MEER hardware (2 to 4 kg [?R]) and onboard temperature sensors (under 0.5 kg [?R]) sit in the cockpit and sensor lines; the cockpit line then carries +5 to 9 kg with the escape set of HMX-7.2-13, and the lines are not re-balanced here.
- **HMX-3.1-09 (A, T)** Structural stiffness and growth ([R] on [?R] inputs, Appendix B). The structure is in series with the hydraulic stiffness of each cylinder-and-linkage joint. For the knee, β = 0.7 GPa, A = 1.257×10⁻³ m², about 1.19×10⁻⁴ m³ per chamber, and a 0.08 m lever give k_h = 1.19×10⁵ N·m/rad; with the 10 kg·m² reflected inertia of HMX-5.1-02 the hydraulic mode alone is 17.4 Hz. A 60 × 2 mm link has k_s = 3.64×10⁴ N·m/rad (20.2 mrad at 735 N·m), and the series stiffness drops the mode to 8.4 Hz, below twice the 5 Hz crossover. *Criterion* [SPEC]: the lowest unloaded mode of structure in series with the hydraulics shall be at least 10 Hz (2× the crossover, with a notch allowed), and leg-link rotation shall not exceed 8.5 mrad (0.49°) at rated joint torque. *Sizing*: OD 70 × 2 mm gives 9.97 Hz, OD 80 × 2 mm 11.3 Hz (k_s = 8.84×10⁴, 8.3 mrad), OD 90 × 2 mm 12.5 Hz (k_s ≈ k_h); links are OD 80 × 2 mm, 1.04 kg per 0.48 m link against 0.77 kg. Meeting 15 Hz (3× the crossover) would need about OD 120 mm and is not adopted. *Strength-governed fraction*: Z of OD 80 × 2 mm is 9.3 cm³ against 6.1 cm³ for notched fatigue and 2.9 cm³ for strength at 735 N·m, so every load-path tube is stiffness- or fatigue-governed and the strength-governed fraction is 0%. *Growth*: about 6.2 m of load-path tube moves from 1.61 to 2.17 kg/m, +3.5 kg, and ×1.5 for fittings gives +4 to +8 kg [?R]. *Payload mode*: with 100 kg on one arm at 1.0 m the reflected inertia is about 105 kg·m² and k_h (Ø46 shoulder cylinder) is 1.99×10⁵ N·m/rad, so the loaded shoulder mode is 6.9 Hz, only 1.4× the crossover; the controller crossover shall be scheduled with rated payload so that it never exceeds half the loaded-mode frequency (3.5 Hz for 100 kg on one arm at 1.0 m) within the uncertainty set of HMX-6.1-09.

### 3.2 Ground Loads and Stability

- **HMX-3.2-01 (A, T, OP-PAD)** Floor loading. The floor limit applies to every build; the pads are the option and are required wherever the site floor is not certified for the bare-foot pressure (CAR-9). Floor loading at the baseline foot area shall not exceed the limit certified for the site (HMX-14-02). Baseline check ([D, S]): ASCE 7 heavy-manufacturing live load, 3,000 lb (13.35 kN) concentrated over 2.5 ft square (0.58 m², implied 23 kPa) [B2-SCRIBD, B2-UOB]. At the 1,000 kg GVWR the single-foot reaction is 9.8 kN (74% of that load); with the 1.25 dynamic factor derived in HMX-14-02 [R on ?R] it is 12.3 kN (92%), and local bare-foot pressure is 65.4 kPa static and about 82 kPa dynamic, 2.8× and 3.6× the code's implied 23 kPa. Pads of 0.53 m² per foot close the dynamic case at 1,000 kg (0.60 m² per foot is adequate to a gross of about 1,125 kg) (*derived*). Two pads of 0.60 m² need 1.2 m² of plan area against the 0.73 m² heritage footprint (0.99 × 0.74 m), so the pads shall stow inside the envelope and deploy beyond it (HMX-3.2-12, HMX-3.3-01).
- **HMX-3.2-02 (A, T)** Under rated payload, the centre of pressure shall stay inside the support polygon by at least 65 mm in any direction on the 3° (about 5%) operating slope ([SPEC] on [R]). The derived margin is twice the sum of an assumed ±10 mm CoP-estimate error, about 4 mm of response lag (both [?R]), and about 15 mm of tether pull at the upper fuse tolerance of 480 N [R]: 2 × 29 = 58 mm; the margin is set to 65 mm by architect decision, 7 mm (12%) above the derived value, and shall be re-confirmed after CAD mass properties (HMX-13.2-09). A 3° slope shifts the CoP about 52 mm at a 1.0 m CoM height [?R]. Holding rated payload 1.0 m from the unloaded centre-of-mass line shifts the combined centre of mass about 0.20 m (*derived*), about half the 0.40 m foot length (a 0.40 × 0.375 m foot at 0.15 m², [?R]), so single support has zero margin at 200 kg and 1.0 m offset before any slope. Stability shall be actively managed, not assumed.
- **HMX-3.2-03 (A, OP-STAB)** Single-support limits and stabilizers. The single-support rule and its limits apply to every build; the stabilizers are the option. *Single support.* The payload moment is limited by m·d ≤ Δx × (M₀ + m), where Δx = 0.20 − 0.065 − h tan 3° − |x₀| (half foot length less the margin, the slope shift, and the fore-aft CoM offset) and M₀ is the build's lower-bound machine-plus-operator mass. For STD the model of Appendix F gives h = 0.970 m, CoM offset 2.9 mm, M₀ = 743 kg, Δx = 0.0813 m: 76.6 kg·m at 200 kg (0.38 m offset) and 68.5 kg·m at 100 kg (0.69 m offset) (*derived*). If the exposure mass of structure growth sat over the stance foot, the limits would rise; that is not credited. *Stabilizers.* Deployable stabilizers shall extend the support polygon during heavy handling [R] consequences, [?R] geometry. A 200 kg lift at 1.0 m needs double support plus stabilizers reaching at least 0.117 m beyond the toe line on the 3° operating slope and 0.1525 m on the 5° test slope (0.155 m with the guard) (*derived*). Baseline reach is 0.17 m [SPEC], a deployed fore-aft extent of 0.74 m equal to the 0.74 m heritage depth (HMX-3.3-01) with no spare [?R]. *Concept* [SPEC]: four foot-mounted telescoping outriggers (two forward, two aft) with floor-conforming feet, chosen because the reaction goes to the floor beside the foot with no moment through the legs and the mechanisms stow inside the 0.99 × 0.74 m envelope; pelvis-mounted outriggers would carry the reaction through the pelvis frame, and hand-held crutches would load the operator, contrary to HMX-1.4-01. *Feet and floor.* A foot extends 0.17 m beyond the toe line and cannot overlap the shoe, so its depth is at most 0.17 m; at a width of 0.40 m it is 0.068 m², carrying 53 kPa static and 66 kPa dynamic at the 3.6 kN reaction, above the 23 kPa code basis and below the 82 kPa of a bare foot. OP-STAB therefore needs a floor certified for 66 kPa dynamic at the stabilizer feet (OP-PAD does not cover them), a site-laid spreader mat, or the TRK variant (HMX-1.4-08). *Reaction and mass.* The GVWR cap bounds the stabilizer reaction at 3.6 kN static and 4.5 kN design (× 1.25 dynamic factor [?R]) per forward stabilizer, against 10.2 kN for a Ø25 mm cylinder at 20.7 MPa, a margin of 2.3 (Appendix B). Stabilizer mass is 20 to 40 kg for the mechanism [?R] plus 7 to 10 kg for the four feet (0.068 m² of 4 mm Ti-6Al-4V each with ribs, × 1.5 to × 2): 27 to 50 kg for OP-STAB. Stabilizer cylinders shall be sized to their load (HMX-4.3-02).
- **HMX-3.2-04 (A)** The machine shall not rely on dynamic balancing. On predicted loss of stability margin it shall freeze, hold payload, and move to the safe posture of §6.3.
- **HMX-3.2-05 (A, GRP)** Sub-requirements HMX-3.2-05.1 to HMX-3.2-05.4.
- **HMX-3.2-05.1 (A)** Structural proof load shall be at least 1.5 × rated static load with a dynamic factor for payload drop and snatch; this proof load is the acceptance test [R].
- **HMX-3.2-05.2 (A)** Design basis ([S]): payload-path members shall be designed to a nominal design factor of at least 3.00 on yielding and buckling, following ASME BTH-1 Design Category B, used where loads are not always predictable or conditions are severe [BTH-1-SUM, B13-BTH1] [D]: Category A 2.00, B 3.00, and C 6.00 per BTH-1 para. 3-1.3.1 as implemented in the design tools for the 2017, 2020, and 2023 editions, with Category B defined for loads that are not predictable or conditions that are severe (the standard itself was not read).
- **HMX-3.2-05.3 (A)** BTH-1 governs below-the-hook lifters, so the equivalence rationale of §1.5 shall be documented.
- **HMX-3.2-05.4 (A)** Fatigue shall use BTH-1 service classes by lifetime cycles [BTH-1-SUM] [D]: SC2 minimum for the payload-lift path (50 to 100 lifts per sortie, 250 sorties a year, 10 years gives 125,000 to 250,000 cycles) and SC3 to SC4 for leg and foot structure (about 5.6 million cycles per leg at 1 h walking per sortie; still SC3 at one tenth of that) [R] on [?R] duty assumptions]. The cycle bands used (SC2 100,001 to 500,000; SC3 500,001 to 2,000,000; SC4 above 2,000,000) were checked against [B3-MAZZELLA] [D]. For members driven by an actuator, the design load may be the relief-valve-limited actuator force where the relief valve alone bounds the load; where an external load can act (payload drop, collision) the design load is rated load times the dynamic factor of HMX-3.2-10 [SPEC]. The 250 sorties a year at 4 h in-zone used for these cycle counts hold only at an ambient dose rate of 20 µSv/h or less (HMX-7.2-10); in higher fields the dose plan allows fewer sorties, so the cycle counts are conservative [R].
- **HMX-3.2-06 (A, D)** Unsupported walking at rated payload shall not be claimed until demonstrated. The heritage record shows only tethered, externally stabilized ambulation [GE-71 p.18]. Development shall use a gantry or safety tether, and the tracked variant TRK is the fallback configuration (HMX-1.4-08).
- **HMX-3.2-07 (T, D)** Walking in transit shall be quasi-static at no more than 0.5 m/s. Heritage listed 2.5 ft/s (0.76 m/s) as a design value [GE-71 p.3A], and its walking was shown only tethered and externally stabilized [p.18], so speed shall not be raised above 0.5 m/s until walking at that speed is demonstrated under HMX-3.2-06.
- **HMX-3.2-08 (A, D)** Recovery after a fall, collapse, or stabilizer failure shall be planned, including righting or removing the machine and rescuing the operator without leaving personnel in the zone longer than the dose plan allows. A 1968 survey notes that a fallen biped is out of service until a crane rights it [NASA-68 p.94] (lug rating and rescue timeline: HMX-9.1-10).
- **HMX-3.2-09 (T)** Stability shall be verified at a 5° test slope, above the 3° operating slope, with stabilizers deployed, against the CoP margin of HMX-3.2-02 [SPEC]. At 65 mm the 5° case needs 0.1525 m of reach beyond the toe line (0.155 m with the guard); with the 0.17 m baseline reach the margin is 15 to 18 mm (*derived*, Appendix B). Development tests stay under gantry or tether protection per HMX-3.2-06.
- **HMX-3.2-10 (A, T)** Dynamic factor ([D] inputs, [SPEC] application). On externally loaded payload-path members the design load is rated load × ψ × Nd, with ψ = 1.15 and Nd = 3.00, an effective 3.45 on yield (*derived*). ψ = 1.15 follows the CMAA 70 hoist load factor, 0.5% of load per ft/min of hoisting speed with a 15% floor and 50% ceiling [B3-NRC-CMAA, B3-HOJ, B13-CMAA] [D] (Article 3.3.2.1.1.3 of CMAA 70, read in an NRC document; a 2025 revision exists whose text was not read); payload vertical speed shall be limited by the controller to at most 0.15 m/s (about 30 ft/min, which gives 14.8%, so the 15% floor governs) [SPEC]. Accidental drop, snatch, or collision shall be checked as 1.5 × rated load (the CMAA ceiling, also used for bucket and magnet duty [D]) against yield with factor 1.0; this case is not governing, since 1.5 is under half of 3.45. Free-fall onto stiff members is not bounded by 1.5, so it shall be limited by actuator relief valves, a compliant payload interface, and a release-height rule [?R]. Members whose load is bounded by the relief valve alone keep the relief-limited design load of HMX-3.2-05. The 1.5 × proof load of HMX-3.2-05 is retained and does not include ψ.
- **HMX-3.2-11 (A)** No stored-energy manoeuvres ([R] on [?R] inputs). The machine shall not use a stored-energy discharge for evasion, leaping, ducking, or bracing, and shall carry no joint-level surge accumulators (HMX-4.3-09). Check (Appendix B): a 0.3 m hop of the machine at the 1,000 kg GVWR needs 2.4 m/s, so about 49 m/s² over 50 ms, a ground reaction of about 58 kN (5.9× weight, 4.4× the 13.35 kN floor basis), at least 59 kW of hydraulic power (5.7× the 10.3 kW peak) or about 170 L/min at 207 bar through valves sized for 30 L/min, and a speed 24× the 0.1 m/s fault speed of HMX-5.2-10 and 4.9× the 0.5 m/s gait (HMX-3.2-07). The operator would need about 5.8 kN to follow (100 kg operator allowance), 39 to 97× the 150 to 60 N cuff limits of HMX-5.2-04, so the frame would leave the operator behind or drag them through the cuffs. A false trigger is uncommanded violent motion (HMX-1.6-02, HMX-1.6-04); the trigger and surge would be a seventh tier 1 function, tightening the allocation of HMX-6.2-02 from 1.6 to 1.43×10⁻⁸ per hour per function (*derived*) with a dangerous-failure fatality fraction well above the assumed 0.1 [?R]. A surge also adds no holding force: bracing capacity is set by the load-holding valves, brakes, and relief settings (HMX-5.2-03), which already hold with no power. Falling-debris protection is the guard (§3.6) and the exclusion and egress measures of §11.3.
- **HMX-3.2-12 (A, T)** Stability model inputs ([R] on [?R] inputs). The stability numbers rest on a 0.40 m foot and the component model of Appendix F.1. (a) *CoM height.* The loaded CoM height is 0.970 m for STD and 0.92 to 0.98 m across the admissible combinations (tallest mass bound, Appendix F.2); the guard alone raises the CoM by up to 15 mm, adding 1.3 mm at the 5° slope; each 0.1 m of CoM height changes the slope shift by 5.2 mm at 3° and 8.7 mm at 5°. Tools and arms held forward move it further. The fore-aft CoM offset is at most 7.2 mm over every combination (CAR-5 limit 20 mm). (b) *Lateral.* The foot is about 0.375 m wide and arms held out shift the CoM sideways; the lateral limit in single support for the 100 kg continuous payload is 0.60 m for STD (Δy = 0.0717 m), lower than the fore-aft limit of 0.69 m, so one-sided holds (a single-arm tool, an asymmetric load) are limited to 0.60 m and lateral stability governs for them. (c) *Pads.* The pads are a pack that shall stow inside the envelope and deploy to 0.60 m² per foot; the stabilizer feet carry the same floor obligation (CAR-9). (d) *Moment rule.* The payload moment limit is applied as m·d ≤ Δx × (M₀ + m), not as a fixed moment, and combined axes by the sum-of-ratios rule of CAR-4. The knee margin is held by the GVWR cap, not by a larger bore.
- **HMX-3.2-13 (A, T)** Joint torque bases ([R] on [?R] inputs). Convention: usable torque = basis × ψ (1.15) × 1.2 margin; the cylinder peak torque at stroke ends is twice the usable torque (the knee convention of HMX-5.1-02; cylinder-and-linkage actuators at 20.7 MPa). (a) *Knee, single support.* The 735 N·m basis of HMX-5.1-02 is the double-support share (half the gross, 0.15 m lever). With the full gross on one leg the same lever gives 1,472 N·m, a margin of 0.61 with ψ = 1.15 against the 1,040 N·m cylinder, so in single support the effective knee lever shall not exceed 77 mm at the 1,000 kg GVWR (margin 1.20, *derived*): the stance leg is held nearly straight. The limit is enforced by a tier 2 limiter that monitors knee angle in single support and inhibits step initiation, and holds, when the lever exceeds 77 mm (HMX-6.2-03), and is demonstrated by HMX-13.2-07. (b) *Ankle flex.* Holding the CoP at its margin limit needs ψ × gross × 9.81 × (0.20 − 0.065) m = 1.52 kN·m at 1,000 kg [?R] (ankle axis at the foot centre), 1.83 kN·m usable; the peak is 3.66 kN·m and at a 0.08 m lever the bore is about 53 mm, 1.76× the area of the Ø40 knee, adding 0.24 L to the safe-posture demand and up to 4 kg assumed to fit the 55 kg leg-actuator line (HMX-4.3-02). (c) *Shoulder and elbow, per arm.* A two-handed 200 kg brief lift loads each shoulder with 100 kg, and the 100 kg continuous rating may be held on one arm, so the basis per arm is 100 kg × 9.81 × 1.0 m = 981 N·m at the shoulder (1,128 N·m with ψ, 1,354 N·m usable, peak 2,708 N·m; bore about 46 mm at a 0.08 m lever, 131 mL/rad) and 100 kg × 9.81 × 0.45 m = 441 N·m at the elbow (609 N·m usable; about 35 mm at a 0.06 m lever). The STD arms are designed to this per-arm basis, which covers every build (demand at the rated payload and offset: STD 0.67 kN·m, stabilizer builds 0.51 to 0.65 kN·m, Appendix F.6), so OP-STAB needs no arm upgrade. (d) *Hip.* Flexion follows the knee (usable 1.04 kN·m, 77 mm lever cap). Abduction in single support is gross × 9.81 × 0.10 m lateral offset = 981 N·m, so the same usable torque and bore as the shoulder, about 46 mm [?R]. (e) *Arm allocation check* [?R]. Bottom-up per arm: tubes 1.5 kg (upper arm OD 76 × 2 mm, Z 8.4 cm³; forearm OD 55 × 2 mm) with housings and brackets × 5 to 10 give 7 to 15 kg, so 15 to 30 kg for both arms; shoulder girdle 15 to 25 kg; total 30 to 55 kg against the 80 kg structure allocation. Actuators per arm: shoulder flex and abduction cylinders 3.5 kg each, elbow cylinder 2.5 kg, four vane actuators at 1.5 kg, seven servo valves at 0.5 kg: 19 kg per arm, 38 kg for both against 45 kg. The 125 kg arm allocation therefore holds, with 25 to 50 kg of margin on structure and 7 kg on actuators, of which the hands of HMX-5.4-01 take 5 kg, leaving 2 kg. The larger shoulder and hip-abduction cylinders add 0.20 L to the safe-posture demand (HMX-4.3-02). (f) *Back flexion* carries the payload moment, since the arms attach through the back: (200 kg × 9.81 × 1.0 m + 392 N·m of leaning upper-body weight) ÷ 2 joints = 1,177 N·m each, 1,625 N·m usable, peak 3,249 N·m, a bore of about 50 mm at a 0.08 m lever, 157 mL/rad (HMX-3.4-04). (g) The inventory of HMX-3.4-04 gives a bottom-up safe-posture volume of 1.95 L (conservative sweeps: hip flexion 1.0 rad, knee and shoulder and elbow 1.57, ankle 0.8, hip abduction 0.5, back flexion 0.8), 5% under the 2.04 L carried in CAR-7, so the margin stands.

### 3.3 Envelope, Reach and Mobility

- **HMX-3.3-01 (A, I)** The overall envelope in operating posture, with decontamination covers fitted and stabilizers both stowed and deployed, shall be stated and shall not exceed the heritage 1.83 × 0.99 × 0.74 m (72 × 39 × 29 in) [GE-71 p.3A] (OP-ODG excepted, see below). *Access basis* [SPEC] on [D] code minimums: the IBC minimum egress door is 32 in (813 mm) clear by 80 in (2,032 mm) [B9-IBC] [D, secondary], and the machine does not pass it: facing forward the 0.99 m width does not fit, and sideways the 0.74 m depth leaves only 36 mm on each side, too little for a quasi-static walker [?R]. The route basis is therefore openings at least 1.09 m wide (0.99 m + 2 × 0.05 m) and, for unguarded builds, at least the greater of the frame height plus clearance (1.83 + 0.10 = 1.93 m) and the tallest rated operator's head plus clearance (1.84 + 0.155 + 0.10 = 2.095 m, rounded to 2.10 m at the baseline footplate height of HMX-3.4-06); with OP-ODG the opening is at least 2.05 m high (1.95 + 0.10 m). Both exceed the IBC minimum door height, so an IBC-minimum route limits unguarded builds to stature 2.032 − 0.10 − 0.155 = 1.77 m (the 59th-percentile man). Pads and stabilizers stow inside the transit envelope and deploy only in the work cell, where the deployed pad plan area is 1.0 to 1.24 m² (HMX-3.2-01). Confirmation against the real doors, airlocks, and corridors of the target facility is a site acceptance criterion (HMX-14-02).
- **HMX-3.3-02 (A, D)** Reach with the operator in the frame shall be at least 1.83 m vertical and 1.0 m horizontal. The horizontal figure covers the heritage 0.91 m [GE-71 p.3A] and matches the 1.0 m payload offset analysed in HMX-3.2-02. Offset ratings are capped at this reach.
- **HMX-3.3-03 (D)** The operator shall be able to walk, bend, turn, and kneel (kneeling is needed for the safe posture of §6.3) with minimum restraint. Heritage stated the intent that the wearer could walk, bend, and turn with minimum restraint [GE-71 p.2]. Restraint is quantified by the acceptance test of HMX-3.4-03.

### 3.4 Joint Inventory and Heritage Kinematics

Heritage joint inventory, per side [GE-71 p.3A]:

| Group | Heritage joints (per side) | Count |
|---|---|---|
| Hand | Thumb-tip flex, thumb flex | 2 |
| Arm | Wrist flex, forearm rotate, elbow flex, upper-arm rotate, shoulder flex, back flex, arm abduction-adduction | 7 |
| Leg | Hip abduction-adduction, hip flex, knee flex, ankle flex | 4 |
| Foot | Ankle inversion, foot rotate | 2 |
| Total | 15 per side × 2 | 30 |

- **HMX-3.4-01 (A, I)** The 30 active DoF of §1.3 are traced joint by joint in HMX-3.4-04. Heritage counted back flex inside each arm's 7 joints (two back-flex joints in all), and its legs had no hip axial rotation [NASA-68 pp.19-20]. The heritage-equivalent inventory is therefore 30: legs 2 × 6, arms 2 × 6, back flex 2, hands 2 × 2. Any further active joint (torso twist, lateral bend, hip axial rotation) shall enter only as an option pack or an STD trade justified by a task or range-of-motion need (HMX-1.8-02), with the checker re-run. Heritage precedent: the Handyman's added DoF gave fewer, not more, reliable operations, and the Hardiman's feet needed their rotate joint for turning [GE-71 pp.7, 14, 18].
- **HMX-3.4-02 (A)** Heritage link lengths shall be the starting kinematic baseline until X geometry is set. They are read from a rotated, noisy scan of p.2A [?] with units not stated on the page (inches assumed); verify against the original image before use.

| Heritage link | From axis | To axis | Length (in) | Length (m, *derived*) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Foot | Ground | 3 | 9.25 | 0.235 |
| Shank | 3 | 4 | 19.00 | 0.483 |
| Thigh | 4 | 5 | 19.00 | 0.483 |
| Girdle | 5 | 8 | 13.20 | 0.335 |
| Back | 8 | 9 | 15.80 | 0.401 |
| Upper arm | 9 | 11 | 15.62 | 0.397 |
| Forearm | 11 | 13 | 9.75 | 0.248 |
| Hand | 13 | Load | 13.00 | 0.330 |

- **HMX-3.4-03 (A, T)** Range of motion, restraint, and axis alignment ([SPEC] on [D] norms, [R] arithmetic). *Baseline rule:* the machine range of each joint is the lesser of the legible heritage excursion [GE-71 p.2A] and the normative range [B11-ROM] [D, secondary]; where the heritage figure gives no legible value, the baseline is 90% of the normative range rounded down to 5°, capped by the actuator (HMX-5.1-02); the operator-reachable range lies at least 2° inside the machine stops (HMX-5.2-07). The heritage values legible in the scan [?] are arm abduction 20° in and 45° out, leg abduction 11° in and 30° out, ankle inversion 18° in and 30° out, foot rotate ±35°, and forearm rotate ±80°; they are carried as the baseline and shall be compared with the original figure at the first design review (Appendix G, EXT-02) (a difference changes the values here and no requirement logic). *Baseline machine ranges:* hip flexion 105° and extension 15°; knee flexion 107° (the cylinder stroke of about 150 mm at a 0.08 m lever, against 120° for 90% of the 135° norm); ankle dorsiflexion 15° and plantarflexion 45°; shoulder flexion 160° and extension 50°; elbow flexion 135°; wrist flexion 70° and extension 60°; plus the heritage values above. *Safe postures:* the kneel of §6.3 is a tall kneel (knee 90°, hip near 0°) and the seat has hip and knee at 90°, both inside these ranges; sitting on the heels (knee about 135°) is not supported. The sweeps of HMX-4.3-02 (1.57 rad) cover both postures, so CAR-7 is unchanged, and the kneel and seat are demonstrated on the hardware limb (HMX-13.2-07). *Restraint:* walk, bend (30° at the back joints), turn, tall kneel, and seat shall be done with cuff effort at most 20 N mean and 60 N peak (HMX-6.5-06) and without the operator reaching an operator-side stop, over the 59 trials of HMX-13.2-11. *Axis alignment:* the literature has no consensus limit, but misalignment raises knee joint loads [B12-MIS] [D] and cuff-to-limb sliding was 1.4 to 4.6 mm in a fitted pilot device [B12-MIS2] [D, secondary]. At don the exoskeleton joint axes shall be adjusted to within ±10 mm of the anatomical axes and ±5° in rotation [SPEC]; the passive degrees of freedom (HMX-3.4-05) and cuff compliance absorb the residual; and the acceptance is a cuff-to-limb displacement of at most 5 mm RMS in walking at the thigh and shank with cuff forces inside HMX-5.2-04, measured in the mockup (HMX-8.1-05) and tightened from its results.
- **HMX-3.4-04 (A, I, INFO)** Joint inventory ([SPEC] inventory, [R] bases over [?R] levers; Appendix B). Usable torque = basis × ψ (1.15) × 1.2 margin; a cylinder-and-linkage joint's peak torque is twice its usable torque (HMX-3.2-13) and a vane's displacement is usable torque ÷ 20.68 MPa. Counts are for both sides.

| Group | Joint | Type | Usable torque N·m | Bore mm / displacement mL/rad | Holding | Count |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Leg | Hip abduction-adduction | cylinder | 1,354 | 46 / 131 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Leg | Hip flexion | cylinder | 1,040 | 40 / 101 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Leg | Knee flexion | cylinder | 1,040 | 40 / 101 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Leg | Ankle flexion | cylinder | 1,828 | 53 / 177 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Leg | Ankle inversion | vane | 971 | 47 | valve | 2 |
| Leg | Foot rotation | vane | 483 | 23 | none | 2 |
| Arm | Shoulder flexion | cylinder | 1,354 | 46 / 131 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Arm | Arm abduction-adduction | vane | 474 | 23 | valve | 2 |
| Arm | Upper-arm rotation | vane | 406 | 20 | none | 2 |
| Arm | Elbow flexion | cylinder | 609 | 35 / 59 | brake and valve | 2 |
| Arm | Forearm rotation | vane | 207 | 10 | none | 2 |
| Arm | Wrist flexion | vane | 203 | 10 | none | 2 |
| Hand | Grip close, thumb-tip positioning | vane or linear | 276 (grip), 40 (thumb tip) | 13.3 (grip), 2 (thumb tip) | latch (grip) | 4 |
| Torso | Back flexion (one per arm chain) | cylinder | 1,625 | 50 / 157 | brake and valve | 2 |

That is 14 cylinder joints and 16 vane or linear joints, 30 in all. Bases: hip abduction gross × 9.81 × 0.10 m; ankle inversion gross × 9.81 × 0.0717 m (the lateral CoP limit at the 65 mm margin and 3° slope); foot rotation 350 N·m of turning friction [?R]; arm abduction 100 kg × 9.81 × 0.35 m; upper-arm rotation 100 kg × 9.81 × 0.30 m; forearm rotation 150 N·m of tool torque reaction [?R]; wrist 100 kg × 9.81 × 0.15 m; grip 2.5 kN × 0.08 m (HMX-5.4-01); back flexion (payload 200 kg × 9.81 × 1.0 m plus a 200 kg upper body leaning 30° with its CoM 0.4 m above the axis, 392 N·m) ÷ 2 joints = 1,177 N·m each. The ankle-inversion vane and the 971 N·m it implies at 47 mL/rad are the largest vane in the machine and need a supplier check [?R]; if no such vane exists the joint becomes a cylinder, shifting mass, volume, and CAR-7 slightly.
- **HMX-3.4-05 (A)** Passive degrees of freedom ([SPEC] ranges [?R]). Hip axial rotation (±15°), torso twist (±10°), and torso lateral bend (±10°) shall be passive, spring-centred, and limited by the end-stops of HMX-5.2-07, so that the operator's limb is not over-constrained (axis misalignment, HMX-3.4-03) without adding an actuator, a valve, or a failure mode. Foot rotation provides turning in place.
- **HMX-3.4-06 (A, I)** Operator standing height (footplate) ([R] on [?R] inputs). The operator's height above the floor, h_f, is a named parameter (Appendix H, P-04). The baseline is h_f = 0.155 m: the heritage ankle axis is 0.235 m above the ground (HMX-3.4-02), the operator's and machine's ankle axes are collinear (HMX-8.1-06), and the operator's ankle axis is about 0.08 m above the sole [?R] (Appendix G, EXT-01), so the operator stands 0.235 − 0.08 = 0.155 m above the floor. Head height is then stature + h_f. The guard stature rating (HMX-3.6-07), the route basis (HMX-3.3-01), and the operator CoM (HMX-8.1-07) shall be recomputed from the measured h_f at the first CAD. A footplate recessed to h_f of 0.08 m or less would raise the guard rating to 1.72 m (Appendix B.11); recessing it, or accepting the lower rating, is a design decision to be taken then.

### 3.5 Structural Health Monitoring Fibre (VNRA-1)

**Applicability:** option pack OP-VNRA-F (HMX-1.8-02); not part of STD.

[SPEC] unless tagged. The network is a monitor-tier array of fibre Bragg grating (FBG) sensors on the load-bearing links. The interrogator sits at the HPS on the clean side, so the sensing path in the zone is passive glass with no electronics. It makes no claim to sense every square centimetre, to detect lattice fatigue before a crack forms, or to map dose in real time (Appendix D).

- **HMX-3.5-01 (A, T, OP-VNRA-F)** Network. Wavelength-multiplexed FBG strain and temperature arrays shall be fitted to the payload-path and leg links, concentrated at link ends, geometric discontinuities, and joint brackets, and bonded to the surface or laid in shallow grooves clear of peak-stress notches, because a groove is a notch in a member whose fatigue governs (HMX-3.1-07). Mass is grams per link and under about 1 kg in total [?R]. The interrogator shall be at the HPS, and the sensing fibres shall be dedicated fibres in the tether, separate from the signal path of §4.2 and the emergency-stop fibre of HMX-9.1-03. Baseline ([SPEC] on [R] arithmetic): fibre Bragg gratings (quasi-distributed, discrete) in radiation-hardened fibre, with femtosecond-written or regenerated gratings preferred (HMX-3.5-04); continuous distributed Rayleigh interrogation is not baseline, since it needs a heavier interrogator and gains nothing at known critical details. Count and routing: 12 load-path links (4 leg, 4 arm, spine, pelvis, 2 shoulder-girdle) × 2 ends × 2 gratings (strain and temperature reference) = 48, plus 16 at joint brackets and other notch details, 64 in all, on 4 active sensing fibres of 16 gratings with 2 spare, 6 dedicated tether fibres in all (Appendix B.10); service loops per HMX-3.5-05.
- **HMX-3.5-02 (A, OP-VNRA-F)** Authority. The network is a monitor: tier 3 (HMX-6.2-02) for display and trending, feeding Go/No-go (HMX-11.2-01). A strain-limit abort may be added at tier 2 only after diagnostic coverage of fibre, interrogator, and temperature compensation is shown. No tier 1 function shall depend on the network, and its loss shall not change the machine's safe states.
- **HMX-3.5-03 (A, T, OP-VNRA-F)** Fatigue accounting [R, ?R]. Measured strain histories shall feed rainflow cycle counting and cumulative damage against the BTH-1 service class and the notched fatigue basis of HMX-3.1-07, giving a consumed-life estimate per link and retirement criteria under HMX-12-03. The network measures strain and temperature at its gratings: it can validate the FEA of HMX-13.2-08 and show stiffness change or strain redistribution near a growing crack, but it cannot see lattice-level damage, or crack initiation away from a grating.
- **HMX-3.5-04 (T, OP-VNRA-F, GRP)** Radiation. Sub-requirements HMX-3.5-04.1 to HMX-3.5-04.3.
