- **HMX-3.5-04.1 (T, OP-VNRA-F)** Gratings and fibre shall be a radiation-hardened type, qualified under HMX-7.2-01 and HMX-7.2-11. In a research-reactor field (up to 124 MGy and 8.7×10¹⁸ n/cm²) grating peaks showed red shifts of hundreds of picometres to nanometres, and femtosecond-written gratings shifted less than UV-written ones [B5-SOREQ] [D]; gratings in radiation-hardened fibre showed a Bragg shift below ±2 pm (about ±0.2 °C) after 200 kGy [B5-FRAUNHOFER] [D].
- **HMX-3.5-04.2 (T, OP-VNRA-F)** The tested doses exceed R3 (10 kGy, qualified to 15 kGy), but fibre type, writing method, and temperature cross-sensitivity (about 10 pm/°C near room temperature [B5-SOREQ] [D]) shall be characterised for the actual build, with reference gratings for strain and temperature separation [?R].
- **HMX-3.5-04.3 (T, OP-VNRA-F)** *Release criterion:* gratings and fibre shall be irradiated to the qualification dose of HMX-7.2-11 for the declared class, and the Bragg shift shall stay within ±25 pm (21 µε at about 1.2 pm/µε, 1% of the 2,100 µε notched-fatigue strain amplitude of HMX-3.1-07), with attenuation inside the link budget of HMX-7.2-02; until it passes the pack is development-only (CAR-8); grating-specific data to 15 kGy were not found (Appendix G, EXT-20).
- **HMX-3.5-05 (A, T, OP-VNRA-F)** Joint crossing. A fibre that crosses a joint needs a service loop or rotary feed-through sized for the joint stroke (107° at the knee cylinder, HMX-5.1-02) and a vendor minimum bend radius. A broken fibre shall degrade the monitor, raise a caution, and leave the machine's safe states unchanged. Fibre breakage at joints is expected to be the main reliability risk [?R].
- **HMX-3.5-06 (A, OP-VNRA-F, INFO)** Dose sensing: not adopted ([R]). A polymer-fibre grating has been shown to track gamma dose at −26.2 pm/kGy over 41 kGy with a resolution of 40 Gy [B5-CYTOP] [D]. That is a machine-TID figure, far too coarse for operator dose, which is planned in mSv, and it adds nothing to the module-swap rule of HMX-7.2-11, which uses the accumulated dose of the machine dosimetry. The pack therefore carries no fibre dosimetry, and operator dose stays with the integrated dosimetry of HMX-7.2-08.

### 3.6 Overhead Debris Guard (ODG)

**Applicability:** option pack OP-ODG; not part of STD. Adds 13 to 17 kg at about 1.9 m, raises the height to 1.95 m, and is rated for operator stature 1.54 to 1.64 m only at the baseline footplate height (HMX-3.6-07, HMX-3.4-06); route openings at least 2.05 m high (HMX-3.3-01).

[SPEC] unless tagged. The hazard is falling and collapsing debris, whose standard analogue is the falling-object protective structure (FOPS, ISO 3449) with its deflection-limiting volume (DLV, ISO 3164). A roll-over structure answers vehicle roll-over, which this machine does not do (quasi-static, 3° operating slope, stabilizers; HMX-3.2-04). The ODG is a passive overhead guard with a defined impact case and a limited crush claim.

- **HMX-3.6-01 (A, T, OP-ODG)** Passive structure. The ODG shall be a passive overhead structure above the operator's head and shoulders, with no powered, kinetic, or 'active' element and no function that needs a tier. It is verified by test (HMX-13.2-04), not by a safety-integrity allocation.
- **HMX-3.6-02 (A, T, OP-ODG)** Impact case ([D] test energies, [SPEC] application). ISO 3449 sets Level I at 1,365 J (about a 45 kg object dropped about 3 m, small objects) and Level II at 11,600 J (about a 227 kg object dropped about 5 m, large objects), the pass criterion being no entry into the DLV and no penetration [R18-ISO3449, R18-FOPS-PRONAR, R18-FOPS-KTU, B13-FOPS] [D]: test reports show 1,365 J as a 45 kg object dropped from 3.117 m and 11,600 J as a 227 kg object dropped from at least 5.21 m (11,668 J imparted), and ISO 3449:2005 is Edition 5, confirmed in 2022 and current [B13-FOPS] [D]. Level II is 8.5× Level I (*derived*). Level I (1,365 J) is the design basis; Level II is not adopted, because it needs either a collapse load of 145 kN at the 80 mm of travel the DLV allows (above the 75 kN two-leg design reaction) or 0.29 m of travel at 40 kN (HMX-3.6-04). Debris above Level I is handled by the exclusion and egress measures of HMX-3.6-06. The standard covers earth-moving machinery, so its use here is by analogy and the equivalence rationale of §1.5 applies [S].
- **HMX-3.6-03 (A, OP-ODG)** Crush claim limited ([R] on [?R] inputs, Appendix B). *Floor.* With pads of 0.60 m² per foot in double support at the 23 kPa implied floor basis (HMX-3.2-01), the supportable gross load is 27.6 kN, about 2.8 t, so about 1.8 t of debris on top of a machine at the 1,000 kg GVWR; this is a design live-load basis, not floor failure, and the real limit is the certified floor capacity of HMX-14-02. 'Thousands of kg' is therefore not a supportable requirement. *Knees.* The knee cylinder's rated 1,040 N·m (HMX-5.1-02) holds only about 4.2 kN at an assumed 0.25 m arm, about 0.42 t per leg, so a crush path through bent legs held by valves and brakes fails before the floor does. The guard's crush path shall therefore run through structure and mechanical stops, not through hydraulic pressure, relief valves, or the cockpit restraint (HMX-3.6-05). Claims of '100% diversion' and an 'uncompromised survival volume' shall not be made until the load path is shown by test (HMX-13.2-04).
- **HMX-3.6-04 (A, OP-ODG, INFO)** Mass and envelope ([?R]). A clamped Ti-6Al-4V plate with yield 880 MPa has a plastic moment M_p = σ_y t² ÷ 4 per unit width and a central collapse load P_u = 4π M_p; at 4 mm that is 3.5 kN·m/m and 44 kN, which absorbs P_u × 0.08 m = 3.5 kJ in 80 mm of travel, 2.6× the 1,365 J of Level I (stopping distance 34 mm), and a 200 mm contact punches through only above about 1.3 MN. Four Ø40 × 2 mm posts 0.5 m long carry 11 kN each with an Euler load of 194 kN. The canopy (0.36 m², 6.4 kg plate) with posts (2.1 kg) and fittings × 1.5 to × 2 is 13 to 17 kg (*derived*). The canopy top is 1.95 m with the underside at about 1.90 m, and the guard is rated for the stature range of HMX-3.6-07.
- **HMX-3.6-05 (A, I, OP-ODG)** Interfaces. The guard shall not block the crane-rated recovery hardpoint (HMX-9.1-02), the rescue release (HMX-9.1-08), the operator's exit path, the field of view (HMX-8.1-03), or the fibre routes of §3.5. Its crush path shall not pass through the cockpit, restraint, or the operator. Its surfaces shall be crevice-free and covered by decontamination skins (HMX-7.2-03, HMX-7.2-04).
- **HMX-3.6-06 (A, OP-ODG)** Controls first. A debris load above the guard rating is not survivable by design. Pre-job structural assessment, exclusion of the debris-fall zone, and the freeze, hold, and egress response of §11.3 remain primary; the machine shall not respond with a stored-energy manoeuvre (HMX-3.2-11).
- **HMX-3.6-07 (A, T, OP-ODG, GRP)** Collapse load and head gap ([R] on [?R] inputs). Sub-requirements HMX-3.6-07.1 to HMX-3.6-07.3.
- **HMX-3.6-07.1 (A, T, OP-ODG)** The guard shall be designed to collapse plastically at a load of at most 45 kN (a 4 mm plate), below the 75 kN two-leg design reaction, so that a debris event above its rating overloads the guard before the legs, and the plastic travel under the Level I case shall not exceed 80 mm.
- **HMX-3.6-07.2 (A, T, OP-ODG)** The gap between the tallest rated operator's head and the guard underside at about 1.90 m shall be at least 100 mm (80 mm of travel plus 20 mm). Head height is stature plus the footplate height h_f (HMX-3.4-06), so the rated stature is 1.90 − 0.10 − h_f: 1.645 m, rated 1.64 m, at the baseline h_f of 0.155 m. That limit is the 5th-percentile man (ANSUR II pilot) and the 45th-percentile woman (pooled placeholder) of HMX-8.1-07; with h_f of 0.08 m it would be 1.72 m (the 31st-percentile man), and with h_f of zero 1.80 m (the 74th-percentile man). A guard that fits the 90th-percentile man (1.84 m) would need a top of 1.84 + 0.155 + 0.10 + 0.05 = 2.15 m and route openings of 2.25 m, above any IBC minimum door (HMX-3.3-01).
- **HMX-3.6-07.3 (A, T, OP-ODG)** The rating shall be re-derived from the measured h_f. The 45 kN cap is conditional on the legs taking 75 kN with straight knees, which the stop design has yet to show (the guard release test of HMX-13.2-12 includes the leg-stop test); until it is shown, 45 kN is a target.


## 4.0 Hydraulic Power, Tether & Distribution

### 4.1 Hydraulic Power Skid (HPS)

| Parameter | Baseline |
|---|---|
| Pump | Pressure-compensated variable-displacement axial piston on a variable-speed electric drive |
| Pressure | 3,000 psi (HMX-5.1-01); 5,000 psi reopened only as a late mass action |
| Flow | 30 L/min peak, 15 L/min continuous |
| Electrical input | ≈ 13 kW at peak, assuming 80% overall efficiency (*derived*) |
| Location | Outside the contaminated zone, within tether length |
| Fluid | HFDU synthetic ester, ISO VG 46 (HMX-4.1-04) |
| Machine-return fluid | 10.4 to 11.3 K rise across the machine at the 5.2 kW continuous bound (*derived*, Appendix B); alarm 70 °C and abort 80 °C (HMX-4.1-06, 4.1-08) |

- **HMX-4.1-01 (A)** Fluid selection shall assess radiation tolerance, fire behaviour, toxicity, and disposal route (HMX-4.1-04).
- **HMX-4.1-02 (T)** Fluid cleanliness shall meet ISO 4406 15/13/10 as the operating target, with alarm and sortie abort above 16/14/11 [R]. Basis: servo-valve guidance of 16/13 maximum and 14/11 recommended in the older two-number code [MOOG-GEN] [D]; 16/14/11 for servo valves at 3,000 psi, to be tightened for safety-critical, cold-start, and shock duty [FILT-ISO] [D]; Moog ServoJet series below 19/16/13 for normal operation and below 17/14/11 for longer life [MOOG-D661] [D]. Filtration shall be β5(c) of 1000 or better onboard and at the HPS [FILT-ISO], with fluid sampling every sortie. Conversion between the older two-number code and the current three-number code shall be confirmed with the valve vendor.
- **HMX-4.1-03 (D)** The HPS shall condition fluid temperature so the system meets servo-valve response limits at the start of every sortie. Machine-inlet fluid shall be 40 to 60 °C in operation (HMX-4.1-04); full 30 L/min flow is not permitted below 40 °C, and the flow limit shall follow measured viscosity so tether pressure drop stays at or below 5% of supply.
- **HMX-4.1-04 (A, T)** Fluid ([SPEC] choice on [D] data). The fluid is a fire-resistant synthetic ester, ISO 12922 HFDU, ISO 15380 HEE type, grade ISO VG 46. Reference product data [B3-FUCHS] [D]: viscosity 309.5, 50.0, 26.5, and 10.4 mm²/s at 0, 40, 60, and 100 °C; viscosity index 203; density 920 kg/m³ at 15 °C; pour point −42 °C; open-cup flash point 270 °C, fire point 364 °C; passes spray-ignition and hot-surface (above 400 °C) tests; FM Approvals 6930 approved; readily biodegradable (above 60% in OECD 301 B); good compatibility with NBR, FKM, and silicone seals, neoprene and EPDM not recommended; miscible with mineral oil. Alternatives: water-glycol HFC is rejected because water loss accelerates ageing [B3-REXROTH] [D] and radiolysis of water produces hydrogen [R], with catalogue pressure ratings of 2,500 to 4,000 psi [B3-XOMETRY, B3-SHELL] [D]; phosphate-ester HFDR, the most fire-resistant, needs EPDM or FKM seals [B3-XOMETRY] [D] and is kept as the fallback if the fire case needs self-extinguishing [SPEC]; mineral oil has the best radiation record but is not fire-resistant (HMX-7.2-06). Radiation: mineral oils showed general resistance to about 1×10⁸ roentgens, roughly 0.9 MGy [B3-OSTI] [D], and lubricant degradation below 1×10⁹ erg/g (100 kGy) is small next to heat and oxidation [B3-IIT] [D], so fluid is not the radiation limit at R3 (10 kGy). The same 1957 study found one diester very poor in gamma stability [B3-OSTI] [D], and no polyol-ester data was found in two searches (Appendix G, EXT-03). A supplier of radiation-resistant fluids states that conventional synthetic lubricants, including esters, are not formulated for sustained ionizing radiation, and a PAG-based fire-resistant fluid line is marketed for nuclear power stations [B13-FLUID] [D, vendor claim]; the latter is a candidate alternative to evaluate. The selected fluid shall be irradiated to at least 2× the class TID with limits on viscosity and acid number [?R]. Spent fluid and filters are organic radioactive waste, routed under HMX-7.2-07. Viscosity at the machine inlet is 50 to 26.5 mm²/s over 40 to 60 °C.
- **HMX-4.1-05 (A, T)** Heat removal at the HPS ([R] on [?R] inputs). Machine heat is carried out in the return flow. At the continuous bound of 5.2 kW (all hydraulic power dissipated) and 15 L/min, the fluid rises 10.4 to 11.3 K (0.224 kg/s at about 897 kg/m³, specific heat 2.06 to 2.24 kJ/kg·K from vendor sheets [B7-QH888, B7-GULF] [D]); the same holds at the 30 L/min, 10.3 kW peak. With the 40 to 60 °C inlet limit (HMX-4.1-04) the return is then at most about 71 °C, and about 56 °C at the 45 to 50 °C setpoint of HMX-4.1-08. The HPS shall hold the inlet limits of HMX-4.1-03 and remove the machine heat in a cooler rated at least 7 kW continuous (5.2 kW machine bound + 0.13 kW tether loss + about 0.6 kW pump loss = 5.9 kW, × 1.15 margin = 6.8 kW, rounded up) [R]; the drive losses outside the fluid are per the HPS vendor.
- **HMX-4.1-06 (A, T)** Onboard cooling not adopted; monitoring adopted ([R, ?R]). A micro-lattice regenerative cooling core in the titanium leg spars was considered and is not adopted. (a) A lattice or channel inside a fatigue-governed link is a notch that cannot be finished (HMX-3.1-07, HMX-4.4-04). (b) Titanium alloy conducts only about 7 W/m·K [?R, verify], and a 2 mm wall (HMX-3.1-07) cannot hold a lattice. (c) The only sink in a sealed, wash-down machine is the hot-cell air: at an assumed 10 W/m²K and 30 K difference [?R] each kilowatt needs about 3.3 m² of surface, and the 5.2 kW bound about 17 m², more than the frame offers, and a high-surface-area exchanger is a decontamination trap against HMX-7.2-04. (d) Bulk fluid temperature is not the cavitation limiter: cavitation follows local pressure at a restriction and dissolved air, so 'cavitation temperature' is not used as a criterion [R]; the temperature concern is oxidation and hydrolysis of the ester. The machine shall carry return-fluid and servo-manifold temperature sensors only (under 0.5 kg [?R]), with alarm at 70 °C and abort to the safe posture at 80 °C ([SPEC] on [D] limits, HMX-4.1-08). The abort is tier 2 (HMX-6.2-03); the display is tier 3.
- **HMX-4.1-07 (A)** No second hydraulic circuit ([R] on [?R] inputs). A dual-loop architecture (loop A for fine positioning, loop B a high-volume circuit for heavy lifts of 200 kg and debris clearance) is rejected. (a) The HPS pump is already pressure-compensated and variable-displacement (§4.1), and pumps are not onboard (HMX-1.4-02), so a second loop means a second tether circuit or a larger hose. (b) The spec numbers do not show starvation: a 200 kg lift at the 0.15 m/s cap needs 0.29 kW (2.8% of the 10.3 kW peak), and standing up at the 1,000 kg GVWR at 0.1 m/s needs 1.0 kW (*derived*); the accumulator covers peaks (HMX-4.3-01). (c) Doubling the flow through the baseline hoses doubles the laminar loss, from 10.7 to about 21 bar at 40 °C (10.3% of 207 bar) and from 5.6 to about 11 bar at 60 °C (5.4%), above the 5% limit of HMX-4.1-03, and at 60 L/min and 60 °C the flow is near the laminar limit (Reynolds number about 3,000), so the loss may be higher [?R]. (d) A second hose pair adds tether mass and stiffness against the 750 mm bend radius and 400 N fuse (HMX-4.2-06). (e) Flow-demand model [R] on [?R] joint speeds (Appendix B), with cylinder displacements of 100 mL/rad at hip flex and knee, 177 at the ankle, 131 at shoulder flex and hip abduction, and 59 at the elbow: walking at 0.5 m/s demands about 467 mL/s, 28 L/min, at mid-swing; standing up from kneeling at 0.1 m/s 18 L/min; a 200 kg lift at the 0.15 m/s cap 5 L/min; and walking with arm micro-articulation 31 L/min, a 1 L/min shortfall for about 2 s that the accumulator covers with 0.03 L of its 3.74 L. The 30 L/min supply is therefore sufficient, no second circuit and no valve staging are needed, and tool operation inhibits locomotion so that the tool port and the machine share the supply (HMX-5.4-07).
- **HMX-4.1-08 (A, T)** Thermal control values ([D] limits, [SPEC] setpoints). The HPS shall control the machine-inlet temperature at a setpoint of 45 to 50 °C, inside the 30 to 50 °C range one HFDU vendor gives for long life [B7-ZS] [D], with an inlet alarm above 55 °C. Return alarm 70 °C is the vendor's maximum for long life [B7-ZS] [D], and abort at 80 °C stays 24 to 40 K below vendor maximum operating temperatures of 104 °C and 120 °C [B7-QH822, B7-TOTAL] [D]. Fluid and hose thermal mass is about 77 kg × 2.06 kJ/kg·K = 159 kJ/K (22 kg tether oil, about 50 kg HPS, 5 kg machine [?R]), so a cooler failure at 6 kW heats the fluid at 2.3 K/min and takes about 15 min to go from 45 °C to the 80 °C abort: the abort is reached by alarm, not by surprise. Oxidation rate roughly doubles for each 10 K [S, rule of thumb], so holding the setpoint rather than the limit is the life measure. The machine heat load on the operator is limited by HMX-8.1-03.

### 4.2 Tether

The tether carries two hydraulic hoses (supply, return), a fibre-optic signal path, 48 V DC control power, the operator supplied-air line, and a strength member.

- **HMX-4.2-01 (T)** The tether shall not be a payload or machine load path. A tension fuse on the tether anchorage, independent of the hose couplings, shall limit the load it can impose, followed by pressure-balanced dry-break couplings (HMX-4.2-06, HMX-4.2-07). Tension shall be sensed and fed to the safety controller.
- **HMX-4.2-02 (A, T)** A hose burst shall be detected and isolated by burst-protection valves at the frame inlet. Hoses shall be guarded so a failure does not spray fluid across the work zone.
- **HMX-4.2-03 (A)** The effect of hose length and compliance on control bandwidth shall be analyzed. A 12.7 mm bore hose at about 3.9 m/s meets velocity guidance (pressure lines 15 to 20 ft/s, up to 25 ft/s at 3,000 psi and above; return lines 10 to 15 ft/s [STLE-HYD] [D, trade source]) but is laminar (Re about 1,570 at an assumed 32 cSt [?R]) and 50 m of supply plus return costs about 21.8 bar (10.5% of supply, about 1.1 kW at peak), roughly tripled with 100 cSt cold oil [R]; it is not the baseline. Pressure drop, not velocity, governs hose size (HMX-4.2-05).
- **HMX-4.2-04 (D)** A tether handler shall manage slack, snag detection, and recovery, and shall lift the tether clear of the floor rather than drag it (HMX-4.2-06).
- **HMX-4.2-05 (A, T)** Baseline hoses ([R, SPEC]): supply 16 mm (5/8 in) ID and return 19 mm (3/4 in) ID over the 50 m tether at 30 L/min peak. Velocities are 2.5 and 1.75 m/s. For the HFDU VG 46 fluid (HMX-4.1-04) the flow is laminar (Re at most about 1,500, at 60 °C on the supply hose; 1,120 at 50 °C) and the total pressure drop is 10.7 bar (5.2% of supply, 0.53 kW) at 40 °C, 7.6 bar (3.7%, 0.38 kW) at 50 °C, 5.6 bar (2.7%, 0.28 kW) at 60 °C, 24 bar (11.6%) at 20 °C, and 68 bar (33%) at 0 °C [R]. Keeping the drop at or below 5% of supply limits flow to about 20 L/min at 30 °C, 13 L/min at 20 °C, and 4.5 L/min at 0 °C, so fluid warm-up per HMX-4.1-03 is mandatory. Tether oil mass is about 22 kg (24.2 L × 0.92). This is not machine mass (HMX-4.2-01) but it loads the tether handler and the break-away limits (HMX-4.2-06). The viscosity-based flow limit of HMX-4.1-03 applies.
- **HMX-4.2-06 (A, T)** Tether limits ([SPEC] unless tagged). Tension fuse at 400 N ±20% (320 to 480 N), attached no more than 0.3 m above the floor; a pull there shifts the CoP about 12 mm nominal and 14.7 mm at 480 N, about a fifth (23%) of the 65 mm margin [R]. Routine tension is handler-held at 200 N or less, with alarm at 250 N and travel halt at 300 N. Strength member of at least 5 kN [S], matching a subsea hose umbilical with a central wire for pulls up to 5,000 N [B2-WORKERS] [D]. Dynamic bend radius of at least 750 mm [S] (that umbilical's 600 mm plus 25%) [B2-IGUS]; strain relief at the moving end only, with allowance for hoses stretching lengthwise. Length 50 m. Tether mass is about 3 kg/m, about 150 kg and off-machine [?R]; a fully dragged tether would pull about 0.7 kN, so the handler must lift it. At 14.7 N/m of drag (friction 0.5 assumed [?R]) the fuse trips once about 27 m (22 m at the 320 N lower tolerance) of tether is dragged, and the 200 N handler-held limit is passed at about 14 m (*derived*).
- **HMX-4.2-07 (A, T)** Break-away basis ([R, SPEC]). The supply hose carries about 4.1 kN of pressure thrust (207 bar × 15.9 mm bore) [R]. A pin-type coupling in the hydraulic line cannot be calibrated near 400 N, and one vendor's break force derates with internal pressure [B2-DIXON] [D]. Break-away shall therefore be the anchorage fuse of HMX-4.2-06, independent of the hose couplings, followed by pressure-balanced dry-break couplings in the hoses [SPEC].

### 4.3 Onboard Accumulators and Electrical Supply

- **HMX-4.3-01 (T)** Accumulators shall cover peak flow and the ride-through of §6.3. The 2.5 L figure is deliverable fluid, not nominal capacity. By the gas law, V₀ = ΔV / ((P₀/P₁)^(1/n) − (P₀/P₂)^(1/n)) with n about 1.4 for fast discharge [UNITEC-ACC] [D], bladder pre-charge at 90% of minimum working pressure, and a compression ratio no greater than 4:1 [PARKER-ACC, HYDAC-ACC] [D], a 100 to 207 bar window with 90 bar pre-charge needs about 6.7 L of gas volume adiabatic (about 5.4 L isothermal) to deliver 2.5 L [R]. Baseline: one 10 L bladder accumulator, 90 bar pre-charge, delivering about 3.7 L adiabatic and storing about 54 kJ [R]. A 6 L unit delivers about 2.25 L and is rejected. Pre-charge shall be checked each sortie.
- **HMX-4.3-02 (A, T)** Onboard stored energy (accumulator plus battery) shall suffice to reach the safe posture and set the payload down after loss of tether power or pressure. Requirement ([R, SPEC]): at least 3.0 L deliverable at 100 bar or more, with the sequence completed in 60 s or less. Basis [?R]: the worst case is transit with stabilizers stowed (OP-STAB fitted). With cylinder joints (HMX-5.1-02) at about 100 mL/rad on 8 joints, vane joints at 36 mL/rad on 6, and every joint sweeping 90°, the joints need about 1.6 L. Stabilizer cylinders sized to their load (four of Ø25 × 200 mm, about 10 kN each at 20.7 MPa against the 5 kN reaction) need about 0.4 L. With 0.4 L for brakes the total is about 2.4 L, so the 10 L unit's 3.7 L gives a 1.5× margin and the 3.0 L requirement holds with 1.25× margin. Realistic kneel-and-set-down sweeps (knee and hip 90°, ankle and shoulder 45°) give about 1.8 L. The 8-cylinder and 6-vane joint split and the 200 mm stabilizer stroke are assumptions. Fallback if the margin erodes: spring-deployed stabilizers held stowed by hydraulic pressure, which take the deploy volume off the accumulator and deploy on loss of pressure [SPEC]. Mechanical work is only about 5 to 10 kJ [?R], so volume, not energy, governs. *Current demand for CAR-7:* the ankle cylinders of HMX-3.2-13(b) (bore about 53 mm, 177 mL/rad against 100) add 2 × 1.57 × 77 mL = 0.24 L, and the shoulder and hip-abduction cylinders of HMX-3.2-13 (bore about 46 mm, 131 mL/rad against 100) add 0.20 L, so the joint term is 2.04 L and the conservative demand is 2.44 L (2.83 L with OP-STAB). The 3.74 L deliverable gives margins of 1.53 and 1.32, above 1.25 (CAR-7).
- **HMX-4.3-03 (T)** All components shall be protected by overpressure relief at the system pressure rating.
- **HMX-4.3-04 (T)** Continuous insulation monitoring shall cover every electrical path in a wash-down environment, with automatic disconnect on isolation loss. The 380 V bus stays in the HPS and is not routed onto the machine.
- **HMX-4.3-05 (A, T)** On loss of supply an isolation valve shall connect the accumulator only to the safe-posture manifold, so servo-valve null leakage cannot drain the reserve [SPEC]. Its failure mode shall not defeat HMX-1.6-02.
- **HMX-4.3-06 (A)** A 48 V safety battery shall power the safety controller, valve drive, and displays (HMX-8.1-02) through the safe-posture sequence and egress. Sizing, chemistry, and radiation tolerance are set in HMX-4.3-08.
- **HMX-4.3-07 (I)** Spatial ([D, verify]): a 10 L, 350 bar bladder accumulator is about Ø170 × 825 mm [PARKER-EHV] (verify), longer than the longest heritage link (0.483 m, HMX-3.4-02). It shall be mounted low on the non-articulating torso frame, not on a moving link.
- **HMX-4.3-08 (A, T)** Safety battery ([R] sizing on [?R] inputs, [D] data, [SPEC] chemistry). *Energy:* 150 W for 30 min with a 3× margin is 225 Wh; with the second safety channel (HMX-6.2-02) the load is assumed to be 225 W, giving 338 Wh; a 20% radiation-and-cold capacity derate [?R] makes the nominal pack 422 Wh (338 ÷ 0.8). At 51.2 V that is 6.6 Ah usable and 8.2 Ah nominal, and 3.4 to 5.3 kg at 80 to 100 Wh/kg pack level [?R], inside the 40 kg controllers line. The 6 min egress budget (HMX-9.1-05) sits well inside the 30 min basis. *Chemistry:* 16S LiFePO₄ (51.2 V nominal) [SPEC]. Commercial NCA and NMC-class cells lost under 2% capacity against controls after 20 Mrad (200 kGy) [B5-NASA] [D]. LFP-specific data are thinner (Appendix G, EXT-21): in-situ cells at 2.2 and 3.6 Mrad (22 to 36 kGy) faded rapidly after irradiation and lost calendar life [B5-ANS] [D], which leaves only 1.5 to 2.4× over the 15 kGy R3 qualification dose [R]. LFP wins on fire: its radiant heat-flux threshold for thermal runaway is 1.88 kW/m² against 1.158 for NCM [B5-RSC] [D] (a fire-exposure figure, not ionizing radiation). *Mode argument* [R]: in M1 the machine sees at most about 0.6 Gy over its life (HMX-7.2-10), so cell radiation tolerance is not the M1 driver; fire with the operator in the frame is, which favours LFP. The thin LFP margin matters only in M2 at R2 and R3, where no operator is in the frame; there the fallback is a single-use primary Li/CFx reserve, which lost under 2% energy after gamma exposure [B5-PEEREF] [D], or remote power [SPEC]. *Weakest link* [R]: the battery-management electronics, not the cells; they follow HMX-7.2-11 (power-management parts tested first).
- **HMX-4.3-09 (A)** No joint-level surge accumulators ([R]; see HMX-3.2-11). Accumulator discharge shall go only to the safe-posture manifold through the isolation valve of HMX-4.3-05 and to servo valves under controlled command. No accumulator shall be fitted at a joint to deliver a stored-energy surge, and the 54 kJ onboard store (HMX-3.1-04) shall not be raised for that purpose.

### 4.4 Redundant Onboard Distribution and Leak Isolation (VNRA-2)

**Applicability:** option pack OP-VNRA-H; not part of STD.

[SPEC] unless tagged. The pack is a second, independent hydraulic path to the joints that carry gravity load, on conventional tubing and manifold blocks, with leak isolation. It buys ride-through to the safe posture after a single onboard line failure; it does not remove the abort. A parallel micro-channel matrix inside the structural titanium links, with rerouting around damage, is not adopted (HMX-4.4-04).

- **HMX-4.4-01 (A, T, OP-VNRA-H)** Dual path. The hip, knee, ankle flex, and shoulder flex joints (HMX-5.1-02) shall each have two independent supply paths from the torso manifold, each with its own isolation valve and leak detection (pressure and per-path flow comparison), so that one onboard hose, fitting, or manifold-block failure cannot remove the means to reach the safe posture (supports HMX-1.6-02). Detection-plus-isolation time shall be at most 30 ms ([R] on [?R] inputs, Appendix B.10): a break in an Ø8 mm joint feeder line discharges the accumulator at about 6.4 L/s (orifice flow, discharge coefficient 0.6, 20 MPa, 897 kg/m³), and limiting the loss to 5% of the 3.74 L reserve (0.19 L) allows 29 ms; for Ø6 mm and Ø10 mm lines the limits are 52 ms and 19 ms, so joint feeder lines shall be Ø8 mm at most (a knee feeder at 7.8 L/min runs at 2.6 m/s). The 30 ms is spent as pressure-drop-rate detection with per-path flow comparison at most 15 ms, normally-open isolation valve closure at most 10 ms, and 5 ms of margin [?R], and is verified by a hydraulic transient test on the hardware limb (HMX-13.2-12).
- **HMX-4.4-02 (A, OP-VNRA-H)** Function tier. Rerouting is an availability function: tier 2 (SIL 2 / PL d) at most, and tier 3 where it only restores performance. It shall not join the tier 1 loop, because every added tier 1 function tightens the shared allocation of HMX-6.2-02 (six functions, each at most 1.6×10⁻⁸ per hour). Passive hold (HMX-5.2-03, HMX-1.6-04) never depends on rerouting, and a spurious isolation shall fail to hold, meaning loss of function and not motion. A path that closes spuriously leaves the second path (a single fault); simultaneous closure of both paths is a common-cause case covered by the FMEA of HMX-10.1-01 and the test of HMX-13.2-12.
- **HMX-4.4-03 (A, OP-VNRA-H, INFO)** The abort still applies. A failed path is a lost redundant channel, and abort is required on loss of a redundant channel (HMX-11.2-02). Rerouting keeps hydraulic power for the safe-posture sequence (HMX-4.3-02) and egress; it does not allow work to continue.
- **HMX-4.4-04 (A, T, OP-VNRA-H, INFO)** Channels inside structural links are not adopted [R, ?R]. (a) Fatigue governs the sized link (HMX-3.1-07), and an internal channel is a notch that cannot be machined; as-built additively manufactured Ti-6Al-4V internal channels have been shown to initiate fatigue cracks at the rough inner surface, and HIP does not cure roughness-initiated cracks without a surface treatment such as machining [B5-DEJESUS, B5-SHOTPEEN] [D]. (b) Internal channels trap powder and can shed particles against the ISO 4406 15/13/10 target (HMX-4.1-02) [?R]. (c) The 60 × 2 mm tube of HMX-3.1-07 has a 2 mm wall, too thin to house channels without added mass [R]. Additively manufactured manifold blocks (non-structural, finished bores, HIP, flush-qualified) are not adopted for the baseline: the torso manifold is a conventional machined block, flushed to the ISO 4406 15/13/10 target of HMX-4.1-02, and an additively manufactured block needs its own flush and fatigue qualification before it can replace it.
- **HMX-4.4-05 (A, OP-VNRA-H, INFO)** No self-sealing claim. A ruptured line leaks until its isolation valve closes. Leaks are collected under HMX-7.2-05.


## 5.0 Actuation & Joints

### 5.1 Actuator Selection

The distal joints use double-acting vane actuators; the large gravity-load joints use cylinder-and-linkage actuators (HMX-5.1-02). Sizing illustration: with half of the 1,000 kg gross load on one leg and a 0.15 m lever about the knee, knee torque is ≈ 735 N·m (*derived*). A single vane gives T = Δp·b·(Ro² − Ri²)/2, so at 20.7 MPa the actuator needs b(Ro² − Ri²)/2 ≈ 3.6×10⁻⁵ m³; for example Ro = 80 mm, Ri = 30 mm, b ≈ 13 mm (*derived*). Valve pressure drop reduces the available Δp, so a sizing margin applies. Vane stroke limits (roughly 280° for a single vane), internal leakage, and seal friction drove the choice of cylinders for the large joints and govern the distal joints that keep vanes.

- **HMX-5.1-01 (A)** Supply pressure ([D, R]). 3,000 psi is retained. Catalog rotary actuators mostly top out there, and rack-and-pinion units are generally 34 to 207 bar [B2-PARKER] [D]. Servo valves reach 5,000 psi, but their tank ports are limited to 3,000 psi [B2-IMTEK] [D]. At 5,000 psi, actuator displacement drops 40%, flow for the same power drops 40% (30 to 18 L/min), and hose loss at the same size drops 64% [R]. Housing mass barely changes, because for a given force the vessel mass is roughly independent of pressure [R]. 5,000 psi shall be reopened only as a late mass action.
- **HMX-5.1-02 (A, T)** Actuator type ([SPEC]). Hip, knee, ankle flex, and shoulder flex shall use linear cylinder-and-linkage actuators. Vane actuators are kept for distal joints. Vanes suffer internal bypass leakage and sealing difficulty at the vane corners [B2-HYDPNEU] [D], while low-friction cylinder seals have reached about a tenth of conventional friction [B2-PHYS] [D]. A hardware-limb test (HMX-6.1-05) can overturn this choice. Sizing [?R]: Ø40 mm bore, 0.08 m lever, about 150 mm stroke at the knee, assuming half torque at stroke ends (a plain clevis cylinder-and-lever gives half torque or less there [B2-PMT]). That is about 100 mL/rad against the vane's 36 mL/rad, 2.8× the displacement at the same torque (*derived*). Peak torque is 2.08 kN·m and half torque 1.04 kN·m at stroke ends, against 735 N·m at the 1,000 kg GVWR: a margin of 1.41×, or 1.23× with ψ = 1.15 (CAR-3), before valve pressure drop (*derived*). Cylinders of 0.40 to 0.45 m shall fit inside the 0.48 m heritage links.

### 5.2 Joint Requirements

- **HMX-5.2-01 (T)** Joint breakaway torque shall be no more than 2% of rated joint torque (14.7 N·m at 735 N·m), with a 1% target, across the wear, contamination, and temperature envelope, so that impedance control rather than friction sets the feel at low force ([R, ?R]). Precedents: stiction at 1% of full-range torque or less in a small rolling-diaphragm actuator, and a 2009 electro-hydrostatic knee back-driving at about 0.7% (14 of 1,988 N·m) [B2-DISNEY, B2-IMD] [D, directional]. A vendor article puts stiction at 2 to 4× kinetic friction, so kinetic friction shall stay at 0.5 to 1% or less [R]. At the knee cylinder, 2% is about 184 N at the rod, 0.7% of its pressure force, which needs low-friction seals (HMX-5.1-02). At 1:1 scaling the operator would feel about 29 N of breakaway at a 0.5 m handle arm, so friction compensation from the dual-channel joint-force sensing (HMX-5.2-05) is also required.
- **HMX-5.2-02 (A, T)** Rolling diaphragms or bellows are retained as a contamination barrier, not as a pressure-matching element. Joint seals shall exclude particulate and fluid ingress, and leak detection shall sit between the hydraulic circuit and the exterior.
- **HMX-5.2-03 (A, T)** Every joint carrying gravity load shall have a pilot-operated load-holding valve and a spring-applied, hydraulically released brake. Both shall fail to hold.
- **HMX-5.2-04 (A, T)** Joint torque, force, and rate at operator-contact interfaces shall remain within human-safe limits under fault conditions, enforced independently of the main controller ([D, SPEC]). Machine-imposed cuff force under any single fault shall stay at or below the value for its body location: shoulder 60 N, upper arm 70 N, pelvis 90 N, forearm 100 N, shin 150 N, thigh 140 N, calf 130 N, palm 150 N. These are a 112-subject study's proposed pain-onset limits (75th percentile, 70% male, blunt pinching) [B2-FRONTIERS] [D]. They come from small-patch cobot contact and ISO/TS 15066 itself calls them preliminary, so applying them to cuffs is [?R]. Double faults and structural failures shall stay under about 3× these values, the study's ratio of blunt-injury onset to pain onset [D]. Torque, rate, and stop rules are in HMX-5.2-10.
- **HMX-5.2-05 (A, T)** Operator-input sensing shall use at least two independent channels per joint, cross-checked. Disagreement drives the joint to its safe state; no sensor failure shall command uncommanded assist or full pressure.
- **HMX-5.2-06 (A, I)** Operator-input mechanisms shall be free of jam-prone linkages, with a defined response if an input is jammed or out of range. A jammed position-input mechanism was a suspected cause of heritage runaway [GE-71 p.17].
- **HMX-5.2-07 (A, I, D)** Mechanical end-stops and pinch or crush shields shall be designed in from the start. The operator-reachable range of every sensing interface shall be smaller than the slave's mechanical range, and contact at a stop shall never generate a drive signal [GE-71 pp.14, 16].
- **HMX-5.2-08 (A, T, GRP)** Sub-requirements HMX-5.2-08.1 to HMX-5.2-08.6.
- **HMX-5.2-08.1 (A, T)** Each operator-input sensing interface shall also have a minimum range: enough that the operator can command the slave's full travel and generate the signals the adjacent joints need, including in double support, without reaching a stop.
- **HMX-5.2-08.2 (A, T)** Per-interface bounds ([SPEC] on [D] perception data, [R] arithmetic): the force channel shall resolve at most 0.5 N with noise at most 0.5 N rms, about a third of one just-noticeable difference at the 20 N mean effort of HMX-6.5-06, since force discrimination is 7 to 10% over 0.5 to 200 N and arm-joint Weber fractions are 0.08 to 0.13 [B9-FORCE] [D].
- **HMX-5.2-08.3 (A, T)** Full scale shall be 3× the pain-onset limit of the interface in HMX-5.2-04, matching the 3× double-fault ratio: shoulder 180 N, upper arm 210 N, pelvis 270 N, forearm 300 N, calf 390 N, thigh 420 N, shin 450 N, palm 450 N, and about 2 kN for the footplate (981 N operator weight × 1.25 dynamic factor × 1.5 [?R]); every full scale exceeds the 100 N minimum range of HMX-5.2-09.
- **HMX-5.2-08.4 (A, T)** Cuff stiffness shall be 4 to 10 N/mm with a travel to the hard stop of 10 to 25 mm (25 mm at 4 N/mm reaches the 100 N minimum range, 10 mm at 10 N/mm), the lower bound that the heritage foot-centering failure demands; the operator-reachable range shall lie at least 2° inside the slave's mechanical stops (HMX-5.2-07), and 25 mm at the 0.25 m thigh lever is 5.7°.
- **HMX-5.2-08.5 (A, T)** Contact at a stop shall saturate the command, not extend it. The sensor chain needs at least 11 bits (±450 N at 0.5 N is 1,800 steps).
- **HMX-5.2-08.6 (A, T)** The 5 N deadband of HMX-5.2-09 is 2 to 3× the just-noticeable difference at 20 N and so is perceptible; it is retained against drift creep and shall be checked for feel in the mockup (HMX-8.1-05). Verification is the mockup and the hardware-limb test (HMX-6.1-05) [GE-71 p.16].
- **HMX-5.2-09 (T)** Control-quality limits per joint class ([R] on [D] valve data and [?R] error budgets; verified on a hardware limb, HMX-6.1-05) [NASA-68 pp.21-22]. *Backlash* (pin and linkage slack referred to the joint): a hand-position error of at most 5 mm over the 1.0 m reach, root-sum-squared over six arm joints, is 2.0 mrad per joint (0.16 mm of slack at the 0.08 m lever); a foot-placement error of at most 10 mm over 0.95 m and six leg joints is 4.3 mrad, carried as 4 mrad (0.32 mm); back flexion 2 mrad; vane joints 5 mrad. *Valve*: threshold at most 0.5%, hysteresis at most 3%, null shift at most 2% per 38 K of rated signal, which the Moog 730, 760, 772, and G761 series meet (threshold 0.3 to 1.0% across the families checked) [B8-MOOG] [D]; with a 10 L/min valve on a 100 mL/rad joint a 0.5% threshold is 0.0083 rad/s, a limit-cycle amplitude of 0.27 mrad at the 5 Hz crossover, and a 2% null shift is 1.06 mrad of hold error without integral action, so every position loop shall carry integral action or null re-trim. *Friction*: breakaway at most 2% and kinetic at most 1% of rated torque (HMX-5.2-01). *Stiffness*: the structure in series with the hydraulics gives a lowest unloaded mode of at least 10 Hz (HMX-3.1-09). *Inertia*: reflected inertia at most 12.8 kg·m² for leg joints (k_eff 5.07×10⁴ N·m/rad at 10 Hz) and 15.8 kg·m² unloaded for arm joints (6.2×10⁴ N·m/rad); a 40 kg tool at 1.0 m gives 6.3 Hz and falls under the crossover schedule of HMX-3.1-09. *Cross-coupling*: off-diagonal gain between joints in double support at most 0.2 of the diagonal (−14 dB) [?R, S, heuristic]. *Force deadband and drift*: a 5 N deadband at the cuff with zero drift at most 2 N (40% of the deadband), so a drifting offset never creeps a joint (HMX-6.5-06); the deadband shall not exceed 5% of the minimum sensing range of HMX-5.2-08, which therefore shall be at least 100 N for a 5 N deadband.
- **HMX-5.2-10 (A, T, GRP)** Cuff torque, rate, and stops. Sub-requirements HMX-5.2-10.1 to HMX-5.2-10.3.
- **HMX-5.2-10.1 (A, T)** Cuff torque is force times cuff arm; thigh 140 N × 0.25 m is about 35 N·m, about 21× below the 735 N·m joint (*derived*), so the independent limiter required by HMX-5.2-04 shall be mechanical: compliant, force-sensed cuffs plus clearance [R].
- **HMX-5.2-10.2 (A, T)** Speed at any OIF contact surface under fault shall not exceed 0.1 m/s [?R]; the study's 24.4 MPa/m contact stiffness and 16.5 kg pendulum give about 0.13 to 0.26 m/s at 70 to 140 N. Mechanical stops, not software limits, shall keep range inside human anatomical limits, as one exoskeleton developer states [B2-PAGES] [D].
- **HMX-5.2-10.3 (A, T)** COVR protocols for torque limitation and restraining energy shall be the verification method [B2-STANDICT] [D].

### 5.3 Servo Valves

- **HMX-5.3-01 (T)** Flapper-nozzle servo valves are used. Valve 90° phase point shall be at least 50 Hz and the −3 dB point at least 40 Hz, at ±25% input, 21 MPa supply, and 40 °C fluid ([SPEC] on [D]), and in any case at least 3× the highest joint hydraulic resonance (HMX-5.3-04). Valves shall be mounted at the joint manifold to minimize line volume and lag.
- **HMX-5.3-02 (T)** Torque-motor coil insulation, seals, and fluid shall be gamma-qualified as an assembly to at least 1.5× the class TID of §7.1, with null shift, hysteresis, and response measured before and after ([S, ?R]). Coil insulation shall be polyimide or ceramic class: ITER remote-handling work replaced ETFE with polyimide after commercial servo motors failed at the insulation at 8 MGy [B3-JAEA] [D]. Moog servo valves have run in ITER remote-handling hydraulics [B3-TUT] [D]. Elastomer radiation figures conflict between sources [B3-KNS], so test results, not literature values, govern. The 1.5× margin is a proposal [SPEC].
- **HMX-5.3-03 (A)** Contamination sensitivity of the valves shall be covered by HMX-4.1-02 and by a valve null-drift check every sortie.
- **HMX-5.3-04 (A, T)** Valve bandwidth basis. The valve 90° phase point should exceed the load resonant frequency by a factor of three or more [B3-MOOG] [D]. Knee resonance estimate [?R]: f = (1/2π)·√(β·A²·(1/V₁ + 1/V₂)/M), with effective bulk modulus β of 0.7 GPa, piston area 1.257×10⁻³ m², 119 mL per chamber (half stroke plus 25 mL of line and valve volume), and reflected mass M = J/r² for joint inertia J of 10 to 50 kg·m² and 0.08 m lever; this gives 17 to 8 Hz, so the valve needs about 52 to 23 Hz (*derived*). The 50 Hz requirement covers the lightest case. A catalogue servovalve meets it: the Moog J869 series lists −3 dB above 40 Hz and 90° phase above 50 Hz at ±25% input, 21 MPa, 40 °C, on petroleum oil [B3-J869] [D]. Response on the ester at 40 to 60 °C shall be confirmed with the vendor, and the hardware-limb test (HMX-6.1-05) shall replace the estimate with a measured resonance.

### 5.4 End Effectors and Tool Interface

**Applicability:** HMX-5.4-01 and HMX-5.4-03 (hand end effectors, operator hand protection) are STD; HMX-5.4-02 and HMX-5.4-04 to 5.4-11 (tool interface, UHTH) are option pack OP-UHTH.

Hardiman I gave each hand two powered joints (thumb and thumb-tip flex) with hydromechanical rate control and force feedback [GE-71 p.3A]. A 1968 survey describes one servoed hand joint and an unservoed on-off thumb tip [NASA-68 p.99], so the heritage hand differs by source and the force-feedback hand is not confirmed heritage [?].

- **HMX-5.4-01 (A, D, GRP)** Sub-requirements HMX-5.4-01.1 to HMX-5.4-01.4.
- **HMX-5.4-01.1 (A, D)** Hand baseline ([SPEC] on [R] arithmetic, [D] grip data). Each hand shall have two powered degrees of freedom: grip close (a servoed thumb joint closing against a palm-finger structure) and thumb-tip positioning (not force-bearing), with force feedback to the operator; these are the four hand joints of HMX-3.4-04, so the count stays 30. *Rated grip.* 2.5 kN per hand. A two-handed 200 kg brief lift loads each hand with 100 kg (981 N); at an assumed friction coefficient of 0.3 over two opposed contacts the normal force is 981 ÷ (2 × 0.3) = 1.64 kN, and × 1.5 gives 2.45 kN; the continuous 100 kg rating needs 1.2 kN.
- **HMX-5.4-01.2 (A, D)** *Limit and feedback.* Grip force shall be limited to the lesser of 2.5 kN and 150 N (the palm limit, HMX-5.2-04) × the selected force ratio, so at 20:1 the operator feels 125 N, and holding the full 200 kg brief needs a ratio of at least 16.7:1; the hand ratio equals the arm ratio (HMX-6.1-03). Normative grip strength at ages 20 to 39 is about 54 to 55 kg (540 N) for men and 32 to 36 kg (310 to 350 N) for women [B9-GRIP] [D, secondary], so 125 N is well inside operator capability.
- **HMX-5.4-01.3 (A, D)** *Sizing.* The grip joint at a 0.08 m lever needs 200 N·m, 276 N·m usable, 13.3 mL/rad as a vane or linear actuator; the thumb-tip joint is at most 40 N·m usable and 2 mL/rad [?R]. A relief valve in the grip circuit shall set the 2.5 kN limit independently of software.
- **HMX-5.4-01.4 (A, D)** *Hold.* The grip shall have a hydraulic lock and a mechanical latch that fail to hold (HMX-1.6-04), with a manual release that does not need the operator's hand (as HMX-5.4-05), so a held payload is not dropped on loss of power and the grip stays latched during the safe-posture set-down (no safe-posture volume is added). *Mass.* About 2 kg for the grip actuator and 0.5 kg for the thumb-tip actuator per hand, 5 kg in all [?R], taken from the 7 kg actuator margin of HMX-3.2-13(e); the grip valve is the seventh valve already counted per arm.
- **HMX-5.4-02 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** A quick-change tool interface shall accept decommissioning tooling (cutters, grapples, positioners), with load rating, contamination sealing, and dropped-tool provisions, implemented as the UHTH (HMX-5.4-04 to 5.4-11).
- **HMX-5.4-03 (A)** The operator's hand shall be protected inside the end-effector housing, as in the heritage co-axial concept [GE-71 p.2].
- **HMX-5.4-04 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Universal Heavy-Tool Hardpoint ([SPEC] on [R] checks). Each forearm shall carry structural rails and a tool carriage so that a tool locks into the slave frame, its weight reaches the floor through the frame (HMX-1.4-01), and the operator's hand dictates micro-articulation through the force-feedback tracking of §6.1 at the selected ratio. The tool weight is not displayed to the operator (displayed at 20:1 a 50 kg tool would add 24.5 N). A tool is payload: its mass counts against the 200 kg and 100 kg ratings and its centre of mass against the CoP margin (HMX-3.2-02, HMX-3.2-03). A 50 kg tool is half the 100 kg continuous rating. In single support the limit is m·d ≤ 0.0813 × (743 + m) kg·m (HMX-3.2-03): a 50 kg tool alone fits out to 1.29 m, but a 50 kg tool plus a 50 kg workpiece (the 100 kg rating) at 0.9 m is 90 kg·m against a limit of 68.5 kg·m, so that case needs the stabilizers; in single support 100 kg holds out to 0.69 m (*derived*). Hardware: 6 to 11 kg for rails, lock, tool port, and isolators [?R] (HMX-3.1-08).
- **HMX-5.4-05 (A, T, OP-UHTH, GRP)** Tool lock and release. Sub-requirements HMX-5.4-05.1 to HMX-5.4-05.3.
- **HMX-5.4-05.1 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** The tool lock shall hold on loss of power, signal, or pressure (HMX-1.6-04) and shall have a manual release that does not need the operator's hand inside the end-effector housing (HMX-5.4-03), for crane recovery and rescue.
- **HMX-5.4-05.2 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** A tool retention lanyard shall hold a released tool.
- **HMX-5.4-05.3 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** The tool shall stay on the machine through operator egress (HMX-9.1-07).
- **HMX-5.4-06 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Tool loads. Tool reactions (jaw closing, percussion, torque reaction, snag on rebar) are external loads: the design load on rails, forearm, and arm chain is rated load × ψ × Nd (HMX-3.2-10), with the accident case of 1.5 × rated load. The tool's own pressure limiter shall bound the force it can apply, set no higher than the structural design load. Rail load case [R] on [?R] inputs: tool weight up to 40 kg (392 N) plus an assumed feed force of 400 N (20 N at the 20:1 ratio), each × ψ × Nd = 3.45, is 1.35 kN and 1.38 kN; with the tool centre 0.35 m from the rail the moment is (392 + 400) × 0.35 × 3.45 = 957 N·m, which the forearm joints and rails shall carry with the 1.5× accident case.
- **HMX-5.4-07 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Tool supply. A tool shall draw from a dedicated tool port, pressure- and flow-limited, with an isolation valve that fails closed and leak detection (HMX-7.2-05). The tool port shall be limited to 20 L/min at no more than 140 bar through a flow regulator, a pressure reducer from 207 bar, and a relief at 155 bar, with a return back pressure of at most 17 bar (the Stanley limit [B7-STANLEY-BR67] [D]); tool operation shall inhibit locomotion and limit machine demand to 10 L/min, so the total stays within the 30 L/min of §4.1. A class T2 tool (HMX-5.4-10) may draw 26 L/min only with machine demand held at 4 L/min. Electrical, gas, or cooling needs of a tool are defined with its class; none are provided in the STD tether.
- **HMX-5.4-08 (A, T, OP-UHTH, GRP)** Tool enable and stop. Sub-requirements HMX-5.4-08.1 to HMX-5.4-08.2.
- **HMX-5.4-08.1 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Cutting, crushing, and thermal actions shall need continuous positive enable (a dead-man grip and a second channel); loss of enable, signal, or pressure shall de-energize the tool, and the lock of HMX-5.4-05 shall hold.
- **HMX-5.4-08.2 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** The tool's working zone shall lie outside the operator's reachable range, with pinch and crush shields per HMX-5.2-07. Tool de-energize is tier 2 (HMX-6.2-03).
- **HMX-5.4-09 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Tool classes. Shears, grapples, and scabblers are admitted subject to HMX-5.4-06 to 5.4-08, with tool vibration and shock isolated from the force-sensing and stability loops (HMX-6.1-09); the scaling lockout of HMX-6.1-08 applies with the tool fitted, and the stability plant model shall include tool mass and compliance. A plasma torch or other thermal cutter is not baseline: it needs its own hazard case covering ignition of a fluid spray (the ester passes spray-ignition and hot-surface tests above 400 °C [B3-FUCHS], which a plasma arc exceeds), aerosol and airborne contamination from cutting, hose routing, and fire response (HMX-7.2-06). Scabblers and needle guns are not catalogued: their supply (pneumatic, electric) is not in the tether.
- **HMX-5.4-10 (A, T, OP-UHTH, INFO)** Tool classes ([D] vendor data, [SPEC] admission). *T1, light hand-held hydraulic hammers:* 19 to 26 kg, 20 L/min at 100 to 120 bar, 40 to 95 J, 1,380 to 1,830 blows/min (23 to 31 Hz), 6.6 to 9.4 m/s² at the handle: Hycon HH15 19.2 kg, 20 L/min, 100 bar, 40 J; HH25 25.3 to 25.8 kg, 20 or 30 L/min, 120 bar, 85 or 95 J [B7-HYCON]; Atlas Copco LH 190 20 L/min at 95 to 110 bar [B7-ATLAS-LH190]. Admitted. *T2, heavy hand-held breakers:* 29 to 39 kg, 26 to 40 L/min at 105 to 140 bar: Hycon HH35 29.4 kg, 30 to 40 L/min, 130 bar, 130 J [B7-HYCON]; Stanley BR67 30 to 33 kg and BR87 38 to 39 kg, 26 to 34 L/min at 105 to 140 bar, relief 155 bar [B7-STANLEY-BR67, B7-STANLEY-BR87]. Conditional: only at 26 L/min with the machine at 4 L/min (HMX-5.4-07), and the 30 L/min of the supply leaves no margin. *T3, robot-class crushers and shears:* Husqvarna DCR 90 32 L/min at 250 bar, DCR 100 55 L/min at 200 bar, DCR 500 85 L/min and 295 kg [B7-HUSQ]: excluded (flow above the supply, pressure above 207 bar, mass above the rating). Tools count as payload (HMX-5.4-04); the lighter classes cost no rating alone.
- **HMX-5.4-11 (A, T, OP-UHTH)** Vibration isolation ([R] on [D] frequencies). Tool blow frequency of 23 to 31 Hz lies on the structural mode of 10 to 12 Hz harmonics and above the 17 Hz hydraulic mode (HMX-3.1-09), so the tool carriage shall sit on an isolator with a natural frequency of at most 8 Hz, giving transmissibility 1 ÷ ((f ÷ f_n)² − 1) of 0.14 at 23 Hz and 0.07 at 30.5 Hz (a 9.4 m/s² handle level becomes at most 1.3 m/s²); the force-sensing path shall carry a notch at the tool blow frequency (HMX-5.4-09, HMX-6.1-09).

## 6.0 Control & Software

### 6.1 Control Architecture

M1 (coupled) uses force-feedback impedance control: handle and footplate sensors detect operator intent and the slave frame amplifies it by the selected ratio. M2 (remote bilateral) adds a master frame and reflects environment force back to the operator.

- **HMX-6.1-01 (T)** The control loop shall run at 1 kHz or faster, with force-to-valve latency (sensor sample to valve command) of at most 2 ms and jitter of at most 0.2 ms ([SPEC]; basis in HMX-6.1-08).
- **HMX-6.1-02 (A, T)** Stability shall be shown by passivity or small-gain analysis across bounded operator-impedance and environment-impedance ranges, with margin (HMX-6.1-09), then by operator-in-loop and rigid-contact tests at rated payload.
- **HMX-6.1-03 (T)** Force-scaling changes shall occur only with the load held stable, shall be rate-limited, and shall be displayed.
- **HMX-6.1-04 (A, T, GRP)** M2 link ([SPEC] on [R] arithmetic, [D] literature). Sub-requirements HMX-6.1-04.1 to HMX-6.1-04.2.
- **HMX-6.1-04.1 (A, T)** *Budget.* The round trip from master sample to slave command and back shall be at most 8 ms with jitter at most 0.5 ms. The phase margin left at the 30° floor of HMX-6.1-09 after the 12 to 15° already allocated is 15 to 18°, and each millisecond costs 1.8° at the assumed 5 Hz crossover, so 8.3 to 10 ms is available and 8 ms is taken (Appendix B.8); the nominal 45° margin would allow about 17 ms. Fibre propagation over 50 m is about 0.5 µs round trip, so the budget is spent in processing and the network, which shall be deterministic (a 1 kHz cycle with at most 3 cycles of latency) [S]. *Basis.* Even a small delay can destabilize a nominally stable bilateral system, stable and transparent operation is possible at small delays with three-channel control, and large delays need model-mediated control [B10-TELEOP] [D]; the link is wired fibre only, and wireless or long-distance M2 is outside this baseline and needs its own qualification (HMX-9.1-03). Survey figures bound human performance but do not set the budget: human loop delay is about 113 to 328 ms and force feedback loses value as delay grows [NASA-68 pp.31-32].
- **HMX-6.1-04.2 (A, T)** *Link loss.* A watchdog shall trip after 3 consecutive missed or late frames (3 ms at 1 kHz) and the slave shall then hold within 1 ms (HMX-5.2-03, §6.3), so the uncommanded motion is at most 1.3 rad/s × 4 ms = 5.2 mrad (0.3°) at a joint and 2 mm at the 0.5 m/s gait speed. The master shall go passive with an alarm, and the link shall resume only by an explicit operator handshake, never automatically.
- **HMX-6.1-05 (A, T, GRP)** Sub-requirements HMX-6.1-05.1 to HMX-6.1-05.3.
- **HMX-6.1-05.1 (A, T)** Stability analysis shall include interaction between serially connected servo joints and shall be verified on a hardware multi-joint limb (for example ankle, knee, and hip) before whole-machine integration [GE-71 p.9].
- **HMX-6.1-05.2 (A, T)** Interaction through series joints can be unstable with no operator in the loop, so it shall be treated as a stability path in its own right.
- **HMX-6.1-05.3 (A, T)** Compensation terms (proportional, rate, lag, velocity feedback) shall be adjustable over wide ranges in development and locked for operation under HMX-6.4-03; the heritage three-joint bilateral stability result to 1,500 lb came from analog simulation [NASA-68 pp.47, 49].
- **HMX-6.1-06 (A, T)** Models used to support stability claims shall be validated against hardware in each leg operating mode, and shall not be used for any mode in which they are unvalidated [GE-71 p.10].
- **HMX-6.1-07 (A)** Arm and leg control shall run as separate loops, coupled only through the mode logic and the safety controller [GE-71 p.7].
- **HMX-6.1-08 (A, T)** Latency basis ([R] with [?R] inputs). A delay T adds 360° × f × T of phase lag at crossover f. With an assumed operator force-loop crossover of 5 Hz [?R], 2 ms adds 3.6°; the valve (50 Hz at 90°, damping 0.7 to 1.0) adds about 8 to 11°, for about 12 to 15° in total [R]. Human balance-correcting reactions start about 130 ms after a disturbance [B3-BECK] [D], so 2 ms is about 1.5% of the human delay. The force loop shall close locally at the joint manifold, not over the tether (50 m of fibre adds about 0.25 µs, HMX-6.1-04). The margin shall be confirmed by the stability analysis of HMX-6.1-02 and the stability margin (HMX-6.1-09).
- **HMX-6.1-09 (A, T)** Stability margins ([D] criteria, [S] application, [SPEC] floor and method). *Nominal:* gain margin at least 6 dB and phase margin at least 45°, the MIL-F-9490D flight-control criteria, now superseded by SAE AS94900 [B5-SJSU] [D]; they are aerospace criteria, so applying them to an exoskeleton is [S]. *Worst-case floor:* 6 dB and 30° across the full uncertainty set [SPEC]; the F-16 relaxes the margins in some regimes and EF-2000 reportedly used 6 dB and 20° [B5-KOREASCI] [D, secondary]. *Method:* disk margins, which give a stronger stability guarantee than classical margins [B5-MATHWORKS] [D], with the operator modelled as any passive impedance, the robust-control formulation for worn robots [B5-FRONTIERS] [D]. For a symmetric disk margin α the gain range is (2−α)/(2+α) to (2+α)/(2−α) and the phase range is ±2·atan(α/2), so the nominal criteria need α ≥ 0.83 (45° governs; gain range 7.7 dB) and the floor needs α ≥ 0.67 (6 dB governs; phase ±36.9°) (*derived*, Appendix B) [R]. *Uncertainty set:* free air to rigid contact, payload 0 to 200 kg, machine-inlet fluid 40 to 60 °C, force scaling 1:1 to 20:1, any added operator-sensing path (HMX-6.6-03), and the M2 round-trip delay up to 8 ms (HMX-6.1-04). *Scaling:* more force amplification narrows the stability range in hydraulic extender work [B5-ISC] [D]; margins shall be shown at the highest scaling offered, and any scaling above the demonstrated value shall be locked out by the controller (extends HMX-6.1-03). *Phase budget* [R]: 12 to 15° is already allocated to latency and the valve (HMX-6.1-08), leaving about 30 to 33° of the 45° and about 15 to 18° of the 30° floor for everything else; each extra millisecond of delay costs 1.8° at the assumed 5 Hz crossover [?R]. *Status:* neither the criteria nor the phase budget can be shown to pass without a plant model; they are targets until the hardware-limb test (HMX-6.1-05, HMX-6.1-06).

### 6.2 Independent Safety Controller

- **HMX-6.2-01 (A, T)** A separate controller (diverse hardware and software, radiation-tolerant by design) shall monitor joint velocity and torque, hydraulic pressure, centre of pressure, tether tension, and operator presence, and shall de-energize valves and apply brakes without the main software.
- **HMX-6.2-02 (A)** Integrity levels ([R] with [SPEC] tiers). Allocation: the 1×10⁻⁸ per hour fatality target of HMX-1.6-06, shared by six tier 1 functions, each with an assumed 0.1 probability that a dangerous failure is fatal [?R], gives PFH of about 1.7×10⁻⁸ per hour per function, so the lower part of the SIL 3 band (10⁻⁸ to below 10⁻⁷ per hour) [B3-PILZ, B3-RS] [D]. **Tier 1, SIL 3 / PL e, PFH target at most 1.6×10⁻⁸ per hour (2×10⁻⁸ would exceed the 1.67×10⁻⁸ allocation by 20%):** independent safety controller de-energize and brake apply (HMX-6.2-01); load-holding and brake hold-on-fault (HMX-5.2-03); hardwired emergency-stop chain (HMX-9.1-03); dual-channel operator-input cross-check (HMX-5.2-05); stability-margin freeze (HMX-3.2-04); uncommanded-motion detection. **Tier 2, SIL 2 / PL d (PFH 10⁻⁷ to below 10⁻⁶ per hour):** tether tension halt, accumulator isolation and safe-posture sequencing, dose hard stop, insulation monitoring, M2 link-loss hold, escape-air alarm, and, if adopted, SHM strain-limit abort (HMX-3.5-02) and onboard line isolation (HMX-4.4-02). **Tier 3, SIL 1 / PL c:** displays, advisory alarms, fluid and cleanliness gating. PL e is 10⁻⁸ to below 10⁻⁷ per hour and PL d is 10⁻⁷ to below 10⁻⁶ [B3-FESTO] [D]. Architecture for SIL 3: hardware fault tolerance 1 with safe failure fraction of 90% or more, or fault tolerance 0 with 99% or more [B3-MOTION] [D]. Radiation-induced upsets shall be in the failure-rate and diagnostic-coverage figures (HMX-6.4-02). The tiers shall be cross-checked against the IEC 62061 and ISO 13849-1 risk graphs during the FMEA (HMX-10.1-01).
- **HMX-6.2-03 (A)** Tiering of added functions ([SPEC]). Tool de-energize (HMX-5.4-08), the return-temperature abort (HMX-4.1-06), and the single-support knee-lever limiter of HMX-3.2-13(a) are tier 2 (SIL 2 / PL d). If the tool hazard analysis shows a route to operator injury that a tier 2 function does not cover, that function moves to tier 1 and the allocation of HMX-6.2-02 shall be recomputed before adoption (the tool hazard analysis of HMX-5.4-09). The MEER and the overhead guard are passive mechanical structures verified by test (HMX-13.2-04, HMX-13.2-05); their release-position and presence monitors are tier 3. No function beyond the six of HMX-6.2-02 is tier 1, so the six-function allocation stands.


### 6.3 Safe States

| Function | Fault | Required safe state |
|---|---|---|
| Joint impedance control | Sensor loss or disagreement | Hold last position with limited, bounded assist; alarm; never full pressure |
| Hydraulic supply | Loss of tether pressure | Load-holding valves and brakes hold; accumulator isolated to the safe-posture manifold (HMX-4.3-05); ride-through to safe posture (payload set down, kneel or sit), at least 3.0 L in 60 s or less (HMX-4.3-02) |
| Hose | Burst or flow surge | Isolation valve closes; hold; alarm |
| Stability | Margin below threshold | Freeze; deploy stabilizers if fitted; controlled kneel |
| Operator presence (M1) | Loss of contact or dead-man release | Assist to zero; brakes applied; hold |
| Communications (M2) | Link loss (3 missed frames) | Slave holds within 1 ms (HMX-6.1-04); master passive with alarm; resume by operator handshake only |
| Tether | Over-tension or snag | Alarm at 250 N; halt travel and hold at 300 N; tension fuse releases at 400 N ±20% (HMX-4.2-06) |
| Main versus safety controller | Disagreement or watchdog trip | De-energize valves; fail to hold |
| Tool (OP-UHTH) | Loss of enable, signal, or pressure; lock fault | Tool de-energized; lock holds; alarm (HMX-5.4-08) |
| Fluid temperature | Return above 70 °C / above 80 °C | Alarm / abort to safe posture (HMX-4.1-06) |

### 6.4 Software Requirements

- **HMX-6.4-01 (A)** Software shall follow an IEC 61508-class lifecycle at the assigned integrity level.
- **HMX-6.4-02 (T)** Hardware-in-the-loop testing and fault injection shall cover sensor loss, valve failure, radiation-induced upsets, and power transients.
- **HMX-6.4-03 (I)** Software shall be under configuration control, with field update locked out during a sortie.
- **HMX-6.4-04 (T)** The alarm philosophy shall define advisory, caution, and warning levels so critical alerts are never masked.

### 6.5 Leg Control Modes

The heritage legs had twelve interacting joints in three control modes, and most of the recorded failures occurred there [GE-71 p.10].

- **HMX-6.5-01 (A, T)** Leg control shall define swing (foot floating), single-support, and double-support modes, with explicit transition logic enforced by the safety controller.
- **HMX-6.5-02 (A, T)** In double support the closed kinematic chain removes independent motions. The controller shall command only the independent degrees of freedom and hold the remainder compliant, so actively controlled joints cannot fight each other [GE-71 pp.11, 17-18].
- **HMX-6.5-03 (T)** A fault or contact event shall not produce a runaway that the operator's instinctive reaction makes worse. Tests shall include operators not trained on the system [GE-71 p.16].
- **HMX-6.5-04 (T)** Footplate force sensing shall give the operator ground-contact cues. Heritage legs used indirect force feedback and lost those cues in ride mode [GE-71 pp.3A, 14].
- **HMX-6.5-05 (A, T)** Leg input method ([SPEC] on [D] literature and [R] cuff arithmetic). The leg input is force-referenced admittance: the cuff force, through the dual-channel stiff force-sensing cuffs of HMX-5.2-05, sets a desired joint velocity through an admittance gain, and a position inner loop follows it, the dual-loop structure of recent lower-limb exoskeleton work [B8-FRONTIERS, B8-SPRINGER] [D], whose authors cite friction as what degrades position tracking and raises interaction impedance [B8-IEEE] [D]. The heritage tickler, a position-referenced input, was found unsatisfactory for the legs [NASA-68 p.100]. In numbers: knee breakaway of at most 14.7 N·m is 59 N at the 0.25 m thigh-cuff lever, 42% of the 140 N thigh pain-onset limit (HMX-5.2-04) if uncompensated and 5.9 N at 90% friction compensation; a knee speed of 1.3 rad/s is 0.33 m/s at the cuff, and 20 N of effort at full gait speed gives an admittance gain of 0.016 m/s per N. A position-referenced input would need cuff compliance, and holding the limb within 5 mm of alignment at 20 N needs 4 N/mm, a cuff as stiff as the force-sensing one, so it gains nothing. Position referencing is the fallback if residual friction after compensation exceeds 5% of rated torque.
- **HMX-6.5-06 (T)** Leg-mode acceptance ([SPEC] limits on [R] arithmetic). In swing, single support, and double support at full gait speed the cuff effort shall be at most 20 N mean and 60 N peak (43% of the 140 N thigh limit), and at most 10 N mean in single support; the 5 N force deadband and 2 N drift limit of HMX-5.2-09 shall hold; and 59 consecutive trials (20 untrained operators × 3) without loss of control or a stability margin below 65 mm at the 5° test slope show a failure probability of at most 5% at 95% confidence (n = ln 0.05 ÷ ln 0.95); failure of this test triggers gate (a) of HMX-1.4-08.

### 6.6 Operator-Intent Feed-Forward (VNRA-3)

**Applicability:** option pack OP-VNRA-I; not part of STD.

[SPEC] unless tagged. The pack is a trial of surface EMG (sEMG) and kinematic intent prediction in the OIF cuffs. Its premise is limited: the 113 to 328 ms of [NASA-68] is human reaction time to a stimulus, which no machine can remove, and it is not machine loop lag (HMX-6.1-04, HMX-6.1-08). In M1 the machine already responds to cuff force within 2 ms. The measurable gap is electromechanical delay (EMD), the time from muscle electrical onset to force at the limb, which is classically 30 to 100 ms [B5-CAVANAGH] [D], about 50 ms from EMG to force in a quadriceps study [B5-BEGOVIC] [D], and about 60 to 84 ms in elbow flexion [B5-ELBOW] [D]. A predictor could therefore lead the force channel by tens of milliseconds; it does not erase lag. The benefit in M1 is uncertain and small against a 2 ms loop; the risk is not, since Hardiman I's runaway was made worse by operator reflex (HMX-6.5-03). It is carried as a default-off trial.

- **HMX-6.6-01 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** Scope. Intent feed-forward shall be M1-only, selectable, and off by default; the machine shall meet all requirements with it off (HMX-1.4-06). Cuff electrodes sit on the operator side inside protective clothing; sweat, cuff shift, and motion artefact degrade sEMG, and electrode placement alone can shift onset detection by up to 20 ms [B5-EJAP2013] [D].
- **HMX-6.6-02 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I, GRP)** Authority. Sub-requirements HMX-6.6-02.1 to HMX-6.6-02.4.
- **HMX-6.6-02.1 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** The estimate shall enter as an additive, rate-limited feed-forward term capped at a fraction of the force-channel command (10% [SPEC]). sEMG alone shall never command motion: a joint moves only with a matching force-channel signal (HMX-1.6-04, HMX-5.2-05).
- **HMX-6.6-02.2 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** Disagreement with the force channels shall zero the term.
- **HMX-6.6-02.3 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** The safety controller shall be able to remove it, and no tier 1 function shall depend on it.
- **HMX-6.6-02.4 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** Startle, tremor, co-contraction, and fatigue shall be tested with operators not trained on the system (HMX-6.5-03).
- **HMX-6.6-03 (A, T, OP-VNRA-I)** Stability and benefit gate. The term shall be included in the uncertainty set of HMX-6.1-09 [R]. The pack is released only if the adoption gate is met in a trial of 59 consecutive runs with 20 operators not trained on the system (the pattern of HMX-6.5-06): at least a 20% reduction in mean cuff effort or RMS tracking error against the default-off machine [SPEC], at no loss of the stability margins of HMX-6.1-09 and with onset detection robust to a 20 ms shift in electrode placement [B5-EJAP2013] [D]; it is dropped if not. Until the gate passes it is development-only (CAR-8). Its electronics are cuff-side and M1-only, so they need R1 qualification only (§7.1).


## 7.0 Nuclear Environment Requirements

### 7.1 Radiation Classes

Classes are bound to operating mode ([R] on [D] data). The numeric values are design targets for the machine; mapping a class to a named facility zone is made by the zone-to-class rule of HMX-14-02.

| Class | Intended area | Mode | Design TID over service life | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Low-dose accessible areas | M1 and M2 | 100 Gy | M1 life dose is bounded at about 0.2 to 5 Gy (HMX-7.2-10), so 100 Gy carries at least 20× margin even at the heroic bound and more than 100× at the realistic one [R] |
| R2 | Moderate-dose work zones | M2 only | 1 kGy | Fukushima PCV robot design budget of 100 Sv/h for 10 h, about 1 kGy [D] |
| R3 | High-dose zones | M2 only | 10 kGy | Radiation-tolerant design; qualification to 1.5× (15 kGy) per HMX-7.2-11 [?R] |

Commercial electronics are commonly reported to degrade in the range of tens to hundreds of Gy [?]. Data points [D]: a COTS single-board computer failed at 111 ± 5.5 Gy through its power-management circuitry [B4-WEST]; a COTS FPGA survived 6,680 Gy with remote power and sacrificial peripherals [B4-ESSEX]. So R1 is marginal for unselected COTS boards, and R2 and R3 imply radiation-tolerant design, selected parts, or standoff.

### 7.2 Requirements

- **HMX-7.2-01 (T)** Sensors, valves, seals, fibre, and electronics shall be gamma-irradiation qualified to the declared class.
- **HMX-7.2-02 (A)** Critical electronics shall sit in shielded, quickly replaceable modules; fibre radiation-induced attenuation shall be in the link budget.
- **HMX-7.2-03 (I)** Materials: minimize cobalt-bearing alloys (Co-60 activation) and other high-activation elements; use non-porous, crevice-free, cleanable surfaces; seals and elastomers shall tolerate radiation and decontamination chemicals.
- **HMX-7.2-04 (T)** Decontamination ([SPEC] on [D] limits). All exterior surfaces shall be covered by sacrificial, removable decontamination skins, and the machine shall be decontaminable with no trapped volumes to two levels, measured by 300 cm² wipe tests with the wipe result divided by the measured wipe efficiency (0.10 may be assumed) [B10-CFR173] [D]. *Level R* (operator-contact surfaces, the doffing interface, and the OIF interior): non-fixed contamination at most 0.4 Bq/cm² for beta and gamma emitters and low-toxicity alpha emitters and 0.04 Bq/cm² for other alpha emitters, the level below which a surface is not classed as contaminated [B10-CFR173] [D]. *Level T* (exterior, for release from the zone): at most 4 Bq/cm² and 0.4 Bq/cm² respectively, the transport limits [B10-CFR173] [D], with contact dose rate at most 5 µSv/h [B10-ICAO, B10-NRC] [D, secondary]. Method: removal of the skins, then wipe-down or wash; fixed contamination is never credited. The required decontamination factor is the zone surface level divided by the limit (Appendix B.8). A tighter site criterion governs (HMX-14-02).
- **HMX-7.2-05 (A, T)** Any external hydraulic leak shall be detected, isolated, and collected so it does not spread contamination.
- **HMX-7.2-06 (A)** Fluid and cable fire behaviour shall be assessed, with ignition-source control and fire-resistant fluid evaluated against the fluid of HMX-4.1-04.
- **HMX-7.2-07 (A)** Waste ([SPEC] on [D] scheme). The machine becomes radioactive waste and shall be designed to be dismantled to waste-package size. *Classes:* the IAEA GSG-1 classes (exempt, very short-lived, very low-level, low-level, intermediate, and high-level) [B10-GSG1] [D]; their numeric boundaries are set by the waste owner with the regulator [B10-NEA] [D, secondary] and so are a site acceptance criterion (HMX-14-02), which makes the class of each stream a [?R] forecast until the site route is known. *Streams, segregated at source:* decontamination skins and protective clothing; filters and fluid (organic: machine fluid about 5 kg and tether fluid about 22 kg, with the HPS charge of about 50 kg treated as potentially contaminated); tether hoses (about 150 kg); electronics modules and the Li-ion safety battery (hazardous mixed waste); the gas-charged accumulator (pre-charge released under procedure before cutting); and structure, which is decontaminated first (HMX-7.2-04) and classified by measurement. *Dismantling:* components at most 100 kg and 1.2 m in the longest dimension, joined by removable fasteners at defined cut planes. *Records:* each removed module carries a waste record (contact dose rate, wipe result, gamma spectrum, activation estimate, fluid exposure). *Minimization:* skins and filters are sized per sortie to what the decontamination step needs, and the per-sortie quantity shall be reported with the first CAD mass properties (HMX-13.2-09).
- **HMX-7.2-08 (A, D)** Operator dose shall follow ALARA ([D] limits, [SPEC] planning values). Limits: ICRP recommends 20 mSv per year averaged over five years with no single year above 50 mSv [B4-NRC-ICRP] [D]; the NRC limit is 50 mSv per year TEDE [B4-NRC-50] [D]; the site limit governs. Planning: at most 20 mSv per year per operator and at most 2 mSv planned per sortie, the plan value TEPCO used for robot-survey tasks, where workers received 0.52 to 0.74 mSv [B4-TEPCO] [D, SPEC]. Integrated dosimetry with a local dose-rate and accumulated-dose display shall give a hard stop at the sooner of the site limit or the sortie plan. Shielding versus mass is closed by HMX-3.1-06 and HMX-7.2-12.
- **HMX-7.2-09 (A, T)** The operator shall receive supplied air through the tether, with an independent escape air supply of at least 15 minutes if the air line is lost (HMX-7.2-13).
- **HMX-7.2-10 (A, T)** Mode binding and ambient ceiling ([R] on [D] limits, [?R] geometry). M1 shall be used only where the planned operator dose of HMX-7.2-08 is met, so the ambient dose rate at the operator is at most (annual constraint ÷ in-zone hours). At 20 mSv per year: 20 µSv/h for 1,000 h (250 sorties of 4 h), 0.1 mSv/h for 200 h, 0.5 mSv/h for 40 h (10 sorties, 2 mSv each), 1 mSv/h for 20 h, 5 mSv/h for 4 h (*derived*). Machine dose in M1 is operator dose divided by the cockpit transmission f: at f = 0.9 (the realistic value of HMX-3.1-06), 22 to 56 mGy per year for operator doses of 20 to 50 mSv, or 0.2 to 0.6 Gy over a 10-year life; at f = 0.1, which needs about 950 kg of lead (Cs-137 tenth-value layer, 4 m² surround [?R]), 2 to 5 Gy (*derived*, Appendix B). Reaching 1 kGy in M1 with the operator held to 500 mSv per decade needs about 11 half-value layers, about 77 mm of lead for Cs-137 (about 3.5 t) or 132 mm for Co-60 (about 6 t) [?R]. Therefore R2 and R3 apply to M2 only. Gy and Sv are treated as equal for photons [R].
- **HMX-7.2-11 (A, T, GRP)** Class qualification ([D] precedents, [?R] application). Sub-requirements HMX-7.2-11.1 to HMX-7.2-11.5.
- **HMX-7.2-11.1 (A, T)** Every electronic module shall be gamma-qualified to 1.5× its class TID (150 Gy, 1.5 kGy, 15 kGy), consistent with HMX-5.3-02.
- **HMX-7.2-11.2 (A, T)** Power-management parts and regulators shall be tested first, since they failed before the silicon in the data above [B4-WEST, B4-ESSEX] [D].
- **HMX-7.2-11.3 (A, T)** Qualification shall use decommissioning-relevant dose rates or justify the rate used, because one SRAM FPGA failed near 2.1 kGy at a high dose rate but worked at 2.5 kGy at a low rate (a result cited second-hand in [B4-ESSEX]) [D, secondary].
- **HMX-7.2-11.4 (A, T)** Modules shall be swapped at 50% of qualified TID [SPEC] by accumulated dose (§12).
- **HMX-7.2-11.5 (A, T)** Time at the Fukushima Unit 2 PCV reading of about 210 Sv/h consumes a 1 kGy budget in about 5 hours [B4-WNN] [D, R], so R2 and R3 sorties shall be dose-planned on the machine as well as on the operator.
- **HMX-7.2-12 (A)** Shielding limits ([R] on [D] half-value layers, [?R] geometry). Half-value and tenth-value layers: Cs-137 lead 0.7 and 2.1 cm, steel 1.6 and 5.3 cm; Co-60 lead 1.2 and 4.0 cm, steel 2.1 and 6.9 cm [B4-OSHA] [D]. For a 0.30 × 0.20 × 0.15 m module (0.27 m²), one half-value layer of lead is 21 kg (Cs-137) or 37 kg (Co-60), and a tenfold reduction is 64 or 122 kg (*derived*). Shielding shall therefore be limited to about one half-value layer on one module [SPEC]; any further reduction comes from module selection, replacement by dose, and standoff. The values are narrow-beam; broad-beam build-up makes real thickness higher [?R].
- **HMX-7.2-13 (A, T)** Escape air ([D] precedents, [R] sizing). Duration 15 minutes at 35 to 40 L/min, which is 525 to 600 L of free air, a 2 L cylinder at 300 bar (*derived*). Precedents: a 2 L, 300 bar composite escape set rated 15 minutes at 35 L/min under EN 402 [B5-SAFENOR] [D]; NIOSH-approved pressure-demand supplied-air respirators offering 5, 10, and 15 minute escape cylinders [B5-HONEYWELL] [D]; a complete 15 minute carbon-composite escape device weighing 4.5 kg [B5-SAFETYSUPPLY] [D]. The set shall be a second air interface on the operator side, independent of the tether supply, with automatic or one-motion changeover and the escape-air alarm of HMX-6.2-02 (tier 2). Mass 3 to 5 kg and about 0.5 × 0.2 × 0.1 m, inside the cockpit line of §3.1 [?R]. Fifteen minutes gives 2.5× margin on the 6 minute egress budget of HMX-9.1-05.

### 7.3 Neutron Environment and Conditional Neutron Layer

**Applicability:** HMX-7.3-01 (survey) is STD; HMX-7.3-02 (conditional layer) is option pack OP-NEUT, reserved and not orderable (HMX-1.8-02).

[R] on [D] data unless tagged. Every dose figure in §7.1 and HMX-7.2-10 is photon dose (Gy and Sv are treated as equal for photons). This specification has no neutron requirement because no neutron field has been defined for the target zones.

- **HMX-7.3-01 (A)** The facility survey (HMX-14-02) shall state whether neutron fields exist in the target zones, their spectrum, and the neutron share of dose. Neutron trigger [SPEC]: if the neutron share exceeds 10% of the total dose equivalent at the operator or electronics position, OP-NEUT becomes a heavy-variant decision (HMX-7.3-02); otherwise no neutron shielding is carried in the mass budget.
- **HMX-7.3-02 (A, OP-NEUT)** Conditional layer [R, ?R]. If the survey shows that the neutron trigger of HMX-7.3-01 is exceeded, the candidate is a hydrogenous layer loaded with boron (borated polymer or gel), not gadolinium and not heavy water. Boron capture emits a 478 keV gamma in about 94% of captures and hydrogen capture a 2.2 MeV gamma, while gadolinium capture emits gammas up to about 7.9 MeV, which are hard to shield [B5-USPTO-BPE, B5-PMC-NCEPT, B5-ISNIE] [D]. The layer shall be credited with no gamma shielding in any budget: 20 mm of water-like material gives a narrow-beam transmission of about 0.84 for Cs-137 and 0.88 for Co-60 (*derived*, Appendix B), and the operator-shielding conclusion of HMX-3.1-06 stands. Mass: 20 mm over the assumed 4 m² surround is 0.08 m³, about 80 to 96 kg at 1.0 to 1.2 g/cm³, which is 3 to 7× the 13 to 29 kg left in the covers line and more than the whole 60 kg margin. A full-surround layer is therefore not affordable, and any layer shall be local (20 mm over the 0.27 m² module of HMX-7.2-12 is about 5 to 6.5 kg) or a payload or scope trade under HMX-3.1-01. Radiolysis gas, containment, decontamination, and waste route (HMX-7.2-07) shall be assessed. Appendix D records why the D₂O ice/fluid shield and the gel gamma and self-sealing claims are not carried.


## 8.0 Operator Interface & Human Factors
