- **HMX-8.1-01 (D, T)** The OIF shall have adjustable cuffs, handles, and footplates with force sensing. Don and doff ([SPEC] on [D] usability data, [?R] shift arithmetic): *Rear-entry docking:* the baseline entry is rear-entry from a clean-side docking platform level with the footplates, so the entry step is at most 0.20 m (as the exit step of HMX-9.1-07) and the operator never touches the contaminated exterior; the platform is a site criterion (HMX-14-02). *Time:* don at most 15 min with two assistants and doff at most 10 min after decontamination; emergency egress is separate (MEER, HMX-9.1-07). An 8 h shift is then 4 h in zone, about 1.5 h of checks, don, transit, doff, and decontamination, and about 2.5 h of rest and briefing [?R]. Simple passive occupational exoskeletons took 6.5 to 25 min to assemble and don, with failure rates up to 49% on one device and two-person don and doff a recurring usability problem [B11-DON] [D, secondary], so the test is hard. *Acceptance:* 59 consecutive error-free don and doff cycles within the time budget by trained operators across the rated stature range (HMX-8.1-07), the zero-failure rule of HMX-13.2-05 (at most 5% failure at 95% confidence).
- **HMX-8.1-02 (T)** Displays shall show payload estimate, scaling ratio, centre-of-pressure margin, hydraulic pressure, tether tension, dose rate and accumulated dose, and alarms, and shall remain functional on battery power.
- **HMX-8.1-03 (A, D, GRP)** Operator comfort and visibility ([SPEC] on [D] limits, [R] arithmetic). Sub-requirements HMX-8.1-03.1 to HMX-8.1-03.3.
- **HMX-8.1-03.1 (A, D)** *Thermal load.* The operator is metabolic class 2 (moderate, above 235 W): 180 W scaled by body mass is 180 × 100 ÷ 70 = 257 W at the 100 kg dressed rated mass (HMX-8.1-07), and 244 W at 95 kg bare; 232 W, the class-1 case, applies only to an operator of 90 kg or less [B11-HEAT] [D]. The ISO 7243 reference value for acclimatized workers is 28 °C effective WBGT for class 2 (30 °C for class 1) [B11-HEAT] [D], so the cockpit WBGT plus the clothing adjustment value shall not exceed 28 °C: the CAV is +3 °C for double-layer woven clothing and +11 °C for limited-use vapor-barrier coveralls and is not valid for fully encapsulating suits [B11-HEAT] [D], which gives a cockpit limit of 25 °C and 17 °C respectively. For encapsulating or supplied-air ensembles, heart-rate and core-temperature monitoring govern, with the ACGIH upper core limit of 38.0 °C as the alarm [B11-HEAT] [D]. The machine-induced WBGT rise at the operator shall be at most 1.0 °C [SPEC], and every machine surface the operator can reach shall be at most 43 °C at the 70 °C return-fluid alarm condition (HMX-4.1-06): 43 °C is the sustained-contact burn threshold for bare metal, with about 51 °C at one minute [B11-TOUCH] [D, secondary], and ISO 13732-1 gives guidance but sets no limit value, so the 43 °C is a design choice. Hot lines within reach shall be insulated or shrouded (assumed up to 2 kg inside the cockpit line [?R]).
- **HMX-8.1-03.2 (A, D)** *Field of view.* The headgear with the OIF and guard shall achieve a visual field score of at least 90, the NIOSH criterion for CBRN full-facepiece respirators [B11-FOV] [D]; the human horizontal field is just under 180° [B11-FOV] [D, secondary]. The target is strict (the NIOSH test covers the facepiece alone), so frame members and guard posts shall stay out of the line of sight and the combined score shall be measured in the mockup (HMX-8.1-05); a shortfall is closed with cameras or mirrors.
- **HMX-8.1-03.3 (A, D)** *Centre of gravity.* The operator's CoM shall stay within ±30 mm fore-aft and laterally of nominal over the whole adjustment range, which shifts the combined CoM by at most 100 kg × 0.03 m ÷ 1,000 kg = 3 mm, inside the 20 mm CoM-offset limit of CAR-5 and the sensitivity sweep of Appendix F.7 (the operator CoM tolerance is separate from the operator's standing height, HMX-3.4-06). Range of motion, restraint, and axis alignment are set in HMX-3.4-03.
- **HMX-8.1-04 (T)** Emergency procedures shall be short, sequenced, and operable under stress, accounting for confinement, panic, and reduced cognitive capacity.
- **HMX-8.1-05 (D)** Joint layout, operator attachment points, and range of motion shall be validated on a passive instrumented frame and mockups before hardware commitment, as GE did to set joint count and human-factors data [GE-71 pp.3, 6]. The published joint-excursion figure [GE-71 p.2A] seeds the range-of-motion baseline (HMX-3.4-03). Analysis, then simulation, then a mockup with a human at the controls is the recommended sequence [NASA-68 p.50]; heritage Walking Truck work used a crane-suspended, unpowered simulator linked to the legs [NASA-68 pp.95-96].
- **HMX-8.1-06 (A, D)** Operator joint axes and the corresponding machine joint axes shall be approximately collinear over the operating range, or the misalignment shall be bounded and its effect on interface force and comfort assessed. Heritage superposed master and slave axes, and made them concentric below the elbow, to simplify controls and improve human-factor correspondence [GE-71 p.2] (acceptance: HMX-3.4-03).
- **HMX-8.1-07 (A, D)** Operator population ([SPEC] on [D] data, [R] arithmetic). The OIF shall accommodate operators of either sex with stature 1.54 to 1.84 m, from the 10th-percentile woman to the 90th-percentile man, the heritage convention [NASA-68 p.99], with adjustment that needs no major rework: a stature span of 0.30 m in the cuffs, footplates, handles, and link lengths. *Men:* the ANSUR II pilot gives stature 1,643, 1,755, and 1,872 mm and weight 63.9, 84.2, and 110.7 kg at the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentiles [B13-ANSUR] [D]; a normal fit gives σ of 69.6 mm and 14.2 kg, so the 90th-percentile man is 1,844 mm and 102 kg [R]. *Women:* no ANSUR II female percentiles were obtained (Appendix G, EXT-01); the pooled international percentiles of 151, 165, and 179 cm [B9-STATURE] [D, secondary] give σ of 8.5 cm and a 10th-percentile woman of 154 cm [R], and stay as a placeholder (the pooled male values, 163, 178, and 194 cm, are about 2 cm taller at the median than ANSUR II and are no longer used). The full ANSUR II survey has 7,435 men and 3,922 women [B13-ANSUR] [D]. *Operator mass:* the 100 kg operator allowance is a dressed mass (95 kg bare plus 5 kg of clothing [?R]), which covers about the 78th-percentile man; a heavier operator reduces the rated brief payload by one kilogram per kilogram (HMX-1.8-04), so the 90th-percentile man at about 107 kg dressed loses about 7 kg. *Guard builds:* OP-ODG builds are rated to stature 1.54 to 1.64 m (HMX-3.6-07) and unguarded builds to 1.54 to 1.84 m, both stated on the rating plate (HMX-1.8-04), at the baseline footplate height of HMX-3.4-06. *Operator CoM:* the operator CoM is 0.55 × stature + h_f [?R], about 1.00 m for the 1.54 m operator and 1.17 m for the 1.84 m operator (1.12 m at the median); the stability model uses 1.00 m. Moving it to 1.12 m changes the STD offset limits by less than 1% (0.381 against 0.384 m at 200 kg, Appendix B.11), so the model stands, and F.1 records the difference.
- **HMX-8.2-01 (I)** Training shall cover familiarization, force-scaling use, simulated hydraulic loss, tether snag, and egress, in a non-radioactive mock-up, with a defined qualification and recurrent currency.

## 9.0 Emergency & Rescue

- **HMX-9.1-01 (D, T)** The operator shall egress with no external power within the 6 minute budget of HMX-9.1-05 via the mechanical release of HMX-9.1-07, with the machine in its safe posture or, if the safe posture cannot be reached, in its last held posture (HMX-9.1-06).
- **HMX-9.1-02 (A, T)** The frame shall carry a crane-rated recovery hardpoint (HMX-9.1-10), and a rescue plan for an incapacitated operator shall be integrated with the site emergency plan. The hardpoint load path and the rescue release (HMX-9.1-08) shall not be blocked by the overhead guard when fitted (HMX-3.6-05).
- **HMX-9.1-03 (T)** Hardwired and fibre emergency stops shall exist at the operator and at the HPS. Wireless links shall not be relied on for safety functions.
- **HMX-9.1-04 (A)** The safe posture shall be holdable indefinitely with no power, by load-holding valves and brakes alone.
- **HMX-9.1-05 (A, D)** Egress time ([SPEC] times on [S] precedent, [R] dose). Total from event to clean side at most 6 minutes: up to 60 s to reach the safe posture (HMX-4.3-02), 60 s manual release with no power, 30 s to exit the frame, and a 3 min walk-out over a path of 100 m or less, which sums to 330 s and leaves 30 s of slack (*derived*). The 60 s release is a limit; the 10 s release target (HMX-9.1-07) would leave 80 s of slack (330 s becomes 280 s) (*derived*). The 90 s release-plus-exit figure mirrors the 90 s aircraft evacuation limit [B5-GOVINFO] [D], which is adjacent, not equivalent [S]. Escape air of 15 minutes gives 2.5× margin (HMX-7.2-13). Egress dose is 0.05 mSv at 0.5 mSv/h and 0.5 mSv at 5 mSv/h, the M1 ambient ceiling of HMX-7.2-10, a quarter of the 2 mSv sortie plan (*derived*). The 100 m path limit is a constraint on work-zone siting; with the 50 m tether it holds if the walking path is no more than twice the straight-line distance [R]. Demonstration with trained and untrained operators in the cold mock-up (§13, HMX-13.2-05).
- **HMX-9.1-06 (A, T)** Release from a held posture ([SPEC]). If the safe posture cannot be reached (hydraulic loss before or during the sequence, or a failed accumulator isolation), the manual release shall still free the operator with the machine held in whatever posture it stopped, payload possibly raised, with no load transferred to the operator, within the 60 s release budget. The safe-posture sequence is not a precondition of egress.
- **HMX-9.1-07 (A, T, D)** Mechanical Emergency Egress Release, MEER ([SPEC] on [S] precedent). One deliberate action (lift the guard cover, then pull) frees every operator restraint (cuffs, footplate clamps, torso restraint) with no external power and no stored energy beyond springs or gas struts. Limit 60 s (HMX-9.1-05); target 10 s [SPEC]; operable one-handed in a contaminated glove. Cover lift at most 25 N and pull at most 89 N (20 lbf) over at most 150 mm, from the 20 lbf low-force release limit of FMVSS 217 for emergency exits [B7-NHTSA-217] [S]; about 10 spring-loaded latches at 8 N each need 80 N of cable tension, met by a 1.5:1 handle lever at 53 N [?R]. The 10 s target is allocated as 2 s to reach and lift the cover, 1 s to pull, 2 s for the clamps and the air changeover, and 5 s to step out. The operator steps out; there is no free-fall exit, and the exit step shall be at most 0.20 m [SPEC] with a hand rail. An NTSB investigation found that minimally trained passengers could not free themselves from a harness with locking carabiners and a poor cutting tool [B7-NTSB] [D], so the release shall need no tool, no carabiner, and no training beyond the demonstration of HMX-13.2-05. The same action shall also break the supplied-air line (HMX-7.2-13) and hand the operator to the escape air set, within the same time, because a restraint release that leaves the operator tied to the air line does not complete egress: a breakaway coupling on the pull cable and a pressure-demand escape regulator with automatic changeover, per HMX-7.2-13. After release the machine holds its posture (HMX-9.1-06, HMX-5.2-03) and remains in the zone as a radioactive chassis; the operator still walks to the clean side (HMX-9.1-05). Pyrotechnic and electrical release derived from ejection-seat technology is not adopted: pyrotechnic initiators put an ignition and fragment source in a fluid-fire and contamination zone (HMX-7.2-06, HMX-7.2-04), need explosives approval and radiation qualification of the energetic parts (HMX-7.2-01), and an electrical firing path adds a tier 1 function with a worst-case inadvertent-firing consequence (HMX-6.2-02); the problem they address, the 60 s release, is met by the mechanical target above [R].
- **HMX-9.1-08 (A, T)** Rescue release ([SPEC]). A second mechanical release, operable by a rescue crew from outside the frame, shall free an incapacitated operator without entering the frame (rear-entry concept, HMX-8.1-01), and shall not be blocked by the guard when fitted (HMX-3.6-05). The rescue release is a handle at the rear of the pelvis, at most 133 N (30 lbf) two-handed, reachable by the rescue crew from outside the frame within 2 s of arrival, between the 89 N and 267 N low- and high-force limits of FMVSS 217 [B7-NHTSA-217] [S].
- **HMX-9.1-09 (A, T, GRP)** Inadvertent release ([SPEC]). Sub-requirements HMX-9.1-09.1 to HMX-9.1-09.3.
- **HMX-9.1-09.1 (A, T)** The release handle shall be guarded against accidental pull (cover plus two-motion action).
- **HMX-9.1-09.2 (A, T)** Release shall register as loss of operator contact, driving assist to zero, brakes applied, and hold (§6.3), and shall alarm; the release function itself shall not depend on the controller or on power.
- **HMX-9.1-09.3 (A, T)** An inadvertent release shall leave the machine held and the operator uninjured, and shall be shown by the test of HMX-13.2-05; the cover plus two-motion action and a detent at the pull force make accidental release a deliberate two-step act.
- **HMX-9.1-10 (A, T)** Recovery hardpoint and rescue timeline ([R] on [S] practice). *Rating:* an occupied machine is lifted like a personnel platform, for which practice requires a structural design factor of 5:1 and 125% proof testing [B7-B30.23] [S]. With the gross rating of 1,000 kg (9.81 kN) and ψ = 1.15 the ultimate rating is 5 × 1.15 × 9.81 = 56.4 kN and the proof load 1.25 × 9.81 = 12.3 kN. Two shoulder lugs shall each carry the full 56.4 kN (a single-lug failure shall not drop the machine); a Ø20 mm Ti-6Al-4V pin in double shear carries about 166 kN, so the lug plate and its load path to the frame govern. The lugs shall lie within ±0.05 m of the loaded CoM line (a 3.8° tilt at the 0.75 m lug height above the CoM), shall pass through the overhead guard without carrying its load (HMX-3.6-05), and shall be reachable with the machine on its side. *Rescue timeline* [SPEC]: the primary path is release and extraction, not a lift: event to rescue-crew arrival 5 min, rescue release and extraction 2 min (HMX-9.1-08), transfer to the clean side 3 min; 10 min in all against the 15 min of escape air (HMX-7.2-13), a margin of 5 min (33%). Crane recovery of the machine follows extraction. The lugs add about 2 kg inside the structure line [?R].

## 10.0 Safety & Reliability Engineering

- **HMX-10.1-01 (A)** The program shall include hazard identification, FMEA, fault trees, reliability block diagrams, and a probabilistic risk assessment of operator-injury events.
- **HMX-10.1-02 (A)** Common-cause analysis shall cover the shared HPS, tether, safety controller, and radiation environment, including simultaneous HPS and communications loss.
- **HMX-10.1-03 (I)** An independent reviewer who did not take part in the design shall review the analyses, with open actions tracked to closure.
- **HMX-10.1-04 (A)** Safety-claim register ([SPEC]). Each claim in the table below shall be closed by an analysis that lists every candidate single point and shared cause for it and records the result; the register is kept with the compliance matrix (Appendix I). None has been analysed yet.

| Claim | Where | Candidate single points and shared causes to analyse | Closing analysis | State |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| No single failure both causes uncommanded motion and removes the means to reach a safe state | HMX-1.6-02 | Safety controller and main controller; accumulator isolation valve (HMX-4.3-05); 48 V safety battery; tether power loss; servo-valve null leakage; shared cause: radiation event, simultaneous HPS and communications loss | FMEA and fault tree (HMX-10.1-01, 10.1-02) | Not performed |
| Every loaded joint holds on loss of power, signal, or pressure | HMX-1.6-04, HMX-5.2-03 | Load-holding valve and brake spring; pilot-line leakage; shared cause: fluid contamination on both holding paths | FMEA with fluid-cleanliness common-cause case | Not performed |
| Tier 1 functions meet the 1.6×10⁻⁸ per hour allocation | HMX-6.2-02 | Shared power, clock, sensors, and software between the six functions | Architecture analysis with diagnostic coverage | Not performed |
| The MEER frees the operator with no power in 60 s | HMX-9.1-07 | Cable, latch group, and cover; operator glove and clothing | Demonstration (HMX-13.2-05) and FMEA | Demonstration defined, FMEA not performed |

| Hazard | Primary treatment | Verification (stage or test) |
|---|---|---|
| Uncommanded or unstable motion | §6.1, §6.2, §6.3 | Fault injection at the limb and integrated gates (HMX-13.2-11); leg-mode trials (HMX-6.5-06) |
| Loss of supply with payload raised | HMX-5.2-03, §6.3 | 299 safe-posture sequences (HMX-13.2-11) |
| Hose burst and spray | HMX-4.2-02, §7.2 | Component hose-burst test (§13 component stage); isolation test for OP-VNRA-H (HMX-13.2-12) |
| Fall or loss of stability | §3.2, §6.3 | 5° stability test under gantry or tether (HMX-3.2-09); knee and single-support tests (HMX-13.2-07) |
| Payload drop or dynamic overload | HMX-3.2-05, HMX-3.2-10 | FEA, modal and stiffness tests (HMX-13.2-08); proof load of HMX-3.2-05 |
| Operator crush, pinch, over-torque | HMX-5.2-04 | Cuff-force and stop tests on the hardware limb (HMX-13.2-11 limb gate) |
| Tether snag or over-tension | §4.2, §6.3 | Fuse and coupling tests (§13 component stage) |
| Radiation-induced controller fault | §6.2, §7.2 | Hardware-in-the-loop fault injection (HMX-6.4-02); irradiation to HMX-7.2-11 |
| Contamination spread | §7.2 | Decontamination trial (§13 subsystem stage) |
| Loss of supplied air | HMX-7.2-09, HMX-7.2-13 | MEER and escape-air demonstration (HMX-13.2-05) |
| Fire | HMX-7.2-06 | Not yet defined (fire-case analysis) |
| Operator trapped in frame | §9.1 (HMX-9.1-07 to 9.1-09) | MEER demonstration (HMX-13.2-05) |
| Structural fatigue or radiation degradation unseen | HMX-3.1-07, §3.5 (monitor only) | Structure verification (HMX-13.2-08); fibre qualification (HMX-3.5-04) |
| Onboard hydraulic line failure with load raised | §4.4, HMX-5.2-03, §6.3 | Transient test (HMX-13.2-12) |
| Falling debris or collapse onto frame | §3.6, HMX-3.2-11, §11.3 | Guard drop and crush tests (HMX-13.2-04) |
| Tool jam, uncommanded tool action, tool-borne fire | HMX-5.4-05 to 5.4-09, HMX-6.2-03 | Tool tests (HMX-13.2-06) |
| Fluid over-temperature | HMX-4.1-05, HMX-4.1-06, §6.3 | Thermal soak (HMX-13.2-06) |


## 11.0 Concept of Operations

### 11.1 Representative Tasks and Mission Profile

Representative tasks: lifting shield blocks and waste containers, removing piping and ducts, supporting heavy cutting tools, and positioning tooling.

| Phase | Content |
|---|---|
| 1. Clean-side preparation | Checks, fluid and cleanliness verification, brake and safe-posture test, donning |
| 2. Transit | Walk to work zone under tether handler |
| 3. Work | Handling and tool support within dose and time limits |
| 4. Exit | Return, doffing through the clean-side interface |
| 5. Post-sortie | Decontamination, skin replacement, inspection, data download |

### 11.2 Go / No-Go and Abort

- **HMX-11.2-01 (D)** A Go decision shall require: fluid within cleanliness and temperature limits, brakes and load-holding valves tested, dual-channel sensors agreeing, dosimetry active, safe-posture test passed, all alarms clear, and a passed MEER function check (cover and linkage free, no obstruction).
- **HMX-11.2-02 (D)** Abort shall be required on: operator dose limit, any warning alarm, tether fault, loss of a redundant channel, or fire indication.
- **HMX-11.2-03 (D)** When a tool is fitted (OP-UHTH), a Go decision shall also require a passed tool-lock hold test, a passed enable-and-stop test, and a recorded tool mass. When the overhead guard is fitted (OP-ODG), a Go decision shall also require an inspection with no impact deformation.

### 11.3 Non-Nominal Scenarios

| Scenario | Automatic response | Operator and site response |
|---|---|---|
| Loss of hydraulic supply | Hold; ride-through to safe posture | Egress or remote recovery |
| Hose burst | Isolation; hold | Abort; decontaminate spill |
| Loss of stability margin | Freeze; stabilizers if fitted; kneel | Hold; recover |
| Air-line loss | Alarm; escape air (15 min) | Exit immediately; egress budget 6 min (HMX-9.1-05) |
| Operator incapacitation | Assist to zero; brakes; hold | Remote or crew rescue |
| Fire | Isolate fluid; alarm | Abort; site fire response |
| Falling debris or structural movement | Freeze; hold; no stored-energy manoeuvre (HMX-3.2-11); guard protects within its rating if fitted | Release (HMX-9.1-07); egress; crane recovery |
| Tool jam or uncommanded tool action | Tool de-energized; lock holds | Abort; release tool (HMX-5.4-05) |
| Return fluid over-temperature | Alarm at 70 °C; abort to safe posture at 80 °C | Cool-down; fluid sample |

## 12.0 Maintenance, Inspection & Lifecycle

| Item | Interval | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Fluid cleanliness, sampling, filters | Every sortie | §4.1 |
| Brakes and load-holding valves | Function test every sortie; wear inspection quarterly [?R] | HMX-5.2-03 |
| Accumulator pre-charge | Every sortie; bladder and gas valve inspected quarterly, bladder replaced at 3 years [?R]; vessel inspection per the site regime | §4.3 |
| Servo-valve null and response | Every sortie; full response check annually; replaced at 50% of qualified TID | §5.3, HMX-7.2-11 |
| Decontamination skins | Replace every sortie | HMX-7.2-04 |
| Sensor calibration, dual-channel agreement | Agreement continuous; calibration quarterly [?R] | HMX-5.2-05 |
| Joint seals, diaphragms | Inspected quarterly; replaced at 2 years or on leak detection [?R] | HMX-5.2-02 |
| Onboard hoses | Visual every sortie; replace every 2 years | HMX-12-03 |
| Tether and HPS hoses | Visual every sortie; replace at 6 years at most including 2 years of storage, earlier on condition or dose | HMX-12-03 |
| Elastomers and electronics | Replaced at 50% of qualified TID; calendar life per vendor [?R] | HMX-7.2-11 |
| Structure: frequent inspection | Every sortie, visual (severe service) | HMX-12-03 |
| Structure: NDT of notched fatigue details | Quarterly | HMX-12-03 |
| Structure: teardown and proof test | Annually, at 1.5 × rated load | HMX-3.2-05, HMX-12-03 |
| Structure: fatigue life | Cycle counters; rework at 80% of design cycles | HMX-12-03 |
| Safety battery capacity and management electronics | Capacity check every sortie; replace below 80% of nominal capacity or at 50% of qualified TID | HMX-4.3-08 |
| MEER release function and rescue release | Function check every sortie; overhaul annually; replacement at 50% of qualified TID | HMX-9.1-07, 9.1-08 |
| Return-temperature and manifold sensors | Calibration quarterly [?R] | HMX-4.1-06 |
| UHTH rails, tool lock, tool-port seals (OP-UHTH) | Every sortie; wear and fatigue inspected quarterly [?R] | §5.4 |
| SHM fibre and interrogator (OP-VNRA-F) | Every sortie, baseline comparison | §3.5 |
| Overhead debris guard (OP-ODG) | Every sortie; after any impact; retire after a design-level impact | §3.6 |

- **HMX-12-01 (I)** A maintenance manual shall define inspection, replacement, and retirement criteria, with records kept alongside telemetry.
- **HMX-12-02 (A)** Retirement and dismantling as radioactive waste shall be planned from the start (HMX-7.2-07).
- **HMX-12-03 (A, I)** Initial maintenance intervals ([SPEC], with the [?R] items marked in the table; each shall be tightened by the FMEA (HMX-10.1-01) and the tests of §13). *Structure:* below-the-hook practice classes service as normal (monthly frequent inspection, annual periodic), heavy (weekly to monthly, semi-annual to quarterly), or severe (daily to weekly) [B11-B3020] [D, secondary]; at 250 sorties a year this machine is severe service, so frequent inspection is every sortie and documented NDT of the notched fatigue details of HMX-3.1-07 is quarterly, with an annual teardown and proof test at 1.5 × rated load (practice allows 125 to 150% [B11-B3020]; HMX-3.2-05 sets 1.5 ×). Cycle counters on stance steps and payload lifts shall retire or rework a member at 80% of its design cycles: 4.5 million of the 5.6 million (SC4) for leg structure and 100,000 to 200,000 of 125,000 to 250,000 (SC2) for the payload path. *Hoses:* German DIN 20066 and DGUV practice is replacement at 6 years including at most 2 years of storage, and at 2 years under increased demands [B11-HOSE] [D, secondary]; onboard hoses (pressure impulses, flexing, decontamination chemicals, radiation) are increased-demand, so 2 years, while tether and HPS hoses are 6 years at most and earlier on condition or dose. Age limits matter because internal tube cracking can be invisible externally: 2 of 6 critical hoses sectioned at an age limit were cracked [B11-HOSE] [D, secondary]. *Battery:* replacement below 80% of nominal capacity, the 20% derate of HMX-4.3-08. *By dose:* servo valves, elastomers, electronics, and the MEER release at 50% of qualified TID (HMX-7.2-11).

## 13.0 Verification & Validation

- **HMX-13.1-01 (I)** Every requirement shall trace to a verification method, acceptance criterion, and test or analysis record in a compliance matrix under configuration control.
- **HMX-13.1-02 (I)** The compliance matrix of Appendix I shall be regenerated from the records at each release. A record whose method is tagged but whose test, analysis, or procedure is not defined is open, and no requirement is verified until its acceptance record exists.
- **HMX-13.2-01 (I)** Development shall proceed arm system first, then one leg with the other locked, then the floating leg, then both legs with gantry support, closing each mode before the next. This is the sequence heritage testing followed [GE-71 pp.10, 15-18].

| Stage | Content | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|
| Component | Servo-valve response; joint friction and endurance; brake and load-holding tests; hose burst; gamma irradiation of valves, seals, sensors, electronics, fibre; release force, time, and inadvertent-release tests; return-temperature soak; and, for fitted packs, tool-lock and tool-port tests and the guard drop test | Margins and limits in §4 to §7 met |
| Subsystem | Hydraulic ride-through; controller hardware-in-the-loop with fault injection; decontamination trial | Requirements closed; 59 trials per safe-state demand and every tier 1 and 2 fault injected once (HMX-13.2-11) |
| Limb and leg staging | Arm system first; one leg with the other locked; floating leg; both legs, all under gantry or tether support, with fixtures per HMX-13.2-10 | Each mode closed with documented results before the next |
| Integrated dry | Inert payloads in a laboratory: stability, passivity validation, safe states, egress | All safe states demonstrated; 299 safe-posture sequences (HMX-13.2-11) |
| Cold mock-up | Non-radioactive decommissioning mock-up with operator training | Procedures and rescue demonstrated; MEER, don and doff, and E-stop counts per HMX-13.2-11 |
| Hot validation | Low-activity then production zones, with regulator approval | Each step closed before the next, after 59 abort-free sorties (HMX-13.2-11) |

- **HMX-13.2-02 (D)** Development shall provide a foot clamp to the floor, a hydraulic lock for any single leg in a folded posture, gantry or tether support, and operation of the slave remotely (extension handles or mode M2), so first multi-joint trials need no operator in the frame. Heritage used all of these [GE-71 pp.14-15] (fixtures sized in HMX-13.2-10).
- **HMX-13.2-03 (I)** Any uncommanded or unexplained motion shall halt testing in that mode until its cause is identified and the fix verified. A workaround that avoids a mode shall not be recorded as closing that mode [GE-71 pp.10, 17].
- **HMX-13.2-04 (T, OP-ODG)** Guard test. A drop test in the manner of ISO 3449 shall be run on a representative canopy and frame bed at Level I, with no entry into the DLV and no penetration, and a static crush test shall be run to the claim of HMX-3.6-03 with the mechanical load path (not hydraulic pressure) carrying the load. The leg-stop test of HMX-3.6-07 (75 kN with straight knees) shall be included.
- **HMX-13.2-05 (D, T)** MEER demonstration. Release by trained and untrained operators in protective clothing and under stress in the cold mock-up, with a payload held and the machine in a posture from which the safe posture cannot be reached, measured against the 10 s target and 60 s limit; inadvertent-release test; rescue-crew release. Stage-gate trial counts follow the zero-failure rule (n = ln 0.05 ÷ ln (1 − p)): 59 consecutive successful releases show a failure probability of at most 5% at 95% confidence, and 299 show at most 1%; the first gate uses 20 untrained subjects × 3 releases, the production gate 299.
- **HMX-13.2-06 (T)** Thermal and tool tests. A return-temperature soak at the 5.2 kW bound with the HPS cooler of HMX-4.1-05 shall be run; and, with OP-UHTH, tool-lock hold and enable-and-stop tests shall be run and the vibration of each tool class shall be injected into the force loop on the hardware limb, with the stability margins of HMX-6.1-09 re-shown with the tool fitted.
- **HMX-13.2-07 (T)** Single-support and joint tests. On the hardware limb at the GVWR the following shall be shown: the single-support knee-lever limiter inhibits step initiation and holds above 77 mm; the ankle delivers 1.83 kN·m usable at the CoP margin limit; the STD arm, shoulder, and elbow deliver the 1.0 m basis of HMX-3.2-13(c); the CoM adjustment of HMX-1.8-06 brings the unloaded CoM offset within 10 mm; and the tall kneel and the seat of HMX-3.4-03 are reached.
- **HMX-13.2-08 (T)** Structure verification. FEA of the leg, torso, and arm load paths at ψ × Nd = 3.45 with notched-fatigue details; a modal test of the hardware limb showing the lowest unloaded mode at least 10 Hz and the loaded-mode frequencies against the crossover schedule of HMX-3.1-09; and a stiffness test showing leg-link rotation at most 8.5 mrad per rated moment.
- **HMX-13.2-09 (T)** Weighing and CoM measurement ([SPEC] on [D] practice). Each unit and each pack shall be weighed on a scale accurate to 0.1% and its CoM measured to 5 mm before a rating is issued; the rating is re-issued from the measured dry mass plus 1% (10 kg at 1,000 kg) and with 10 mm added to the measured fore-aft CoM offset (15% of the 65 mm CoP margin), and the CoM adjustment of HMX-1.8-06 is applied at this step. Until then the parametric model of Appendix B governs: a bottom-up current best estimate plus a growth allowance that depletes with maturity, as NASA and JPL practice does (an example schedule is more than 30% at concept, 20% at preliminary design, 15% at critical design, 10% before environmental test, and 2 to 5% at shipment [B8-THEMIS] [D, secondary]; spacecraft dry mass has grown 25 to 30% through the later phases and 20 to 48% from Phase B start [B8-THEMIS] [D, secondary]), and the model is replaced by CAD mass properties at preliminary design.
- **HMX-13.2-10 (T, I)** Development fixtures ([R] on [?R] inputs). *Gantry:* hardpoint load rating 56.4 kN ultimate (HMX-9.1-10); the catch is an energy absorber limiting the arrest force to 3 × the 9.81 kN gross, 29.4 kN, with the cable slack set so the machine can tilt 6° before it takes load, a drop of at most 0.05 m, and an absorber stroke of at least W h ÷ (F − W) = 9.81 kN × 0.05 m ÷ 19.6 kN = 25 mm; no dynamic stability test outside the gantry or tether (HMX-3.2-06). *Foot clamp:* 25 kN uplift and 10 kN shear per foot (toe moment 9.81 kN × 0.20 m = 1.96 kN·m over a 0.30 m bolt spacing is 6.5 kN, × 3.45 = 22.6 kN). *Leg lock:* pins holding the hip flexion, knee, and ankle at 5.3 kN·m each (the single-support design torques × 3.45: 5.08 kN·m at the knee and hip, 5.24 kN·m at the ankle). *Remote handles:* master handles for unoccupied tests rated 150 N, the palm limit of HMX-5.2-04, which at 20:1 gives 98 N for the 200 kg payload (HMX-5.4-04).
- **HMX-13.2-11 (T, A)** Gate counts ([SPEC] on [R] statistics). The success-run rule n = ln (1 − C) ÷ ln R gives the consecutive successes needed to show reliability R at confidence C: at C = 95%, R = 0.95 needs 59 and R = 0.99 needs 299. *Limb and subsystem gates:* 59 trials of each safe-state demand of §6.3 (R ≥ 0.95), and each failure of an FMEA tier 1 or tier 2 function injected once with a 100% safe-state response. *Integrated dry gate:* 299 safe-posture sequences with inert payload (R ≥ 0.99), about 5 h of sequence time at 60 s each. *Cold mock-up gate:* MEER 59 then 299 (HMX-13.2-05), don and doff 59 (HMX-8.1-01), and the E-stop chain 299. *Hot validation:* 59 abort-free sorties at each activity step, which shows an unplanned-abort rate of at most 5% per sortie at 95% confidence. A failure restarts the count for that function after the cause is fixed (HMX-13.2-03). *Tier 1 limit:* the tier 1 target of 1.6×10⁻⁸ per hour cannot be shown by test, since zero-failure demonstration at 95% needs t = −ln 0.05 ÷ λ = 3.0 ÷ 1.6×10⁻⁸ = 1.9×10⁸ hours; those functions are closed by analysis (FMEA, diagnostic coverage, HMX-6.2-02) and the tests confirm function and fault response.
- **HMX-13.2-12 (T)** Pack release tests ([SPEC]). *OP-VNRA-H:* a hydraulic transient test on the hardware limb of a simulated Ø8 mm feeder break showing isolation within 30 ms and a loss of at most 0.19 L, and a spurious-isolation test showing that the second path carries the joint (HMX-4.4-01, HMX-4.4-02). *OP-VNRA-F:* the grating and fibre irradiation of HMX-3.5-04 to the class qualification dose, with the ±25 pm drift limit. *OP-VNRA-I:* the adoption trial of HMX-6.6-03. *OP-UHTH and OP-ODG:* the tests of HMX-13.2-04 and HMX-13.2-06. A pack that fails its test stays development-only (CAR-8).

No stage is claimed complete. This SSD is pre-hardware.

## 14.0 Environmental

- **HMX-14-01 (A)** The environmental and disposal profile of the hydraulic fluid, decontamination chemicals, and secondary waste shall be assessed against the target site's waste acceptance criteria (HMX-7.2-07, HMX-14-02).
- **HMX-14-02 (A, I)** Site acceptance criteria ([SPEC] on [D] code values and [R] arithmetic). A unit shall be released to a site only when a facility survey records that every criterion below is met; the record is the first item of the Go check (HMX-11.2-01). (1) *Floor:* certified for at least 82 kPa dynamic over a 0.15 m² foot (1.25 × 1,000 kg × 9.81 ÷ 0.15 m²), or for at least 23 kPa over 0.60 m² with OP-PAD, and for at least 66 kPa dynamic over 0.068 m² under OP-STAB feet or covered by a spreader mat (HMX-3.2-01, HMX-3.2-03, CAR-9). (2) *Slope:* at most 3° over the work cell and the route. (3) *Environment:* 5 to 40 °C, at most 95% RH non-condensing, wash-down to IP66, sealed concrete floor, with dust and debris conditions stated in the survey. (4) *Route:* openings and corridors per HMX-3.3-01 and a 50 m tether route with a bend radius of at least 750 mm. (5) *Dose and class:* the M1 ambient ceiling of HMX-7.2-10, and for M2 a zone-to-class rule: a zone is assigned the lowest class whose design TID (§7.1) is at least the machine dose planned for the campaign, the zone dose rate times the planned in-zone hours; at 1 Gy/h the classes R1, R2, and R3 allow 100, 1,000, and 10,000 h; the survey also records the neutron spectrum and dose share (HMX-7.3-01). (6) *Waste and decontamination:* the site's waste acceptance criteria and numeric class boundaries (HMX-7.2-07) and a decontamination facility able to meet the levels of HMX-7.2-04. (7) *Governing basis:* the jurisdiction, regulator, and standards editions in force (§1.5), recorded in the compliance matrix (Appendix G, EXT-28). (8) *Docking:* a clean-side docking platform at footplate height with room for two assistants (HMX-8.1-01). *Dynamic factor:* the floor check uses 1.25 on the gross, derived as 1 + 0.15 × 200 ÷ 1,000 (the hoist factor of HMX-3.2-10 on the payload) + 0.5 × 200 ÷ 1,000 (the 1.5× accident case on the payload) + 0.10 (a walking allowance [?R]) = 1.23, rounded up. The generic ASCE 7 allowance of 50% for power-driven units is not applied: the code's floor live loads already include an allowance for ordinary impact and its machinery percentages may be set by the manufacturer [B10-ASCE] [D, secondary]. Sensitivity at 1.5 (Appendix B.8): single-foot reaction 14.7 kN against the 13.35 kN basis, so a site that requires the 50% factor needs a gross rating of about 907 kg and a new run of the checker; bare-foot pressure 98 kPa, stabilizer feet 79 kPa, pads of 0.64 m² (0.60 m² is adequate only to 938 kg gross), and TRK 24.6 kPa against the 23 kPa basis.

**Evidence status.** A decided value is not a verified one. At this baseline: of the 25 source checks in Appendix C.1, 4 are verified, 4 are partly verified, and 17 are not verified; 30 external evidence requests are open (Appendix G); of the 136 records tagged T, 31 are named in a §13 test or stage definition (Appendix I); the safety-claim register of HMX-10.1-04 holds 4 claims, none analysed; and Appendix F has not been regenerated since the earlier baseline (F.0). The evidence still required before design freeze is listed in Appendix E.

## Appendix A. Carry-Over Map from KINETIC-ADS SSD v1.0

| KINETIC-ADS item | Disposition | HARDIMAN-X |
|---|---|---|
| §3.2 force-feedback impedance control, dual-channel sensing | Carried; extended to bilateral mode | §5.2, §6.1 |
| §3.2 rolling diaphragms (pressure matching) | Repurposed as contamination barrier | HMX-5.2-02 |
| §3.2-01 friction below 0.05 at 60 bar | Replaced by breakaway-torque limit | HMX-5.2-01 |
| §3.3 flapper-nozzle servo valves | Carried; bandwidth and radiation qualification added | §5.3 |
| §3.3 accumulators, 2 × 0.5 L | Carried; resized | §4.3 |
| §3.1 3,000 psi network, EAL, 4.5 L/min | Pressure carried; flow raised to 30 L/min; fluid replaced | §4.1 |
| §3.1-02 fluid conditioning | Carried; cleanliness added | §4.1 |
| §4.1-01 insulation monitoring | Carried to wash-down electrical paths | HMX-4.3-04 |
| §8.2 safety controller, §8.3 safe states | Carried and rewritten | §6.2, §6.3 |

| K-FESS, vacuum housing, magnetic bearings, inductive dock, surge bank, emergency battery, pressure hull, ascent systems | Not carried | None |

The flywheel was not carried because a 65,000 RPM rotor on a walking machine adds gyroscopic torque during turns and a burst hazard (23.4 MJ stored in KINETIC-ADS), while a tether removes the need for onboard storage. A composite flywheel proposed as a Kinetic Gyro-Buffer was reviewed and rejected on the same grounds, and the joint micro-surge accumulators are a different device rejected on their own grounds (HMX-3.2-11, Appendix D).

## Appendix B. Derived Values

Calculations behind the values marked *derived*. Inputs marked [?R] are assumptions, not data.

**B.1 Power, hydraulics, and hoses**
- Hydraulic power P = p·Q: 20.68 MPa × 5.0×10⁻⁴ m³/s (30 L/min) = 10.3 kW; × 2.5×10⁻⁴ m³/s (15 L/min) = 5.2 kW; × 7.5×10⁻⁵ m³/s (4.5 L/min, KINETIC-ADS) = 1.55 kW. Electrical input 10.3 kW ÷ 0.80 = 12.9 kW. Peak ratio 10.3 ÷ 1.55 = 6.6.
- Handle force: 200 kg × 9.81 ÷ 20 = 98 N. Fibre delay: 50 m × 5 ns/m ≈ 0.25 µs one way (refractive index ≈ 1.5).
- Fluid: kinematic viscosity by ASTM D341 Walther fit to 40 and 100 °C (checked at 0 and 60 °C): 310 mm²/s at 0 °C, 111 at 20, 73 at 30, 50 at 40, 36 at 50, 26.5 at 60. Density ρ = 920 × (1 − 0.0007 × (T − 15)) kg/m³ (temperature coefficient assumed [?R]). Laminar loss ΔP = 32 μ L v / D² per hose, μ = ν ρ, 50 m, 30 L/min, hoses 15.9 and 19.05 mm: total 68.3, 24.1, 15.6, 10.7, 7.6, 5.6 bar at 0, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60 °C. Flow cap at 5% of 207 bar (10.3 bar), loss scaling with Q: 4.5, 12.9, 19.9 L/min at 0, 20, 30 °C. Reynolds number on the supply hose: v = 5.0×10⁻⁴ ÷ 1.986×10⁻⁴ = 2.52 m/s; Re = 1,511 at 60 °C and 1,112 at 50 °C; return hose 940 at 50 °C. Tether oil 24.2 L × 0.92 = 22.3 kg (9.9 L in the supply hose, 14.3 L in the return).
- Hose comparison (HMX-4.2-03): 12.7 mm bore at 32 cSt, 870 kg/m³ (μ = 0.0278 Pa·s): v = 3.95 m/s, Re = 1,570; ΔP = 32 μ L v / D² = 1.09 MPa per hose, 2.18 MPa for two = 10.5% of 20.7 MPa, loss 1.09 kW. At fixed Q, ΔP scales as D⁻⁴.
- Double flow (HMX-4.1-07): laminar loss is linear in Q, so 2 × 10.7 = 21.4 bar at 40 °C (10.3%) and 2 × 5.6 = 11.2 bar at 60 °C (5.4%). At 60 L/min, 15.9 mm, 60 °C: v = 5.04 m/s, Re = 5.04 × 0.0159 ÷ 26.5×10⁻⁶ = 3,020.
- Pressure comparison: 3,000 ÷ 5,000 = 0.6, so displacement −40% and flow for equal power 30 × 0.6 = 18 L/min; laminar hose loss ∝ Q², 0.6² = 0.36, so −64%.
- Thermal: ρ at 50 °C = 897 kg/m³; ṁ = 15 L/min × 897 = 0.224 kg/s; ΔT = 5,200 ÷ (0.224 × cp) for cp 2.0, 2.06, 2.24 kJ/kg·K gives 11.6, 11.3, 10.4 K. Tether loss at 15 L/min and 40 °C: 5.35 bar × 2.5×10⁻⁴ m³/s = 0.13 kW. Cooler load 5.2 + 0.13 + 0.6 = 5.93 kW, × 1.15 = 6.8 kW. Thermal mass 77 kg × 2.06 = 159 kJ/K; 6 kW ÷ 159 = 0.0378 K/s = 2.3 K/min; 35 K ÷ 2.3 = 15 min. Air-side rejection surface A = Q ÷ (h ΔT) = 1,000 ÷ (10 × 30) = 3.3 m² per kW, 5,200 ÷ 300 = 17 m² (h and ΔT assumed [?R]).
- Flow demand, joint speeds [?R]: walking, swing leg hip 0.9, knee 1.3, ankle 0.7 rad/s: 100 × 0.9 + 100 × 1.3 + 177 × 0.7 = 344 mL/s; stance leg 0.3, 0.2, 0.3 rad/s: 103 mL/s; torso and arms 20 mL/s; total 467 mL/s = 28.0 L/min; with arms 3 L/min, 31 L/min; deficit 1 L/min × 2 s = 0.033 L against 3.74 L. Stand-up: 2 × (100 × 0.5 + 100 × 0.5 + 177 × 0.3) = 306 mL/s = 18.4 L/min. Lift at 0.15 m/s: 2 × (131 × 0.15 + 59 × 0.3) + 10 = 85 mL/s = 5.1 L/min. Power: 200 × 9.81 × 0.15 = 294 W ÷ 10,350 W = 2.8%; 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.1 = 0.98 kW.
- Accumulator (absolute: P₀ = 91 bar, P₁ = 101 bar, P₂ = 208 bar, n = 1.4): (P₀/P₁)^(1/n) = 0.9282; (P₀/P₂)^(1/n) = 0.5538; difference 0.3744; V₀ = 2.5 ÷ 0.3744 = 6.68 L. Isothermal: 0.9010 − 0.4375 = 0.4635; V₀ = 5.39 L. For a 10 L unit: ΔV = 3.74 L; gas volume at 208 bar = 5.54 L, so oil at full charge = 4.46 L (3.9 kg); energy = (P₂V₂ − P₁V₁)/(n − 1) = (115.2 − 93.7) kJ ÷ 0.4 = 53.7 kJ. A 6 L unit: ΔV = 2.25 L. The 2.5 L comes from 15 L/min ÷ 60 × 10 s.
- Safe-posture volume: vane displacement 735 N·m ÷ 20.7 MPa ≈ 36 mL/rad. Basis: 8 cylinder joints × 1.57 rad × 100 mL = 1.26 L plus 6 vane joints × 1.57 × 36 mL = 0.34 L = 1.60 L; realistic sweeps (2 legs × (knee 1.57 + hip 1.57 + ankle 0.79) + 2 shoulders × 0.79 = 9.4 rad × 100 mL = 0.94 L, plus 6 vane × 0.5 rad × 36 mL = 0.11 L) = 1.05 L. Stabilizers: Ø25 mm A = 4.91×10⁻⁴ m², 4 × A × 0.20 m = 0.39 L; force 20.68 MPa × A = 10.2 kN against a 4.5 kN design reaction. Brakes 0.4 L [?R]. Current demand: ankle cylinders 2 × 1.57 × (177 − 100) mL = 0.24 L, shoulders and hip abduction 0.20 L, so the joint term is 1.60 + 0.24 + 0.20 = 2.04 L, the conservative demand 2.04 + 0.40 = 2.44 L (2.83 L with OP-STAB), and the margins 3.74 ÷ 2.44 = 1.53 and 3.74 ÷ 2.83 = 1.32. Bottom-up from the joint inventory: legs 2 × (101 × 1.0 + 101 × 1.57 + 177 × 0.8 + 131 × 0.5) = 928 mL; arms 2 × (131 × 1.57 + 59 × 1.57) = 597 mL; back 2 × 157 × 0.8 = 251 mL; vanes 168 mL; total 1.95 L against 2.04 L.
- Tether: pressure thrust 20.7 MPa × π/4 × 0.0159² m² = 4.1 kN. Fuse CoP shift: 400 N × 0.3 m ÷ 9.81 kN = 12.2 mm (14.7 mm at 480 N; 14.7 ÷ 65 = 22.6%). Mass 3 kg/m × 50 m = 150 kg; drag 3 × 9.81 × 0.5 = 14.7 N/m, so the fuse trips at 400 ÷ 14.7 = 27 m (320 ÷ 14.7 = 22 m) and 200 N is passed at 13.6 m; dragged pull about 0.74 kN. Bend radius 600 mm × 1.25 = 750 mm.
- Radiation units: 1 Gy = 10⁴ erg/g, so 10⁹ erg/g = 100 kGy; 10⁸ R × 0.00877 Gy/R ≈ 0.88 MGy; R3 (10 kGy) is about 1/88 of the mineral-oil figure.

**B.2 Mass**
- Mass budget: 330 + 120 + 50 + 40 + 50 + 50 = 640 kg; margin 60 ÷ 640 = 9.4%. Payload ratio 200 ÷ 680 = 0.29. Heritage conversions: 1,500 lb × 0.4536 = 680 kg; 25 hp × 745.7 W = 18.6 kW; 2.5 ft/s × 0.3048 = 0.76 m/s; 72 in = 1.83 m; 39 in = 0.99 m; 29 in = 0.74 m; 36 in = 0.91 m. Heritage link lengths: 9.25 in = 0.235 m; 19.00 in = 0.483 m; 13.20 in = 0.335 m; 15.80 in = 0.401 m; 15.62 in = 0.397 m; 9.75 in = 0.248 m; 13.00 in = 0.330 m (units assumed inches). Joint count: heritage (2 + 7 + 4 + 2) × 2 = 30.
- Dynamic factor: 0.15 m/s × 196.85 = 29.5 ft/min; × 0.5% = 14.8%, so the 15% floor gives ψ = 1.15; 3.00 × 1.15 = 3.45; accident case 1.5 ÷ 3.45 = 0.43. Structure growth exposure: f × 330 kg × (3.45 ÷ 1.5 − 1) = f × 330 × 1.30 = 0 (f = 0) to 43 kg (f = 0.10).
- Pads: 0.48 m² × 0.006 m × 2,700 kg/m³ = 7.8 kg per foot, 15.6 kg for two (low); 0.60 m² gives 9.7 kg per foot, 19.4 kg for two, × 1.5 = 29.2 kg (high). Payload mass action: 100 ÷ 1,000 = 10% of load; 0.30 × 330 × 0.10 = 9.9 kg of structure + 25 × 0.10 = 2.5 kg of pads = 12.4 kg.
- Mass model: actuators: cylinders 2 × (3.5 + 3.0 + 3.0 + 4.5) + 2 × (3.5 + 2.5) + 2 × 4.0 = 48 kg; vanes 2 × (6 + 3) + 2 × (3 + 2.5 + 1.5 + 1.5) + 2 = 37 kg; servo valves 30 × 0.6 = 18 kg; brakes and holding valves 14 × 1.1 = 15.4 kg; total 118 kg against 120. Structure: leg tubes 4 × 1.04 = 4.2 kg, feet 14, pelvis 12, links with brackets × 5 to × 10: 47 to 68 kg; torso 38 to 61 kg (spine 1.2 m × 2.17 kg/m × 5 to × 10 plus girdle 15 to 25 plus frame 10); arms 15 to 30; total 100 to 159 kg against 330. Current best estimate (accumulators 40 to 50, sensors and controllers 30 to 45, cockpit 40 to 55, covers and lead 40 to 55, actuators 100 to 136): 350 to 500 kg; × 1.25 to 1.30 = 437 to 650 kg.
- Pack mass estimates [?R]: OP-VNRA-H: 12 × 1.5 mm steel tube 0.39 kg/m plus fluid 0.058 kg/m = 0.45 kg/m; 25 m = 11 kg; four manifold blocks at 1.5 kg = 6 kg; eight isolation valves at 0.5 kg = 4 kg; total 21 kg, carried as 12 to 25 kg. OP-VNRA-F under 1 kg (HMX-3.5-01); OP-VNRA-I 0.3 to 0.8 kg. OP-NEUT 80 to 96 kg (HMX-7.3-02). OP-STAB, OP-PAD, OP-UHTH, OP-ODG as in HMX-3.1-05 and HMX-3.1-08.
- Safety battery: 150 W × 0.5 h × 3 = 225 Wh; 225 W × 0.5 h × 3 = 337.5 Wh; derate ÷ 0.8 = 422 Wh. 337.5 ÷ 51.2 V = 6.6 Ah; 422 ÷ 51.2 = 8.2 Ah. Mass 337.5 ÷ 100 = 3.4 kg; 422 ÷ 80 = 5.3 kg. 16 × 3.2 V = 51.2 V. LFP margin over 15 kGy: 22 ÷ 15 = 1.5; 36 ÷ 15 = 2.4.

**B.3 Stability, floor, stabilizers, and configuration model**
- Floor: 3,000 lb × 4.448 = 13.35 kN; (2.5 × 0.3048 m)² = 0.58 m²; 13.35 ÷ 0.58 = 23 kPa. Ground pressure at 1,000 kg: 9.81 kN; ÷ 0.30 m² = 32.7 kPa; ÷ 0.15 m² = 65.4 kPa; × 1.25 = 12.26 kN and 81.8 kPa. Reaction ÷ 13.35 kN = 73.5% static and 91.9% with the dynamic factor; 65.4 and 81.8 kPa ÷ 23 = 2.8 and 3.6. Pad area 9.81 ÷ 23 = 0.43 m² static; 12.26 ÷ 23 = 0.53 m² dynamic (0.60 m² per foot is adequate to a gross of 0.60 × 23 ÷ 1.25 ÷ 9.81 = 1,125 kg). Two pads of 0.60 m² = 1.20 m² against 0.99 × 0.74 = 0.73 m².
- CoP margin: 2 × (10 + 4 + 15) = 58 mm, set to 65 mm by architect decision (+7 mm, +12%). Slope shift 1.0 m × tan 3° = 52.4 mm. Foot: 0.15 m² ÷ 0.40 m = 0.375 m wide. Centre-of-mass shift: 200 kg × 1.0 m ÷ 1,000 kg = 0.20 m.
- Single-support payload moment: the combined CoM shift is m·d ÷ (M₀ + m), so the limit is m·d ≤ Δx × (M₀ + m), with Δx = 0.20 [+ 0.17 with stabilizers] − 0.065 − h tan 3° − |x₀|. M₀ is the lower-bound machine-plus-operator mass of the build (743 kg for STD) and h the tallest CoM height. STD: h = 0.970 m, x₀ = 2.9 mm, Δx = 0.20 − 0.065 − 0.970 × 0.0524 − 0.0029 = 0.0813 m; limits 0.0813 × 943 = 76.6 kg·m at 200 kg (0.38 m) and 0.0813 × 843 = 68.5 kg·m at 100 kg (0.69 m); 50 kg alone 0.0813 × 793 ÷ 50 = 1.29 m. Lateral: Δy = 0.1875 − 0.065 − 0.970 × 0.0524 = 0.0717 m, giving L_y = 60.4 kg·m and 0.60 m at 100 kg (L_x = 68.5). Combined axes (CAR-4): 0.5 m fore-aft with 0.3 m lateral: 100 × 0.5 ÷ 68.5 + 100 × 0.3 ÷ 60.4 = 1.23 (not admissible); 0.4 m and 0.2 m: 0.91. Deployed depth 0.40 + 2 × 0.17 = 0.74 m.
- Reach and stabilizer reaction: required reach = h tan θ + margin. 3°: 52.4 + 65 = 117 mm. 5°: 87.5 + 65 = 152.5 mm. 5° with the guard (h = 1.031 m, the guard-alone tallest bound, 90.2 mm slope): 155.2 mm. Reach 0.15 m: −2.5 and −5.2 mm; reach 0.17 m: +17.5 and +14.8 mm. Static reaction on two forward stabilizers with the feet carrying to the toe line: R = gross × 9.81 × max (0, m ÷ (M₀ + m) + z tan 5° + |x₀| + 0.065 − 0.20) ÷ 0.17 ÷ 2, evaluated over every admissible combination by the checker of Appendix F; the maximum is 3.6 kN, design × 1.25 = 4.5 kN, against 10.2 kN for a Ø25 cylinder (20.68 MPa × 4.91×10⁻⁴ m²), margin 2.3. Foot: 0.17 × 0.40 = 0.068 m²; 3.60 kN ÷ 0.068 = 53 kPa; × 1.25 = 66 kPa. Plate 0.068 × 0.004 × 4,430 = 1.2 kg, × 1.5 to × 2 = 1.8 to 2.4 kg, four feet 7.2 to 9.6 kg (carried as 7 to 10).
- Rating and CoM model: brief = min (200, 1,000 − 100 − M_dry,hi); continuous = min (100, brief ÷ 2). CoM: z = Σ m z ÷ Σ m and x = Σ m x ÷ Σ m over STD, packs, operator (100 kg at 1.00 m), and payload (at 1.10 m); loaded z is used in the slope term. STD components (Appendix F.1) sum to 643 to 688 kg (640 + 3 to 5 kg fuse + 0 to 43 kg structure growth); headroom to the 700 kg budget is 12 to 57 kg. Knee margin at gross 1,000 kg: 1,040 ÷ (0.5 × 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.15 × 1.15) = 1.23. Sensitivity sweep [?R]: 6,000 samples per build, seed 7; component masses uniform within their ranges, heights ±0.10 m and fore-aft positions ±0.05 m about the Appendix F values, operator height ±0.08 m and position ±0.03 m. STD: dry mass 646, 665, 685 kg and CoM offset 0.6, 6.4, 18.3 mm at the 5th, 50th, and 95th percentile; 3.3% of samples exceed 20 mm; offset limits 0.57, 0.67, 0.73 m (100 kg) and 0.32, 0.38, 0.41 m (200 kg).
- TRK: 643 − 195 + 200 = 648 kg and 688 − 195 + 300 = 793 kg; payload 1,000 − 100 − 793 = 107 kg; tracks 2 × 1.2 × 0.25 = 0.60 m²; 1,000 × 9.81 ÷ 0.60 = 16.4 kPa, × 1.25 = 20.4; margin 0.60 − 0.20 − 0.0875 = 0.31 m; drive 1,000 × 9.81 × (0.1 + 0.052) × 0.5 = 746 W; ÷ 20.7 MPa = 2.2 L/min.
- Guard: 11,600 ÷ 1,365 = 8.5; 227 × 9.81 × 5.2 = 11.6 kJ (drop height 5.2 m); 45 kg drop height 1,365 ÷ (45 × 9.81) = 3.1 m. M_p = 880×10⁶ × 0.004² ÷ 4 = 3,520 N·m/m; P_u = 4π × 3,520 = 44.2 kN; energy 44.2 kN × 0.08 m = 3.54 kJ; Level I stopping distance 1,365 ÷ 44.2 kN = 31 mm (34 mm at 40 kN); Level II needs 11,600 ÷ 0.08 = 145 kN or 11,600 ÷ 40,000 = 0.29 m; punching 0.6 × 880 MPa × π × 0.2 × 0.004 = 1.33 MN. Posts Ø40 × 2: 238 mm², 0.53 kg each; Euler π² E I ÷ L² = π² × 113.8×10⁹ × 4.32×10⁻⁸ ÷ 0.25 = 194 kN. Mass (6.4 + 2.1) × 1.5 = 12.7 and × 2 = 17.0 kg; canopy 0.36 m² × 0.004 m × 4,430 = 6.4 kg. Floor: 2 × 0.60 m² × 23 kPa = 27.6 kN = 2,813 kg gross; 2,813 − 1,000 = 1,813 kg of debris. Knee: 1,040 N·m ÷ 0.25 m = 4,160 N = 424 kg per leg (arm assumed [?R]).
- Micro-surge check (0.3 m hop and 50 ms [?R]): v = √(2 × 9.81 × 0.3) = 2.43 m/s; at the 1,000 kg GVWR KE = ½ × 1,000 × 2.43² = 2.94 kJ; a = 2.43 ÷ 0.05 = 48.5 m/s²; F = 1,000 × (48.5 + 9.81) = 58.3 kN = 5.9 × weight, 58.3 ÷ 13.35 = 4.4; power 2.94 kJ ÷ 0.05 s = 58.9 kW (5.7 × 10.3 kW); flow 58.9 kW ÷ 20.7 MPa = 2.85 L/s = 171 L/min; speed ratios 2.43 ÷ 0.1 = 24, ÷ 0.5 = 4.9; operator force 100 × 58.3 ÷ 1,000 = 5.8 kN, ÷ 150 N = 39, ÷ 60 N = 97; tier 1 allocation 1×10⁻⁸ ÷ (7 × 0.1) = 1.43×10⁻⁸ per hour.
- Gyro-buffer check (rotor assumptions [?R]): tipping moment 200 kg × 9.81 × 1.0 m = 1.96 kN·m. Solid disc 20 kg, r = 0.15 m, I = ½ m r² = 0.225 kg·m². A control-moment gyro gives τ = H × gimbal rate, so 1.96 kN·m at 1 rad/s needs H = 1.96 kN·m·s, ω = 8,720 rad/s = 83,000 rpm. At 10,000 rpm: H = 236 N·m·s, E = ½ I ω² = 123 kJ, gimbal rate 8.3 rad/s. At 30,000 rpm: H = 707 N·m·s, E = 1.11 MJ, gimbal rate 2.8 rad/s. Compare the 54 kJ accumulator and the 5 to 10 kJ safe-posture work.
- UHTH: 50 ÷ 100 = 50% of the continuous rating; tool alone 76.6 kg·m at 200 kg and 0.0813 × 793 ÷ 50 = 1.29 m at 50 kg; tool plus workpiece 100 kg at 0.9 m = 90 kg·m against 68.5 kg·m. Displayed tool weight at 20:1: 50 × 9.81 ÷ 20 = 24.5 N.

**B.4 Structure and stiffness**
- Knee torque: 0.5 × 1,000 kg × 9.81 × 0.15 m = 735 N·m; b(Ro² − Ri²)/2 = 735 ÷ 20.7×10⁶ = 3.6×10⁻⁵ m³. Knee cylinder: A = π/4 × 0.04² = 1.257×10⁻³ m²; F = 20.68 MPa × A = 26.0 kN; peak torque 26.0 kN × 0.08 m = 2.08 kN·m, half torque 1.04 kN·m; displacement A × 0.08 m = 1.00×10⁻⁴ m³/rad = 100 mL/rad, 100 ÷ 36 = 2.8×; stroke angle 0.15 ÷ 0.08 = 1.9 rad (107°). Margins: 1,040 ÷ 735 = 1.41; with ψ, 1,040 ÷ (735 × 1.15) = 1.23.
- Breakaway: 2% × 735 = 14.7 N·m; 1% = 7.35 N·m; 14.7 ÷ 0.08 m = 184 N; 184 ÷ 26,000 N = 0.7%; 14.7 ÷ 0.5 m = 29 N; 14 ÷ 1,988 = 0.70%; kinetic friction 2% ÷ (2 to 4) = 0.5 to 1%. Cuff torque: 140 N × 0.25 m = 35 N·m; 735 ÷ 35 = 21.
- Leg link (tube 60 × 2 mm, d = 56 mm, 0.48 m): I = π/64 × (60⁴ − 56⁴) = 153,400 mm⁴ = 15.3 cm⁴; Z = I ÷ 30 mm = 5.11 cm³; area 364 mm²; mass 364 mm² × 480 mm × 4.43 g/cm³ = 0.77 kg. M = 735 N·m. Strength: Z = 735 × 3.45 ÷ 880 MPa = 2.88 cm³. Fatigue at safety factor 2: 735 × 2 ÷ 510 MPa = 2.88 cm³ plain; ÷ 240 MPa = 6.12 cm³ notched. Buckling: P = 9.81 kN × 3.45 = 33.8 kN; I = P L² ÷ (π² E) = 33,800 × 0.2304 ÷ (9.87 × 113.8×10⁹) = 0.69 cm⁴. Rotation θ = M L ÷ (E I) = 735 × 0.48 ÷ (113.8×10⁹ × 1.534×10⁻⁷) = 0.0202 rad.
- Stiffness: k_h = βA²(1/V₁ + 1/V₂)l² = 0.7×10⁹ × (1.257×10⁻³)² × (2 ÷ 1.19×10⁻⁴) × 0.08² = 1.19×10⁵ N·m/rad; k_s = E I ÷ L with E = 113.8 GPa, L = 0.48 m: OD 60×2 I = 15.3 cm⁴, 3.64×10⁴; OD 70×2 24.7 cm⁴, 5.86×10⁴; OD 80×2 37.3 cm⁴, 8.84×10⁴; OD 90×2 53.6 cm⁴, 1.27×10⁵. Series k = k_h k_s ÷ (k_h + k_s); f = √(k ÷ 10) ÷ 2π: 17.4 Hz (hydraulic), 8.40, 9.97, 11.3, 12.5 Hz. Rotation at 735 N·m: 20.2, 12.5, 8.3, 5.8 mrad. Z(OD80×2) = 372,950 ÷ 40 = 9.3 cm³ against 6.1 cm³ notched. Tube mass: π/4 (80² − 76²) = 490 mm² × 4,430 = 2.17 kg/m against 1.61; 6.2 m × 0.56 = 3.5 kg; × 1.5 = 5.2 kg. Shoulder: A = π/4 × 0.046² = 1.66×10⁻³; V = 1.25×10⁻⁴; k_h = 1.99×10⁵; f = √(1.99×10⁵ ÷ 105) ÷ 2π = 6.9 Hz.
- Hydraulic resonance (HMX-5.3-04): k = β A² (2/V) = 1.86×10⁷ N/m; M = J/r² = 1,563, 3,125, 7,813 kg for J = 10, 20, 50 kg·m² at 0.08 m; f = √(k/M)/2π = 17.4, 12.3, 7.8 Hz; × 3 = 52, 37, 23 Hz.
- Fatigue cycles: 0.5 m/s × 3,600 s ÷ 0.4 m step = 4,500 steps per hour, 2,250 per leg; × 250 sorties × 10 years = 5.6 million per leg (SC4); at one tenth, 0.56 million (SC3). Lifts: 50 to 100 × 250 × 10 = 125,000 to 250,000 (SC2).
- Latency: phase = 360° × 5 Hz × 0.002 s = 3.6°; second-order valve with 90° at 50 Hz: atan(2ζr/(1 − r²)) at r = 0.1 gives 8.0° (ζ = 0.7) and 11.4° (ζ = 1.0). Disk margin: symmetric disk margin α gives gain range (2−α)/(2+α) to (2+α)/(2−α) and phase ±2·atan(α/2). 45°: α = 2·tan 22.5° = 0.828, gain upper bound 2.414 (7.7 dB). 6 dB (a factor 2): α = 2/3, phase ±2·atan(1/3) = ±36.9°. 30°: α = 2·tan 15° = 0.536, gain upper bound 1.732 (4.8 dB). Phase per millisecond 360° × 5 Hz × 0.001 s = 1.8°. Remaining phase: 45 − 15 = 30° and 45 − 12 = 33°; 30 − 15 = 15° and 30 − 12 = 18°.
- Control-quality limits: RSS 5 mm ÷ √6 = 2.04 mrad; 10.5 mrad ÷ √6 = 4.29 mrad; slack = mrad × 0.08 m = 0.16 and 0.32 mm. Valve: 10 L/min = 167 mL/s; 0.005 × 167 ÷ 100 = 0.0083 rad/s; ÷ (2π × 5) = 0.27 mrad; 0.02 × 167 ÷ 100 = 0.033 rad/s; ÷ 31.4 = 1.06 mrad. Leg k_eff = 1.19×10⁵ × 8.84×10⁴ ÷ (2.07×10⁵) = 5.07×10⁴; J_max = 5.07×10⁴ ÷ (2π × 10)² = 12.8 kg·m². Arm k_s = E I ÷ L = 113.8 GPa × 31.85 cm⁴ ÷ 0.40 m = 9.06×10⁴; k_eff = 1.99×10⁵ × 9.06×10⁴ ÷ 2.90×10⁵ = 6.22×10⁴; J_max = 15.8; with 40 kg at 1.0 m f = √(6.22×10⁴ ÷ 40) ÷ 2π = 6.3 Hz. Leg input: 14.7 N·m ÷ 0.25 m = 58.8 N; ÷ 140 N = 42%; at 90% compensation 5.9 N; cuff speed 1.3 × 0.25 = 0.325 m/s; gain 0.325 ÷ 20 = 0.016 m/s per N; 20 N ÷ 5 mm = 4 N/mm; 60 ÷ 140 = 43%; trials n = ln 0.05 ÷ ln 0.95 = 58.4, so 59.

**B.5 Joint bases**
- Convention: usable = basis × 1.15 × 1.2; peak = 2 × usable; F = peak ÷ lever; A = F ÷ 20.68 MPa. Knee, single support, full gross on one leg at 0.15 m lever: 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.15 = 1,472 N·m; with ψ 1,692 N·m; margin 1,040 ÷ 1,692 = 0.61; lever cap 1,040 ÷ (1.20 × 1.15 × 1,000 × 9.81) = 0.0768 m. Ankle flexion: 1.15 × 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.135 = 1,523 N·m; usable × 1.2 = 1,828 N·m; peak 3,655 N·m; at 0.08 m F = 45.7 kN, A = 2.21×10⁻³ m², bore 53.0 mm (1.76× the Ø40 knee area), 177 mL/rad. Shoulder: 981 → 1,128 → 1,354 → 2,708 N·m; F = 33.8 kN; A = 1.64×10⁻³ m²; bore 45.6 mm; 131 mL/rad. Hip abduction: 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.10 = 981 N·m, same. Elbow (0.06 m lever): 441 → 508 → 609 → 1,218 N·m; F = 20.3 kN; bore 35.4 mm; 59 mL/rad. Back flexion: (200 × 9.81 × 1.0 + 200 × 9.81 × 0.4 × sin 30°) ÷ 2 = (1,962 + 392) ÷ 2 = 1,177 N·m; × 1.15 × 1.2 = 1,625 N·m; peak 3,249 N·m; F = 40.6 kN; A = 1.96×10⁻³ m²; bore 50.0 mm; 157 mL/rad. Vanes at 20.68 MPa (displacement = usable ÷ 20.68 MPa): ankle inversion 703 × 1.38 = 971 N·m, 47 mL/rad; foot rotation 483, 23; arm abduction 474, 23; upper-arm rotation 406, 20; forearm rotation and wrist 207 and 203, 10.
- Per-arm demand at the rated payload and offset: STD max (100 × 9.81 × 0.38, 100 × 9.81 × 0.69) = 0.67 kN·m. Arm bottom-up: Z(OD76×2) = π/64 (76⁴ − 72⁴) ÷ 38 = 8.4 cm³; tube mass 2.06 kg/m × 0.40 + 1.48 kg/m × 0.45 = 1.49 kg; × 5 to 10 = 7.4 to 14.9 kg per arm. Rail load: 40 × 9.81 = 392 N; (392 + 400) × 0.35 × 3.45 = 957 N·m. Tool vibration: blow frequency 1,380 ÷ 60 = 23.0 Hz to 1,830 ÷ 60 = 30.5 Hz; transmissibility 1 ÷ ((f ÷ f_n)² − 1): f_n = 8 Hz gives 0.14 at 23 Hz and 0.074 at 30.5 Hz.
- Hardpoint and MEER: 5 × 1.15 × 9.81 = 56.4 kN; 1.25 × 9.81 = 12.3 kN; pin Ø20: π/4 × 0.02² × 0.6 × 880 MPa = 166 kN; tilt atan (0.05 ÷ 0.75) = 3.8°. 20 lbf = 89 N; 60 lbf = 267 N; 30 lbf = 133 N; 10 latches × 8 N = 80 N ÷ 1.5 = 53 N. Zero-failure trials n = ln 0.05 ÷ ln (1 − p): 59 (p = 0.05), 299 (p = 0.01). Gantry and fixtures: arrest 3 × 9.81 = 29.4 kN; stroke 9.81 × 0.05 ÷ (29.4 − 9.81) = 25 mm; clamp 9.81 × 0.20 ÷ 0.30 = 6.5 kN; × 3.45 = 22.6 kN; leg lock 1,000 × 9.81 × 0.15 × 3.45 = 5.08 kN·m; ankle 1.52 × 3.45 = 5.24 kN·m.

**B.6 Risk, dose, shielding, and egress**
- Risk: 1×10⁻⁵ per year ÷ 250 sorties = 4×10⁻⁸ per sortie; ÷ 4 h = 1×10⁻⁸ per hour. Tier 1 allocation: 1×10⁻⁸ ÷ (6 functions × 0.1) = 1.67×10⁻⁸ per hour, set at 1.6×10⁻⁸ so that 6 × 1.6×10⁻⁸ × 0.1 = 9.6×10⁻⁹ (at 2×10⁻⁸ it would be 1.2×10⁻⁸, 20% over). At 4×10⁻⁷ per sortie (10⁻⁷ per hour) the same allocation gives 1.7×10⁻⁷, SIL 2.
- Operator-dose coupling: machine dose = operator dose ÷ f. 20 mSv ÷ 0.9 = 22 mGy per year, 50 mSv ÷ 0.9 = 56 mGy per year; × 10 years = 0.22 and 0.56 Gy. f = 0.5: 40 and 100 mGy per year; f = 0.1: 200 and 500 mGy per year, 2 and 5 Gy over 10 years. Ambient ceiling = 20 mSv ÷ hours: 1,000 h gives 20 µSv/h; 200 h gives 0.1 mSv/h; 40 h gives 0.5 mSv/h; 20 h gives 1 mSv/h; 4 h gives 5 mSv/h.
- Shielding: lead density 11,340 kg/m³. 50 kg ÷ 11,340 ÷ 4 m² = 1.10 mm; ÷ 7 mm (Cs-137 HVL) = 0.157 HVL, transmission 0.5^0.157 = 0.90; ÷ 12 mm (Co-60) = 0.092 HVL, transmission 0.94. Operator surround for 1 HVL: 4 × 0.007 × 11,340 = 318 kg (Cs-137), 544 kg (Co-60); 1 TVL: 953 and 1,814 kg. Module area 2 × (0.06 + 0.045 + 0.03) = 0.27 m²: 1 HVL 21.4 kg (Cs-137), 36.7 kg (Co-60); 1 TVL 64.3 and 122.5 kg. Required transmission for 1 kGy machine dose against 500 mSv operator dose: 0.5 Gy ÷ 1,000 Gy = 5×10⁻⁴, log₂(2,000) = 10.97 HVL: 77 mm lead (Cs-137, 3,482 kg over 4 m²), 132 mm (Co-60, 5,969 kg).
- Neutron layer: water-like attenuation μ/ρ about 0.0857 cm²/g at 0.662 MeV and 0.0632 cm²/g at 1.25 MeV (recalled NIST values; verify, Appendix C). 20 mm at 1.0 g/cm³ (2.0 g/cm²): Cs-137 e^(−0.0857 × 2) = 0.84, Co-60 e^(−0.0632 × 2) = 0.88; half-value layers 8.1 and 11.0 cm. Layer volume 0.02 m × 4 m² = 0.08 m³: 80 kg at 1.0 g/cm³, 96 kg at 1.2; 80 ÷ 29 = 2.8 and 96 ÷ 13 = 7.4 times the covers line; module 0.02 × 0.27 = 0.0054 m³ = 5.4 to 6.5 kg. Per kilogram, lead gives 0.157 HVL ÷ 50 kg = 0.0031 HVL/kg and the water-like layer 0.25 HVL ÷ 80 kg = 0.0031 HVL/kg, so mass, not material, limits gamma shielding.
- Exposure time: 100 Sv/h × 10 h = 1,000 Sv, about 1 kGy; 1,000 ÷ 210 = 4.8 h; 1,000 ÷ 650 = 1.5 h. R3 average rate over 10 years at 1,000 h per year: 10,000 Gy ÷ 10,000 h = 1 Gy/h; a 111 Gy COTS board at that rate lasts about 111 h.
- Escape and egress: 15 min × 35 L/min = 525 L; × 40 = 600 L; 2 L × 300 bar = 600 L (ideal gas, 1 bar ambient). Egress 60 + 60 + 30 + 180 = 330 s against 360 s; with the 10 s release 60 + 10 + 30 + 180 = 280 s, slack 80 s; walk 100 m ÷ 180 s = 0.56 m/s; air margin 15 ÷ 6 = 2.5. Dose: 0.5 mSv/h × 0.1 h = 0.05 mSv; 5 mSv/h × 0.1 h = 0.5 mSv; 0.5 ÷ 2 = 25%.


**B.7 Operator population, access, sensing, and hand**
- Stature and mass: ANSUR II pilot men, σ = (1,872 − 1,643) ÷ 3.29 = 69.6 mm; 90th percentile 1,755 + 1.2816 × 69.6 = 1,844 mm; 1,800 mm gives z = (1,800 − 1,755) ÷ 69.6 = 0.65, the 74th percentile. Weight σ = (110.7 − 63.9) ÷ 3.29 = 14.2 kg; 90th percentile 84.2 + 1.2816 × 14.2 = 102.4 kg; 95 kg bare gives z = (95 − 84.2) ÷ 14.2 = 0.76, the 78th percentile; 102.4 + 5 − 100 = 7.4 kg of derating. Pooled women: σ = (179 − 151) ÷ 3.29 = 8.51 cm; 10th percentile 165 − 1.2816 × 8.51 = 154.1 cm; 1.80 m gives z = 1.76, the 96th percentile. Operator CoM 0.55 × 1.54 = 0.85 m and 0.55 × 1.84 = 1.01 m [?R].
- Guard and head height: underside 1.95 − 0.05 = 1.90 m (0.05 m for plate and fittings [?R]); head top = stature + h_f with h_f = 0.235 − 0.08 = 0.155 m; rated stature 1.90 − 0.10 − 0.155 = 1.645 m, rated 1.64 m. A 1.84 m operator needs 1.84 + 0.155 + 0.10 + 0.05 = 2.145 m, and the route opening 2.145 + 0.10 = 2.25 m against the IBC minimum 2.032 m. Unguarded route height 1.84 + 0.155 + 0.10 = 2.095 m, rounded to 2.10 m; IBC-minimum stature limit 2.032 − 0.10 − 0.155 = 1.777 m.
- Access: forward 0.99 + 2 × 0.05 = 1.09 m; sideways clearance (813 − 740) ÷ 2 = 36.5 mm each side; a 36 in nominal door is about 33.5 in = 851 mm clear; heights 1.83 + 0.10 = 1.93 m and 1.95 + 0.10 = 2.05 m against the IBC minimum 2.032 m.
- Sensing: 3 × (60, 70, 90, 100, 130, 140, 150, 150 N) = 180, 210, 270, 300, 390, 420, 450, 450 N; footplate 981 × 1.25 × 1.5 = 1,839 N, rounded to 2 kN. JND at 20 N: 8% = 1.6 N, 13% = 2.6 N; 0.5 ÷ 1.6 = 31%; deadband ratio 5 ÷ 2.6 = 1.9 and 5 ÷ 1.6 = 3.1. ±450 N at 0.5 N = 900 ÷ 0.5 = 1,800 steps < 2,048 = 2¹¹. Travel at 100 N: 100 ÷ 4 = 25 mm, 100 ÷ 10 = 10 mm; 25 mm ÷ 250 mm = 0.100 rad = 5.7°.
- Hand: per hand 100 × 9.81 = 981 N; N = 981 ÷ (2 × 0.3) = 1,635 N; × 1.5 = 2,453 N, rated 2.5 kN; continuous (50 × 9.81 = 491 N) ÷ 0.6 × 1.5 = 1,226 N. Ratio 2,500 ÷ 150 = 16.7; 2,500 ÷ 20 = 125 N. Grip strength 55 × 9.81 = 540 N; 32 × 9.81 = 314 N; 36 × 9.81 = 353 N. Torque 2,500 × 0.08 = 200 N·m; × 1.15 × 1.2 = 276 N·m; ÷ 20.68 MPa = 13.3 mL/rad; 40 ÷ 20.68 = 1.9, rounded to 2 mL/rad. Mass 2 × (2 + 0.5) = 5 kg against the 7 kg margin.

**B.8 Site criteria, M2 link, decontamination, and the EHA trade**
- M2 budget: leftover phase at the floor 30° − (12 to 15)° = 15 to 18°; per millisecond 360° × 5 Hz × 0.001 s = 1.8°; 15 ÷ 1.8 = 8.3 ms and 18 ÷ 1.8 = 10 ms; nominal 45° − 15° = 30°, 30 ÷ 1.8 = 16.7 ms. Fibre round trip 2 × 50 m × 5 ns/m = 0.5 µs. Link loss: 3 frames × 1 ms + 1 ms = 4 ms; 1.3 rad/s × 0.004 s = 5.2 mrad = 0.30°; 0.5 m/s × 0.004 s = 2.0 mm.
- Decontamination: DF = C_before ÷ C_after, and the required DF is C_zone ÷ limit. For a zone surface at 400 Bq/cm² beta and gamma: 400 ÷ 0.4 = 1,000 for Level R and 400 ÷ 4 = 100 for Level T. Wipe correction: a wipe reading of 0.04 Bq/cm² at the default efficiency of 0.10 is 0.04 ÷ 0.10 = 0.4 Bq/cm².
- Floor: 1.25 × 1,000 × 9.81 = 12.26 kN; ÷ 0.15 m² = 81.8 kPa; ÷ 0.60 m² = 20.4 kPa (≤ 23 kPa); stabilizer foot 52.9 × 1.25 = 66.2 kPa. Dynamic factor: 1 + 0.15 × 0.2 + 0.5 × 0.2 + 0.10 = 1.23. At 1.5: 1.5 × 9.81 = 14.7 kN, 14.7 ÷ 13.35 = 110%; GVWR 13,350 ÷ (1.5 × 9.81) = 907 kg; bare foot 65.4 × 1.5 = 98.1 kPa; feet 52.9 × 1.5 = 79.4 kPa; pad area 14.7 ÷ 23 = 0.640 m²; gross adequate at 0.60 m² = 0.60 × 23,000 ÷ (1.5 × 9.81) = 938 kg; TRK 16.4 × 1.5 = 24.6 kPa.
- Zone to class: hours = TID ÷ dose rate; at 1 Gy/h, 100 Gy gives 100 h, 1 kGy gives 1,000 h, and 10 kGy gives 10,000 h.
- EHA: heat = P_mech × (1 ÷ η − 1) = P_mech × 0.43 at η = 0.7, so 1 to 3 kW gives 0.43 to 1.29 kW; against the 5.2 kW of the oil path. Rated cylinder forces (kN): hip flexion 2 × 26.0, knee 2 × 26.0, ankle 2 × 45.7, shoulder 2 × 33.8, elbow 2 × 20.3, hip abduction 2 × 33.8, back flexion 2 × 40.6, sum 452.4 kN; ÷ 2,518 N/kg = 179.7 kg, and 226.2 kN ÷ 2,518 N/kg = 89.8 kg at the usable force; against 48 kg of cylinders and 18 kg of servo valves.
- Waste: fluid 22 + 5 + 50 = 77 kg; tether 3 kg/m × 50 m = 150 kg.

**B.9 Operator comfort, maintenance, and gate counts**
- Metabolic rate: 180 W × 90 ÷ 70 = 231 W, inside the 125 to 235 W band of class 1. Cockpit limits: 30 − 3 = 27 °C and 30 − 11 = 19 °C. CoG: 100 kg × 0.03 m ÷ 1,000 kg = 3 mm. Shift: 8 − 4 − 1.5 = 2.5 h.
- Maintenance: 0.8 × 5.6×10⁶ = 4.48×10⁶ cycles; 0.8 × 125,000 = 100,000 and 0.8 × 250,000 = 200,000.
- Gate counts: n = ln 0.05 ÷ ln 0.95 = 58.4, so 59; ln 0.05 ÷ ln 0.99 = 298.1, so 299; 299 × 60 s = 17,940 s = 5.0 h; t = −ln 0.05 ÷ 1.6×10⁻⁸ per hour = 3.0 ÷ 1.6×10⁻⁸ = 1.9×10⁸ h.

**B.10 Closing the pack and residual items**
- Feeder break: v = √(2ΔP ÷ ρ) = √(2 × 20×10⁶ ÷ 897) = 211 m/s; Q = C_d A v with C_d = 0.6: Ø6 mm (A = 2.83×10⁻⁵ m²) 3.58 L/s, Ø8 mm (5.03×10⁻⁵ m²) 6.37 L/s, Ø10 mm (7.85×10⁻⁵ m²) 9.95 L/s. Allowed loss 0.05 × 3.74 L = 0.187 L gives 52, 29, and 19 ms; at 50 ms the loss would be 0.18, 0.32, and 0.50 L. Feeder velocity 7.8 L/min ÷ 5.03×10⁻⁵ m² = 2.6 m/s; 28 L/min in Ø8 mm would be 9.3 m/s, so trunk lines are larger.
- Fibre network: 12 × 2 × 2 = 48; 48 + 16 = 64; 64 ÷ 4 = 16 gratings per fibre; 4 + 2 spare = 6 fibres. Strain amplitude 240 MPa ÷ 113.8 GPa = 2,109 µε; 1% = 21 µε; × 1.2 pm/µε = 25 pm.
- Range of motion: 0.9 × (180, 60, 150, 80, 70, 120, 20, 20, 50) = 162, 54, 135, 72, 63, 108, 18, 18, 45, rounded down to 5°: 160, 50, 135, 70, 60, 105, 15, 15, 45 (shoulder flexion and extension, elbow, wrist flexion and extension, hip flexion and extension, ankle dorsiflexion and plantarflexion). Knee: 0.9 × 135 = 121.5, capped by the actuator at 107°.

**B.11 Population, footplate-height, and operator-CoM sweeps**
- Footplate height h_f: rated guard stature = 1.90 − 0.10 − h_f. The guard top for a 1.84 m operator = 1.84 + h_f + 0.10 + 0.05; the unguarded route height = 1.84 + h_f + 0.10; the IBC-minimum stature limit = 2.032 − 0.10 − h_f, capped at 1.84 m. Percentiles use the normal fits of B.7 (men: ANSUR II pilot; women: pooled placeholder).

| h_f m | Rated guard stature m | Man percentile | Woman percentile | Guard top for 1.84 m | Unguarded route height | IBC-minimum stature limit |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0.000 | 1.80 | 74% | 96% | 1.99 m | 1.94 m | 1.84 m |
| 0.050 | 1.75 | 47% | 88% | 2.04 m | 1.99 m | 1.84 m |
| 0.080 | 1.72 | 31% | 79% | 2.07 m | 2.02 m | 1.84 m |
| 0.155 | 1.64 | 5% | 45% | 2.15 m | 2.10 m | 1.78 m |

- Operator mass: metabolic rate = 180 W × bare mass ÷ 70; class 1 at or below 235 W (reference WBGT 30 °C), class 2 above (28 °C); cockpit limit = reference − CAV (3 °C or 11 °C); dressed mass = bare + 5 kg; payload derating = dressed mass − 100 kg, if positive.

| Bare mass kg | Dressed kg | Metabolic W | Class | Reference WBGT | Cockpit limit, double layer / vapor barrier | Payload derating kg |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 80 | 85 | 206 | 1 | 30 °C | 27 / 19 °C | 0 |
| 90 | 95 | 231 | 1 | 30 °C | 27 / 19 °C | 0 |
| 95 | 100 | 244 | 2 | 28 °C | 25 / 17 °C | 0 |
| 102 | 107 | 262 | 2 | 28 °C | 25 / 17 °C | 7 |

- Operator CoM (STD, upper-bound masses, payload at 1.10 m; re-implementation of F.1, F.0): operator CoM 1.00 m gives loaded CoM height 0.968 m and offset limits 0.384 m and 0.686 m at 200 kg and 100 kg; at 1.12 m (median stature, h_f = 0.155 m) 0.980 m, 0.381 m and 0.681 m; at 1.17 m (1.84 m operator) 0.985 m, 0.379 m and 0.678 m. The limits fall by 0.8% and 1.3%, so the 1.00 m model stands.
- Sensitivity, by effect on a headline limit: footplate height moves the guard stature one for one (±0.05 m is about 20 percentile points of men); operator mass moves the payload rating one for one above 100 kg dressed and the cockpit WBGT limit by 2 °C at the class boundary; the floor dynamic factor of 1.25 against 1.5 moves the gross rating to about 907 kg (HMX-14-02); the operator CoM moves the offset limits by about 1%.

### C.2 Assumptions carried as [?R] or [SPEC] inputs

A change in any of these moves the numbers that rest on them.

1. *Stability and floor:* CoM height of the loaded machine from the component model of Appendix F.1, the 1.10 m payload hold height, the 100 kg operator centre at 1.00 m, the foot 0.40 × 0.375 m, feet carrying to the toe line in the stabilizer reaction model, the 0.17 m stabilizer reach, tether friction 0.5, a 0.15 m knee arm, and the 1.25 dynamic factor.
2. *Configuration model:* the component masses and positions of Appendix F.1, the pack masses and positions of F.3 (the OP-VNRA-H estimate is derived in Appendix B from assumed tubing, manifold, and valve counts), the 1.95 m guard top, and the thresholds of CAR-2, CAR-3, and CAR-5. Until CAD mass properties replace them (HMX-13.2-09), every rating is a design-phase figure. The sensitivity-sweep ranges, the 0.08 m ankle lever, the ankle axis at the foot centre, the 1.2 margin, and the 5th-percentile quoting rule are [?R] or [SPEC].
3. *Arms, ankle, feet:* the 1.0 m reach basis for the STD arms, the 125 kg arm-allocation downside of about 100 kg if torque-proportional mass was sized for 746 N·m, the 4 mm plate and ×1.2 to ×1.5 rib factors for the stabilizer feet, the 1 kg CoM-adjustment mount, and the 2 kg per ankle upsizing.
4. *Thermal and guard geometry:* ester specific heat 2.0 kJ/kg·K (now replaced by vendor values 2.06 to 2.24), natural-convection coefficient 10 W/m²K, 30 K difference, titanium conductivity about 7 W/m·K, a 0.25 m knee arm, a 0.36 m² canopy, and a 0.3 m hop in 50 ms.
5. *Dynamics and flow:* joint speeds in the flow model, the 10 kg·m² reflected inertia and 105 kg·m² payload inertia, bulk modulus 0.7 GPa and chamber volumes, load-path tube length 6.2 m and the fitting factors, arm component masses, the 400 N feed force, the 10-latch count and 8 N latch force, pump loss 0.6 kW, thermal mass 77 kg, the 45 kN collapse cap, and the 5-minute crew arrival.
6. *Joint inventory and fixtures:* the lever arms (0.08 m, 0.06 m), the 0.35 m, 0.30 m, and 0.15 m payload levers, the 350 N·m foot-rotation and 150 N·m forearm torques, the back-flexion lean of 30° and 200 kg upper body at 0.4 m, the passive ranges (±15°, ±10°), the 5 mm and 10 mm position budgets, component masses in the bottom-up model and the × 5 to × 10 bracket factors, the tracked-base mass of 200 to 300 kg and the 0.25 m track width, the 0.1 rolling resistance, the 0.05 m gantry drop, and the 10 kN foot-clamp shear.
7. *Checker validation:* the generator `hmx_config_check.py` has not been shown to a reader of this document; the v3.1 re-implementation of F.0 checks the F.4 mass, rating, and offset columns for five builds, and neither validates the assumptions above.
8. *Operator, access, sensing, and hand:* head height equal to stature (footplate and hood offset zero), the 0.55 × stature CoM factor, 0.05 m of guard plate and fittings, 0.05 m of passage clearance per side, friction coefficient 0.3 and a 1.5 grip factor, cuff stiffness of 4 to 10 N/mm, the 0.08 m grip lever, the hand actuator masses, and the 0.25 m thigh-cuff lever.
9. *Site, link, and waste:* the 0.10 walking allowance in the dynamic factor, the 5 Hz crossover and 1.3 rad/s joint speed in the M2 link-loss arithmetic, a 1 kHz link cycle, the 400 Bq/cm² zone level used only as an example, the 100 kg and 1.2 m dismantling limits, the HPS fluid charge of about 50 kg, EHA efficiency 0.7 with 1 to 3 kW of mechanical power, and the single-prototype force-to-weight ratio of 2,518 N/kg.
10. *Comfort, maintenance, and gates:* operator metabolic class 1 at 232 W, the 1.0 °C machine-induced WBGT rise and the 43 °C touch limit (design choices), the ±30 mm operator CoM tolerance, the 1.5 h shift overhead and the 2 assistants, the quarterly and 3-year intervals marked [?R] in §12, the 2-year onboard hose interval, the 80% cycle and capacity thresholds, and the 59-sortie hot-validation count.
11. *Pack and residual items:* the 0.6 discharge coefficient, 20 MPa pressure, 897 kg/m³ density, and 5% reserve-loss limit; the 15, 10, and 5 ms split of the 30 ms isolation time; Ø8 mm joint feeders; the count of 64 gratings on 6 fibres; the 1.2 pm/µε sensitivity; the 20% adoption gate and 10% cap; the 10% neutron trigger; the ±10 mm, ±5°, and 5 mm RMS alignment values; and the tall-kneel and seat postures of HMX-3.4-03.
12. *Evidence pass:* head height taken as stature, the 5 kg of clothing in the 100 kg dressed allowance, the 0.55 × stature CoM factor, the pooled female placeholder, and the single-prototype EHA force-to-weight ratio of 2,518 N/kg applied to the rated cylinder forces.
