## Appendix D. Concept Dispositions

Concepts that were proposed and what became of each. Adopted items are carried as option packs (OP-UHTH, OP-ODG, OP-VNRA-F, -H, -I) or STD (MEER, thermal control, HMX-1.8-02). Rejected concepts are recorded so the reasoning is not lost; they are not requirements. Numbers are in Appendix B and sources in the references.

| Concept | Disposition | Reason | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| Distributed FBG structural and radiation skin | Adopted, reshaped: monitor-tier FBG network on load-bearing links | Passive glass in the zone with the interrogator on the clean side is a real strength. FBGs are discrete gratings, not a continuum; they measure strain and temperature at the grating and cannot see lattice-level fatigue before a crack forms; dose mapping 'at every square centimetre' is not supportable, and fibre dosimetry resolves tens of Gy | HMX-3.5-01 to 3.5-06 |
| Autonomous fluidic rerouting inside structural titanium links | Adopted, reshaped: dual-path onboard distribution on conventional tubing and manifold blocks, tier 2 at most | Channels in links are notches in a fatigue-governed member and cannot be finished; additively manufactured channels risk contamination; a 2 mm wall cannot house them; rerouting does not avoid the HMX-11.2-02 abort; adding it to the SIL 3 loop would tighten the tier 1 allocation | HMX-4.4-01 to 4.4-05 |
| Predictive neural-intent blending (sEMG) | Retained as a default-off trial with an authority cap | EMD is tens of ms, not a fraction of a millisecond; 113 to 328 ms is human reaction time, not machine lag; the M1 loop already responds in 2 ms; the benefit is uncertain and reflex or artefact is a hazard | HMX-6.6-01 to 6.6-03 |
| Thermal-neutron phase shield: 15 to 20 mm circulating D₂O ice/fluid hybrid in the inner hull | Rejected | D₂O is used in reactors because it absorbs neutrons weakly (deuterium thermal capture about 0.5 mb against about 332 mb for hydrogen), which makes it a poor shield; 20 mm over 4 m² is about 89 kg of D₂O, more than the 60 kg margin and 3 to 7× the covers line; deuterium capture makes tritium, a waste and contamination problem; D₂O freezes at 3.8 °C, so an 'ice/fluid hybrid' needs active chilling and a circulating loop in the zone, against HMX-1.4-02; gamma, the actual driver (HMX-7.2-10), is untouched (about 0.84 transmission at Cs-137); and no neutron field is defined | HMX-7.3-01 |
| Kinetic Gyro-Buffer: composite flywheel for stabilization and egress energy | Rejected | A gyro gives transient torque, not a held moment, and a payload offset is a static moment (control-moment gyros saturate); for 2 kN·m a 20 kg, 0.15 m rotor needs H of about 2 kN·m·s per rad/s of gimbal rate, which is 83,000 rpm at 1 rad/s or a 2.8 to 8.3 rad/s gimbal at 30,000 to 10,000 rpm storing 1.1 to 0.12 MJ beside the operator; Appendix A already rejected flywheels for gyroscopic torque in turns and burst hazard; HMX-3.2-04 forbids reliance on dynamic balancing; egress is a manual release needing no stored energy (safe-posture work is 5 to 10 kJ and volume-governed, HMX-4.3-02), and untethered operation is out of scope | Appendix A, HMX-3.2-04 |
| Shielding gel (thixotropic, doped with B₄C or gadolinium salts) as gamma and neutron shield | Reduced to a conditional option: local borated hydrogenous layer, credited with no gamma shielding | Gamma attenuation per kilogram is within about 30% of lead at 0.66 MeV (Compton regime), so 80 to 96 kg buys only about 0.25 to 0.3 half-value layers, and HMX-3.1-06 already shows operator shielding is not affordable; gadolinium capture emits gammas up to about 7.9 MeV; a 'self-sealing' layer is not a leak barrier; polymer radiolysis, gas, and decontamination are open; boron is the right absorber if neutrons exist | HMX-7.3-02 |
| Universal Heavy-Tool Hardpoint | Adopted as OP-UHTH on the forearm rails | Tool weight to the floor through the frame matches HMX-1.4-01; the tool is payload (50% of the continuous rating for 50 kg); tool reactions take ψ × Nd; plasma cutting needs its own hazard case | HMX-5.4-04 to 5.4-11 |
| High-pressure micro-surge accumulators at each joint | Rejected | A 50 ms surge of the machine at the 1,000 kg GVWR needs about 59 kW (5.7× peak), about 170 L/min, 5.9× weight ground reaction, and about 5.8 kN on the operator against 60 to 150 N cuff limits; a false trigger is uncommanded violent motion; it adds a seventh tier 1 function; it adds no holding force (valves and brakes already hold); it contradicts HMX-3.2-04, 1.6-04, 5.2-04, and the 0.5 m/s gait | HMX-3.2-11, 4.3-09 |
| Pyrotechnic and mechanical emergency egress release (PMEER) | Adopted, reshaped: MEER, mechanical only, in STD | The pyrotechnic element puts an ignition and fragment source in a fluid-fire and contamination zone, needs explosives approval and radiation-qualified initiators, and an electrical firing path adds a tier 1 function with an inadvertent-firing hazard; the real gain (a faster release than 60 s) is met by a guarded mechanical release at a 10 s target, with a rescue-crew release | HMX-9.1-07 to 9.1-09 |
| Dual-loop high-flow hydraulic architecture | Rejected | The HPS pump is already variable-displacement; pumps stay off the machine; spec numbers show 0.29 kW for a 200 kg lift at the speed cap; doubling flow breaks the 5% pressure-drop rule; the 30 L/min supply covers the demand | HMX-4.1-07 |
| Micro-lattice regenerative cooling core in the titanium leg spars | Not adopted; cooling relocated to the HPS | Channels in a fatigue-governed link are notches (as in HMX-4.4-04); titanium conducts poorly and a 2 mm wall cannot hold a lattice; the only air-side sink is the hot-cell air (about 17 m² for 5.2 kW); a high-surface exchanger is a decontamination trap; the machine heat leaves in the return flow (about 12 K rise) | HMX-4.1-05, 4.1-06 |
| Active kinetic ROPS diverting 100% of crush force through the feet | Adopted, reshaped: passive overhead debris guard (OP-ODG) | Roll-over is not the hazard (FOPS is the standard analogue); '100% diversion' and 'uncompromised survival box' cannot be claimed; the floor design basis limits debris to about 1.8 t and hydraulically held knees hold about 0.42 t per leg; 'active' and 'kinetic' elements add uncommanded-motion risk; a multi-ton cage does not fit the 60 kg margin | HMX-3.6-01 to 3.6-07, 13.2-04 |
| Onboard electro-hydrostatic actuators in place of the remote HPS | Rejected for the baseline | About 28 motor-pump modules with power drives would sit in the zone where only valve torque motors sit now; a power bus on a wash-down machine; heat dissipation is poor without a central tank, though the load (0.4 to 1.3 kW) is not decisive; mass of 90 to 180 kg against about 66 kg for cylinders and valves; the valve-based plant and test plan would be redone. Reopen triggers are in HMX-1.4-02 | HMX-1.4-02 |
| Seated ride-mode variant of M1 | Not adopted; replaced by TRK | Keeps the legs, stabilizers, and floor-pressure problem; heritage found it less natural; a tracked base removes the floor problem (20.4 kPa against 82) | HMX-1.4-05, 1.4-08 |

## Appendix E. Review Session Prompt

The architect's standing prompt, retained for the case where a TBD exists; none is open at v3.1, so use the evidence-closure prompt after it. Paste the chosen block at the start of the session together with the current specification.

```
Act as a Principal Systems Architect and Red-Team Engineering Partner with multidisciplinary mastery across:
• Structural & Mechanical Engineering: BTH-1 design factors, dynamic load factors (psi = 1.15), fatigue cycles (CMAA/ASME), and center of pressure (CoP) margins.
• Fluid Power & Hydraulics: Viscous flow losses across 50m tethers, HFDU ester fluid phase behaviors, servo valve bandwidth, and bladder accumulator gas dynamics.
• Control Theory & Robotics: Impedance control, 1 kHz loop timing, passivity analysis, and joint-in-series stability.
• Nuclear Systems & Health Physics: Total Ionizing Dose (TID) degradation, half-value layer (HVL) lead shielding math, ICRP/NRC occupational dose caps, and low-cobalt activation limits.
• Functional Safety Engineering: Probabilistic Fault Per Hour (PFH) allocations, IEC 61508 safety loops, hazard trees, and ISO 13849 PL targets.
• Human Factors & Ergonomics: Anthropometric percentiles (10th-90th) and COVR pain-onset force limits per body segment.

We are systematically reviewing my current System Architecture Specification to resolve a fresh batch of remaining open items (TBDs / Unresolved Questions) in this document.

Instructions for this session:
1. Target Selection: Select the top TBD items from the specification that are most critical to structural integrity, system safety, or physical viability.
2. Grounded Resolution: Resolve each selected item using rigorous physical principles, existing engineering precedents, and verified real-world vendor/academic data (citing sources where available).
3. Epistemic Tagging: Label every resolution using our established framework:
◦ [D] Directly confirmed via cited source
◦ [R] Reasoned from confirmed evidence
◦ [S] Structurally inferred from adjacent technology
◦ [?R] Extrapolated beyond available data
◦ [SPEC] Speculative / Architectural proposal
4. Impact Audit: For every item resolved, clearly detail its downstream impact on mass/weight budgets, energy/power allocations, structural limits, and spatial manifests.

Let's begin by selecting the first batch of TBDs to tackle step-by-step.
```

Evidence-closure prompt (v3.1), for the items of Appendix G:

```
Act as a systems engineer closing evidence gaps in the attached specification. For each item of Appendix G that I name, search for primary or near-primary sources, quote nothing beyond what the licence allows, and report: Verified (confirmed in a second independent source), Partly, or Not found. For every value you confirm or correct, name each requirement and parameter that depends on it (use Appendix H), recompute the derived numbers, tag the result with the evidence tags of §0, and state what changes in the mass, power, structure, and space budgets. Do not close an item on one secondary source, and list what only a test or a site survey can supply.
```

State at v3.1, for the next session:

- **Open items:** none (§15). The remaining work is evidence, not values: the external evidence requests of Appendix G (public documents and data to be searched in depth), the weighing and CAD mass properties (HMX-13.2-09), the hardware-limb and pack release tests (HMX-13.2-07, HMX-13.2-11, HMX-13.2-12), and the facility survey against HMX-14-02.
- **Evidence dependencies of settled items:** the FEA and modal test of HMX-13.2-08, a plant model and hardware-limb test for HMX-6.1-09, the ester irradiation test (HMX-4.1-04), and the facility survey that maps radiation classes and neutron fields (HMX-14-02).
- **Configuration check:** run `hmx_config_check.py` after any change to a pack, mass, or position, and carry its output into Appendix F.
- **Conventions:** every resolution carries the five epistemic tags. Each batch updates the affected requirements in place, adds one row to the change log, updates Appendix B for any new derivation and the §15 register, and records any new source in the references and Appendix C. Closed items leave the register; the reasoning stays in the requirement that settled them.


## Epistemic Status and References

- **[D]** Baselines carried from KINETIC-ADS SSD v1.0 are as documented there. Hardiman I facts cited as [GE-71 p.N] were read from that report (single source). Facts cited as [NASA-68 p.N] were read from the OCR text layer of a scanned secondary source. Vendor and trade values are [D] only as quoted from the cited document; their documents are secondary and several are scanned or garbled extracts, so Appendix C.1 lists them for verification before design freeze.
- **[R]** Derived values follow from stated baselines (Appendix B); the mapping from heritage findings to requirements is reasoned.
- **[S]** Tethered operation, nuclear decommissioning as the use case, and the M1/M2 split are strategic choices. Standards analogies (BTH-1, ISO 3449, FMVSS 217, ASME B30.23) are [S].
- **[?]** All TBD items; the heritage link lengths and joint excursions read from the p.2A scan (§3.4); the radiation-tolerance range in §7.1; the causes of the heritage runaway that the report itself calls unverified; the contents of the earlier Hardiman reports.
- **[SPEC]** The configuration architecture (§1.8, Appendix F), the option packs, the VNRA set, the UHTH, MEER, thermal, and guard requirements, the 65 mm CoP margin, and the thresholds of the admissibility rules are architectural proposals; their model results are [R] on [?R] allocations and are reproduced by `hmx_config_check.py`. The inputs of every [R] result that rest on assumptions are listed in Appendix C.2.

## Appendix F. Configuration Matrix and Option Packs

[SPEC] on [R] arithmetic over [?R] allocations. The tables below were produced by the companion file `hmx_config_check.py`; their provenance, and what has and has not been re-derived since, is recorded in F.0. Each limit is rated at its own worst-case mass bound (HMX-1.8-04): payload, floor, and knee at the high bound; stability at the low bound and the tallest CoM; offsets are capped at the 1.0 m reach. OP-NEUT is reserved; the 128 orderable combinations are the seven other packs. The TRK variant (HMX-1.4-08) is a separate build, not a pack, and is not in these tables.

### F.0 Provenance of this appendix

- **Generator:** `hmx_config_check.py`. It is not published with this document, and its version hash and the date of its last run were not recorded; they shall be published with each release (§0, EXT-30).
- **Last regeneration:** the F.1 to F.8 figures are those of the earlier baseline from which this document was cleaned; they have not been regenerated since. Edits since then that touch mass or position assumptions are: the hand actuators (5 kg inside the 7 kg arm-actuator margin, HMX-5.4-01), the cockpit hot-line insulation (up to 2 kg inside the cockpit line, HMX-8.1-03), and the operator-mass rating rule (HMX-1.8-04). Each fits inside an existing allocation, so no figure here is expected to change, but the rule of §0 is not yet met.
- **Independent check:** a re-implementation of the F.1 model written for the v3.1 review (same reviewer, so not independent corroboration) reproduces the mass, rated payload, loaded CoM height (within 2 mm), CoM offset (within 0.1 mm), and offset limits of the STD, HANDLING, TOOLING, and PROTECTED rows of F.4, and was used for the added F.4b row. The CoM, offset, and sweep columns of the remaining rows, and F.2, F.6, and F.7, were not recomputed.
- **Statement:** the F tables are a design-phase model whose generator has not been shown to a reader of this document.

### F.1 Standard build mass and CoM model

Positions are height z above the floor and fore-aft x forward of the foot centre, for a standing posture [?R]. The 640 kg of §3.1 is split over the allocation lines; the 0 to 43 kg row is the structure-growth exposure of HMX-3.1-03 (the stiffness sizing of HMX-3.1-09, +4 to +8 kg, lies inside it) and the 3 to 5 kg row the fuse module of HMX-3.1-05. The bottom-up estimate of Appendix B (current best estimate 350 to 500 kg, 437 to 650 kg with growth allowance) sits below the 640 kg allocation and does not change it (HMX-13.2-09). Operator 100 kg at 1.00 m; payload at 1.10 m. The operator CoM implied by the standing height of HMX-3.4-06 is about 1.12 m at the median stature; the effect on the limits is under 1% (Appendix B.11).

| Component | Mass kg | z m | x m |
|---|---|---|---|
| Structure: legs | 140 | 0.42 | +0.00 |
| Structure: pelvis and torso | 110 | 1.05 | +0.00 |
| Structure: arms and shoulders | 80 | 1.30 | +0.10 |
| Structure growth (HMX-3.1-03) | 0 to 43 | 0.95 | +0.00 |
| Actuators: legs | 55 | 0.50 | +0.00 |
| Actuators: torso | 20 | 1.05 | +0.00 |
| Actuators: arms | 45 | 1.25 | +0.10 |
| Accumulator, manifolds, fluid | 50 | 1.05 | -0.12 |
| Sensors and controllers | 40 | 1.20 | -0.12 |
| Cockpit, restraint, air, MEER | 50 | 1.10 | +0.00 |
| Decontamination covers | 25 | 1.00 | +0.00 |
| Electronics-module lead | 25 | 1.20 | -0.12 |
| Tether fuse module | 3 to 5 | 0.30 | -0.20 |
| **STD total** | **643 to 688** | 0.935 (with operator, unloaded, tallest bound) | +2.9 mm |

STD headroom under the 700 kg budget: 12 kg at the upper bound, 57 kg at the lower (8 and 53 kg if the ankle upsizing of up to 4 kg does not fit its line). M₀ for the stability rule is the lower-bound machine plus operator, 743 kg for STD.

### F.2 What the model shows

- Loaded CoM height 0.970 m for STD and 0.92 to 0.98 m across admissible combinations (tallest bound); fore-aft CoM offset at most 7.2 mm over every combination (limit 20 mm).
- STD single-support limits (3° slope): 0.38 m at 200 kg and 0.69 m at 100 kg fore-aft; 0.60 m lateral for 100 kg, which governs one-sided holds; combined axes by the sum-of-ratios rule (CAR-4).
- Knee margin with ψ is 1.23 at the 1,000 kg gross (double-support basis) and the single-support lever is capped at 77 mm; stabilizer reaction at most 3.6 kN static (4.5 kN design), 53 kPa static and 66 kPa dynamic on the 0.068 m² feet.
- Safe-posture margin 1.53 for STD and 1.32 with OP-STAB (demand 2.04 + 0.40 L, plus 0.39 L; the inventory of HMX-3.4-04 gives 1.95 L for the joint term).
- Of 128 orderable combinations, 116 are admissible at the upper-bound mass and 124 with the composite accumulator shell. Without the mass action, 12 fail the 100 kg minimum rating (CAR-2).

| Pack | Mass kg | CoM z / x m | Brief rating alone kg | What it gives | Cost, dependencies, conflicts | Gating |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| OP-STAB: Deployable stabilizers and feet (HMX-3.2-03) | 27 to 50 | 0.30 / +0.10 | 162 | Carries the brief payload at 1.0 m offset and beyond: 162 kg alone, reach-limited; the 200 kg rating at 1.0 m is no longer reachable once the stabilizer mass uses the headroom | Four outrigger feet 0.17 × 0.40 m (in the mass): 53 kPa static, 66 kPa dynamic, so a floor certified for that pressure, a spreader mat, or the TRK variant is required (OP-PAD does not cover them); deployed depth 0.74 m equals the heritage depth; +0.39 L safe-posture volume; relies on the STD per-arm basis (HMX-3.2-13(c)) | certified floor 66 kPa dynamic or mat |

| OP-NEUT: Conditional neutron layer (HMX-7.3-02) | 80 to 96 | 1.00 / +0.00 | 116 | Borated hydrogenous layer, no gamma credit | RESERVED, not orderable (HMX-1.8-02); falls below the 100 kg minimum with any stability or floor pack | reserved (neutron trigger, HMX-7.3-01) |

The STD arms are designed to the per-arm basis, so OP-STAB needs no arm upgrade (HMX-3.2-13(c)).

### F.4 Trims and configuration matrix

Ratings are issued from the upper-bound mass (HMX-1.8-04). Offsets at the reach are capped at 1.0 m. The composite-shell mass action removes up to 15 kg from the accumulator line [?R]. Pre-weigh-in offset ratings are quoted at the 5th-percentile values of F.7. Every build containing STAB needs the stabilizer-foot floor condition of HMX-3.2-03. Knee margin uses each build's own gross at its rated payload (988 kg for STD at the upper-bound mass), so STD shows 1.24 here against 1.23 at the 1,000 kg GVWR.

| HANDLING | 685 to 768 | 132 / 66 | 0.58 m / >= 1.0 m (reach) | >= 1.0 m (reach) / >= 1.0 m (reach) | 0.925 | 3.1 | 1.23 | pass |

| PROTECTED-TOOLING | 704 to 796 | 104 / 52 | 0.69 m / >= 1.0 m (reach) | >= 1.0 m (reach) / >= 1.0 m (reach) | 0.935 | 6.7 | 1.23 | pass |
| FULL | 797 to 919 | 0 / 0 | >= 1.0 m (reach) / >= 1.0 m (reach) | >= 1.0 m (reach) / >= 1.0 m (reach) | 0.921 | 4.7 | 1.21 | FAIL |
| PROTECTED-TOOLING + COMPOSITE mass action | 689 to 781 | 119 / 60 | 0.59 m / >= 1.0 m (reach) | >= 1.0 m (reach) / >= 1.0 m (reach) | 0.936 | 8.9 | 1.23 | pass |

FULL is shown only to bound the space.

### F.4b Minimum deployable build (STD plus OP-PAD)

Computed with the re-implementation described in F.0, not by the generator. Mass bounds as in F.4.

| Build | Dry mass kg | Rated brief / continuous kg | Offset, no stabilizers, brief / cont. | Offset, stabilizers, brief / cont. | Loaded CoM z m | CoM offset mm | Knee margin | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| STD + PAD (MIN-DEPLOY) | 658 to 718 | 182 / 91 | 0.43 m / 0.77 m | n/a / n/a | 0.951 | 2.8 | 1.23 | pass |

The build exceeds the 700 kg dry-mass budget by up to 18 kg at the upper bound; it meets CAR-2 (rated brief at least 100 kg) and CAR-9 (pads under the feet) and rates 182 kg because the pads' mass uses part of the headroom (GVWR rule).

### F.5 Rules in force

CAR-1 rating; CAR-2 brief rating at least 100 kg; CAR-3 knee margin at least 1.20 with the single-support lever and joint bases of HMX-3.2-13; CAR-4 5° stability margin at least 65 mm with OP-STAB, M₀ at the low bound, and the sum-of-ratios rule for combined offsets; CAR-5 CoM offset within 20 mm; CAR-6 stowed envelope at most 1.83 × 0.99 × 0.74 m, OP-ODG height 1.95 m only with the stature rating and route openings of HMX-3.3-01; CAR-7 safe-posture margin at least 1.25 (demand 2.04 + 0.40 L, plus 0.39 L with OP-STAB); CAR-8 release gates passed before release, OP-NEUT reserved; CAR-9 certified floor or pads under the feet, and a floor certified for 66 kPa dynamic (or a mat, or TRK) under the stabilizer feet. Full statement: HMX-1.8-05.

### F.6 Joint torque bases by build

Single-support knee lever cap at margin 1.20 and ψ (at each build's own gross, so STD shows 78 mm against 77 mm at the 1,000 kg GVWR); ankle basis at the CoP margin limit with ψ (and with a 1.2 margin; bore about 53 mm); shoulder demand per arm from the build's own rated payload and offset (two-handed brief, one-arm continuous), which the STD per-arm design basis of 0.98 kN·m covers (HMX-3.2-13(c)). The full 30-joint table is HMX-3.4-04.

| Build | Single-support knee lever cap mm | Ankle basis kN·m (× 1.2) | Shoulder demand per arm kN·m | Lateral limit, 100 kg class m |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| STD | 78 | 1.50 (1.81) | 0.67 | 0.60 m |
| HANDLING | 77 | 1.52 (1.83) | 0.65 | 0.95 m |
| TOOLING | 77 | 1.52 (1.83) | 0.59 | >= 1.0 m (reach) |
| PROTECTED | 77 | 1.52 (1.83) | 0.56 | >= 1.0 m (reach) |
| PROTECTED-TOOLING | 77 | 1.52 (1.83) | 0.51 | >= 1.0 m (reach) |

### F.7 Sensitivity of the ratings and the pre-weigh-in quoting values

6,000 samples per build, seed 7: component masses uniform within their ranges, heights ±0.10 m, fore-aft positions ±0.05 m, operator height ±0.08 m and position ±0.03 m (Appendix B). The 5th-percentile limits are the values quoted before the first weighing (HMX-1.8-04).

| Build | Offset limit, 100 kg class, m (5th / 50th / 95th) | Offset limit, brief rating, m (5th / 50th / 95th) | CoM offset mm (50th / 95th), share over 20 mm |
|---|---|---|---|
| STD | 0.57 / 0.67 / 0.73 | 0.32 / 0.38 / 0.41 | 6.4 / 18.3, 3.3% |
| HANDLING | 0.70 / 0.84 / 1.00 | 0.38 / 0.46 / 0.55 | 5.9 / 16.8, 1.8% |
| TOOLING | 0.71 / 0.88 / 1.00 | 0.39 / 0.48 / 0.58 | 7.0 / 19.3, 4.1% |
| PROTECTED | 0.75 / 0.92 / 1.00 | 0.41 / 0.50 / 0.61 | 5.9 / 16.9, 2.0% |

Offsets are the no-stabilizer limits at the build's own sampled mass; the CoM-offset share falls to near zero after the first-weighing adjustment of HMX-1.8-06.

### F.8 Combined offsets (CAR-4)

STD at 100 kg: L_x = 68.5 kg·m and L_y = 60.4 kg·m (Appendix B). A payload at 0.4 m fore-aft and 0.2 m lateral gives a ratio of 0.91 and is allowed; at 0.5 m and 0.3 m it gives 1.23 and is not.

## Appendix I. Compliance Matrix

Generated from the records of this document at v3.1. **Type** is atomic (one property), sub (a sub-requirement of a group), group (the parent of subs), or info (an informative design-basis statement with no verification). **Methods** are the tags A (analysis), T (test), I (inspection), D (demonstration). **Test or stage defined in §13** lists the §13 records that name the requirement; "not yet defined" means a method is tagged but no test, analysis, or procedure is defined for it. **Numeric criterion** says whether the record itself states a limit that a test could be judged against. No acceptance record exists for any requirement yet; this matrix shows where the definitions are missing.

Totals: 174 atomic records, 56 sub-requirements in 16 groups, 16 informative records. Of the 136 records tagged T (test), 31 are named in a §13 test or stage definition and 105 are not. Of all 230 verifiable records, 40 are named in §13 and 190 are not.
